## Unknown session IDs get a 404 and a console log, so a Pulsar restart no longer needs a Claude restart (2026-11-06; live-verified after a Pulsar restart and a window reload)

**Before:** the session map `transports` lives in memory, so every server restart or hot reload forgets all sessions. A request carrying the old `mcp-session-id` fell through to `400 Bad Request: No valid session ID provided` (POST) or `400 Invalid or missing session ID` (GET/DELETE). A 400 reads as a plain error, so Claude did not send a new `initialize` and had to be restarted. Credentials were never the cause: there is no auth and the port is fixed (`mcpServerPort`). Nothing was logged on the Pulsar side when a stale ID was rejected.

**Now:** a request with a session ID that is not in `transports` gets `404`, the MCP Streamable HTTP response for an unknown session, which tells the client to drop the session and send a new `initialize`. POST answers with a JSON-RPC error (code -32001, "Session not found"); GET and DELETE answer with the text "Session not found". A missing header still gets `400`, and so does a POST with no session ID that is not an `initialize`. Each 404 also writes one line to the Pulsar console: `[pulsar-edit-mcp-server] <ISO time> stale session ID rejected (<METHOD>): <id> -> 404, client should re-initialize`. Sessions are deliberately not kept across a reload: a kept session would keep the old tool handlers and the client would not get the new tool list.

**Code:** `app.post('/mcp')` and `handleSessionRequest` in `lib/pulsar-edit-mcp-server.js`. No test added; checked live.

**Live result (2026-11-06):** after a Pulsar restart with Claude left open, tool calls kept working without restarting Claude. A `curl` POST with a made-up session ID returned `HTTP/1.1 404` with `{"jsonrpc":"2.0","error":{"code":-32001,"message":"Session not found"},"id":null}`. The console log lines did not appear until a window reload (Ctrl+Shift+F5); after it, the console showed the line for a real stale session ID. The first one or two tool calls after a reload fail ("connector returned an error") before the client re-initializes, then calls succeed. Not probed: GET and DELETE with a stale ID, and which client (Claude's bridge or the package's own client) sent the logged request.

**Found, not fixed:** `deactivate()` throws `_this$statusBarTile.destroy is not a function` on window unload. `setStatusbar` stores the status-bar service in `this.statusBarTile` (line 497) instead of the tile that `addLeftTile` returns, so `this.statusBarTile?.destroy()` (line 414) fails. The lines before it in `deactivate` have already run; only the tile is not removed. Not related to the session change.

## `replace-across-files` saves each file after a commit and its preview counts occurrences (2026-11-05; live-verified 2026-11-06 after a Pulsar restart)

**Found by:** the first live run of the confirm path (TODO R3b), on a scratch file. Two defects, TODO R3c and R3d.

**R3c, before:** the commit edited each buffer with `setTextViaDiff` and never saved it. Right after a commit the file was `modified: true`, so the disk copy lagged until something else saved it (the `run-command` pre-flight did, and listed the file under `savedBeforeRun`). Every other edit tool saves after a commit. **Now:** each file is saved after its edit, through a new `applyAndSave` in `lib/raf-commit.js` (`buffer.save()`, as the framework does for the other tools). A save that fails does not hide the edit: the file is listed in `skipped` with the reason "edited in the buffer but not saved: ...".

**R3d, before:** the preview counted lines ("2 match(es)") while the commit counted occurrences ("replaced 3 occurrence(s)"), so the number changed between the two steps whenever a line held the text twice. **Now:** the summary reads "Preview: 2 line(s) with 3 occurrence(s) across 1 file(s)", and the result carries `totalOccurrences` and, per file, `occurrenceCount`. `totalMatches` is still the number of lines and `maxMatches` still caps lines. The no-match wording is unchanged.

**Code and tests:** new `lib/raf-commit.js` (pure: `occurrencesInLine`, `previewSummary`, `applyAndSave`), the handler and tool description in `lib/mcp-registration.js`, `lib/tool-catalogue.js` and the README row. New `test/raf-commit.test.js` (35 checks: counting, summary wording, save order and save failure with a mock editor, and source checks on the handler; seen failing first, and against the old `mcp-registration.js` 9 wiring checks fail). Suite: 54 test files, 5113 checks, 0 failing. Probed live on 2026-11-06, see the result below.

**Live result (2026-11-06, after a full Pulsar restart; scratch files `r3b_scratch.txt` and `r3b_scratch2.txt` in the package root):** (1) Preview of a glob on one file: "Preview: 2 line(s) with 3 occurrence(s) across 1 file(s)", with `totalOccurrences: 3` and `occurrenceCount: 3` on the file entry (R3d). (2) Commit on a file that was NOT open in a tab: "replaced 3 occurrence(s) across 1 file(s)"; the commit opened a buffer for it, and `get-active-editor-info` read `modified: false` (R3c), checked before any `run-command` pre-flight could save it. The text was correct afterwards. (3) A glob matching two files, one open in a tab and one closed: preview "4 line(s) with 6 occurrence(s) across 2 file(s)", commit "replaced 6 occurrence(s) across 2 file(s)", both files `modified: false`, all six occurrences replaced. Side effects: a commit leaves a tab open for each file that was closed, and `grep-project` shows a STALE warning for the files it just wrote (the on-disk content changed since the server last touched them), which is expected. Not probed live: the failed-save path (needs a read-only file; unit-tested with a mock only).

## `session-notes` keeps one notes file per project folder (2026-11-05; live-verified after a Pulsar restart)

**Before:** one shared `session/session-notes.ndjson`. The optional `project` was only a label: `read` ignored it, `tail:N` counted across every project, and edit and delete used one shared index, so notes from different codebases mixed.

**Now:** `session/notes/<project folder name>.ndjson`, one file per project (new `lib/session-notes-store.js`). `read` with no `project` returns the notes of the CURRENT project (the open root that holds the active file, else the first root) and ends with a one-line index of the other projects that have notes (name, count, last title). `project:"name"` reads another project and `project:"all"` reads every project; `tail:N` now applies per project, and indexes are per project. `write` with no `project` appends to the current project, and a new name must match an open folder or an existing file, so a typo cannot create a stray file. `edit`, `delete` and `clear` require an explicit `project` (`"all"` is refused, so `clear` can no longer wipe everything), and edit and delete refuse a file that has unreadable lines, because rewriting it would drop them. `write` is now a real append, not a read-modify-write of the whole file. Fixed: `delete` reported `removed.timestamp`, which newer records do not have ("was: undefined"); it now uses `ts`.

**Migration:** the first call after the restart splits the old file by its `project` label into the new files (case variants merge; records with no usable label go to the package's own folder name, `pulsar-edit-mcp-server`), keeps the old file as `session-notes.ndjson.migrated`, never mixes into a folder that already has per-project files, and removes what it wrote if anything fails midway. A dry run on the real file showed all 18 notes landing in `pulsar-edit-mcp-server`.

**Code and tests:** `lib/session-notes-store.js` (new), the session-notes block in `lib/mcp-registration.js`, and the tool catalogue and README text. New `test/session-notes-store.test.js` (82 checks: names, current-project rules, append/load/save, listing, project resolution, formatting, the migration, and source checks on the wiring; the wiring checks were seen failing first). Suite: 53 test files, 5078 checks, 0 failing. **Live result (2026-11-05, after PJ's restart):** the first call reported that it migrated the 19 existing notes into per-project files (pulsar-edit-mcp-server 19). Probed live and passing: `read` with no project (current project plus the index footer), `read` of an unknown project (refused, lists the known projects), `edit` with no project (refused), `clear` with `project:"all"` (refused), `write` to another open root and read it back (indexes per project), in-place `edit` (timestamp kept), `read` with `project:"all"` and `tail:1` (one note per project), and `clear` of the throwaway project. Not probed live: `delete`, and a check on disk that `session/notes/pulsar-edit-mcp-server.ndjson` and `session-notes.ndjson.migrated` exist.

## Destructive `run-command` calls are approved with a Run / Cancel button in the Command Output panel (2026-11-04; needs a Pulsar restart to go live)

**A person approves, in the panel they are already watching.** `run-command` still matches the command against the same pattern (`rm`, `rmdir`, `del`, `rd`, `format`, `Remove-Item`, `ri`, `rd /s`). Before, a match asked in the chat panel and was **blocked outright** when no chat panel was open, which is the usual case with Claude Desktop. Now the Command Output panel opens (without taking editor focus), shows the exact command and its working folder, and offers a red **Run** and a **Cancel** button. The tool call waits for the click.

**Only a click on Run lets it through.** The new `lib/command-confirm.js` takes no `confirm` or `force` argument, so `confirm:true` from the caller no longer skips the question (the parameter stays in the schema and is ignored). No answer within two minutes means "do not run" (outcome `timeout`), closing the panel answers every open prompt (`closed`), and any answer other than exactly `approved` is a refusal. The command text is shown with `textContent`, never `innerHTML`. The chat panel's own Run / Cancel row was removed the same day (needs a second restart): the question is asked only in the Command Output panel, and if that panel cannot be opened the command is still blocked, with an error that says to open it. Live-verified after the first restart: Run ran the command and Cancel returned `cancelled: true`; the two-minute timeout is covered by the unit test only.

**Code.** `CommandTerminalPanel.askConfirm(command, { cwd, timeoutMs })` and `destroy()` answering open prompts (`lib/command-terminal-panel.js`); `confirmDestructive()` and `revealCommandPanel()` (`lib/command-confirm.js`, new); the guard in `run-command` rewritten (`lib/mcp-registration.js`); the tool description and README warning say that `confirm:true` does not skip the prompt. New test `test/command-confirm.test.js` (56 checks: fake-DOM tests of the prompt, stub tests of the decision logic, source checks that the guard runs before the process is spawned). Negative controls: against the old panel the test crashes, and against the old `mcp-registration.js` checks 6a to 6d and 6f fail. Suite: 51 test files, 4974 checks, 0 failing.

**Output only in the Command Output panel (same day; needs a restart).** `run-command` no longer writes anything to the chat panel: the `$ command` echo, stdout and stderr lines, the exit line, the pre-flight summary, the reloaded-files notice and a spawn error now go to the Command Output panel only, and the tool description says so. New checks 7a to 7i in `test/command-confirm.test.js` (65 checks in all; seen failing first); suite: 51 test files, 4983 checks, 0 failing. Tool-fault lines for other tools (`appendFault`) still go to the chat panel.
## Four failure-wording fixes (TODO F4): PARTIAL MATCH reason, missing-file note, zero-match `grep-file` banner, `insert` out-of-range retry line (2026-11-03; live-verified after a Pulsar restart)

**PARTIAL MATCH says why the line diverged.** The message always ended "Likely a trailing-whitespace or indentation difference", even when the line differed in content. New `partialWhy()` in `lib/fail-diagnostics.js` picks one of three sentences: the whitespace sentence when the two lines are equal once all whitespace is removed; "Line N differs in content, not only in whitespace."; or "The searched region ends after those lines, so line N has nothing to match." when the needle runs past the end of the region. Tests: the existing partialMatch check pinned the misleading wording and now expects the content sentence (seen failing first); three new checks cover whitespace-only, content and past-the-end; the source check for this message now reads `lib/fail-diagnostics.js`, because the module no longer copies the retired `str_replace` text on purpose (`str_replace` keeps its old wording). Live: a `replace` dryRun whose third line was wrong printed "Line 3 differs in content, not only in whitespace."; a whitespace-only difference was matched by `fuzzyWhitespace`, so that sentence is covered by the unit test only.

**A missing path is named as the likely cause.** An edit tool pointed at a path that is not on disk gets an empty buffer, so every failure blamed the anchor ("afterString not found"). `lib/tool-framework.js` now notes when an edit tool's file is missing on disk and its buffer is empty, and `annotateMissingFile()` in `lib/fail-diagnostics.js` appends "The file does not exist on disk: <path> ... probably caused by a wrong path" to a FAILING result only (an error text starting with the cross mark, or `matched:false`). Successes and dry-run previews are untouched, and an edit that succeeds on a missing path still creates the file as before. Tests: three checks in `test/fail-diagnostics.test.js` plus cases 5 to 7 in the new `test/tool-framework.no-match.test.js`. Live: an `insert` dryRun with an afterString on a nonexistent file printed the original line plus the note.

**A search that finds nothing is not a failure.** `grep-file` answered a zero-match search through `ctx.fail`, which counted it toward the consecutive-failure counter, so the 3rd empty search in a row got the tool-switch suggestion appended. New `ctx.noMatch(msg, extra)` in `lib/tool-framework.js` returns the same shape and still bumps `fails.noMatch` (so the stats panel is unchanged), but does not touch the counter and adds no suggestion. `grep-file`'s zero-match answer uses it; the `occurrence N not found` case stays a real failure, and `ctx.fail` is unchanged. Tests: cases 1 to 4 in `test/tool-framework.no-match.test.js` (seen failing first). Live: four empty `grep-file` calls in a row returned only the plain zero-match JSON.

**`insert` out-of-range line says how to retry.** The message showed the end of the file but no way forward. It now adds "Retry: pass a line number from 1 to N, or use endOfFile:true (append) or startOfFile:true (prepend)." Pinned by a source check in `test/fail-diagnostics.test.js` (seen failing first). Live: `insert` dryRun with `onLine:9999` on a 5-line file printed the new line.

Suite before and after: 49 files, 4902 checks before the two small fixes above; now 50 test files, 4918 checks, 0 failing (one new test file).

## Two small fixes: the double space in the FOUND OUTSIDE SCOPE message (TODO F4) and a dead `diffLines` call in `apply-patch` (TODO G6 cleanup) (2026-11-03; live-verified after a Pulsar restart)

**FOUND OUTSIDE SCOPE double space.** Scope labels begin with a space (for example ` within betweenHint region`) and the message template added another one, so the text read "NOT inside  within ...". `lib/fail-diagnostics.js` (the shared diagnosis) and the retired `str_replace` copy of the message in `lib/mcp-registration.js` now trim the label. Tests: check H4 in `test/match-engine.fail-response.test.js` had been pinning the double space; it now expects one space (seen failing against the old code, then passing), and the source check in `test/fail-diagnostics.test.js` follows the new wording. Live: a `replace` dryRun with a `betweenHint` region that did not hold the text printed "NOT inside within betweenHint region." with a single space. Left as it is: "inside within" still reads clumsily because the labels themselves start with "within"; changing that would touch every scope label and the tests that pin them.

**Dead code in the `apply-patch` `confirm:true` path.** The line `_diffHunks = diffLines(buffer.getText(), newText)` diffed the buffer against itself and its result was never used. It is removed together with the `newText` line above it, which nothing else in that block used. `diffLines` itself is still used elsewhere in the file. Live: a patch with a whitespace-drifted context on a scratch file was rescued by `fuzzyWhitespace`, `confirm:true` applied it ("1 hunk(s) [rescued]") and the file read back correct.

Suite before and after: 49 test files, 4902 checks, 0 failing (no check was added or removed).

## `grep-file`, `grep-project` and `find-text` get `wordRegexp` (grep -w) and `lineRegexp` (grep -x) (TODO R4g, second step) (2026-11-03; live-verified after a full Pulsar restart)

**New parameters, on `grep-file`, `grep-project` and `find-text`** (not on `search-functions`, which matches function names): `wordRegexp:true` keeps only matches that form whole words, like `grep -w`: the character before the match and the one after it must not be a letter, digit or underscore. `lineRegexp:true` keeps only lines that match entirely, like `grep -x`. When both are given `lineRegexp` wins, as in grep. Both work with `regex:true` (BRE or `extended:true`) and without it (the query stays a literal, like `grep -F -w` and `grep -F -x`), and with `caseSensitive:false`. They also shape `onlyMatching`: `-ow` lists each bounded match. Without them nothing changes, and the plain literal path is still a plain `RegExp`.

**How grep does it, and what was copied.** For `-w`, GNU grep takes the leftmost-longest match; if the characters around it fail the word test it tries shorter matches anchored at the same start (matching the text cut one character short), and only then the next start. `lib/search-engine.js` does exactly that (`findWord`), so `a[a-z ]*` with `-w` on "a bc1" finds `a`, where a plain boundary wrapper would find nothing. `-x` requires the leftmost-longest match to be the whole line. Word characters are letters, digits and the underscore (Unicode letters and digits count, like a UTF-8 grep). `compilePattern({ query, regex, extended, caseSensitive, wordRegexp, lineRegexp })` builds a matcher from one `find(line, from)` function; the POSIX path and a new literal path (`compileLiteralWhole`, overlapping occurrences included) share it.

**Checked against the real thing.** New mode `node test/grep-oracle/grep-diff.js words` runs every pattern of the curated BRE and ERE lists plus a list of fixed strings through `-w`, `-x` and both, compares `grep -n` and `grep -o -n` with `scanLines`, and also covers `-F` and `-i`: 876 pattern and flag combinations, **0 new differences** (39 back-reference cases skipped because that grep build cannot run them; 69 known: the GNU-internal disagreements listed in the earlier entries, and one quirk, `grep -o -w -x` prints an empty match for an empty line, which neither flag does alone). Before the change the same run showed 1,106 differences.

**Tests.** `test/search-engine.test.js`: 94 checks (13 new in section 13, with answers taken from real grep 3.11: literal, BRE and ERE, `-i`, the shorter-match rule, `-x` winning over `-w`, the empty pattern, special characters, `onlyMatching`; and two source checks that exactly `find-text`, `grep-file` and `grep-project` pass and declare the options). Suite: 49 test files, 4902 checks, 0 failing.

**Still to do in R4g.** `-L` (files without a match) and `--exclude` for `grep-project`, `-z`, and the default case sensitivity (undecided: the engine is insensitive by default, real grep is sensitive, and the `grep-file` description says sensitive). Live-verified after the restart (2026-11-03) on a scratch file: `wordRegexp` on `foo` keeps the whole-word lines only (`foo`, `foo bar`, `foo-bar`, `(foo)`, `a foo b`, `foofoo foo`, and `Foo` because matching is still case-insensitive by default); `lineRegexp` keeps `foo` and `Foo`; with both, `lineRegexp` wins; an ERE `a[a-z ]*` with `wordRegexp` and `onlyMatching` gives `a` for "a bc1" (the shorter-match rule) and the whole bounded `a foo b`; `grep-project` with `wordRegexp`: the prefix `REGEX_REMOVED` finds 3 lines in `lib/schema.js` without the option and 0 with it, the whole identifier finds 3. Not probed live: `find-text` (needs the right active editor). The case default is still open.

## The search tools accept the patterns GNU grep accepts: a grep syntax mode for the POSIX engine (TODO R4j) (2026-10-31; live-verified after a full Pulsar restart)

**Why.** The grep oracle (`test/grep-oracle/`, real GNU grep 3.11) showed that the matching of `regex:true` already agreed with grep (300 random patterns, 0 differences) but that GNU grep is more lenient than `sed` about malformed patterns, and the engine had been written to follow `sed`. So `grep-file`, `grep-project`, `find-text` and `search-functions` returned `invalidRegex` for patterns that real grep runs. **What now works, as in grep:** in an ERE a repetition operator with nothing before it is ignored (`*a`, `+a`, `?a`, `(*a)`, `a|*b` behave as `a`, `a`, `a`, `(a)`, `a|b`) and so is every operator right after it (`+{2,1}`), and a valid interval there (`{1}`, `{,2}`, `{1,2}a`) is ignored too; a `{` that does not start a valid interval is literal text (`{`, `a{`, `a{1`, `a{x}`, `a{1,x}`); an unmatched `)` is literal text (`)`, `a)`); `(?:a)` is accepted and is the group `(:a)`; an operator right after an anchor repeats the anchor, so `^*a` and `a$*` select every line with an `a`; stacked repetition is allowed in both syntaxes (`a**`, `a?*`, and in a BRE `a\{1\}*` and `a\+*`); in a BRE a leading `\{` is a literal brace. **What stays an error, as in grep:** a well-formed but wrong interval after an atom (`a{2,1}`, `a{}`, `a{99999}`), an unmatched `(`, `\(` or `\)` in a BRE, a BRE `\{` that is never closed (`a\{1`), and a bad back-reference.

**How.** `lib/posix-regex.js` has a new option `grep: true` (`compile(pattern, { extended, icase, multiline, grep })`), documented in the file header. Everything above is behind it, so `sed` keeps its strict behaviour; `compilePosix` in `lib/search-engine.js` turns it on. Two things in the engine changed for both modes, because real GNU sed 4.9 confirmed them: a brace followed by junk and then the end of the pattern (`a\{(ab`, `a\{1,x`, `a{(ab`) is now "Unmatched \{" (glibc reads on to the next comma or closing brace; it used to say "Invalid content of \{\}"), while junk followed by a closing brace still gives "Invalid content of \{\}". Where GNU grep's own two engines disagree with each other (line selection and `-o` give different answers for a leading `{1}` and for an operator right after an anchor) the line selection is followed, so `-o` output can differ for those patterns; the oracle harness counts them as known.

**Checked against the real thing.** `grep-diff.js curated` (264 patterns): 26 differences before, 0 new now (13 back-reference cases skipped because that grep build cannot run them, 2 known). New mode `grep-diff.js lenient` (random strings of operators, braces, parentheses and anchors in odd places, both syntaxes): 0 new differences over three seeds of 500 (1,500 patterns per syntax, about 50 per seed counted as known GNU-internal disagreements), and `fuzz` seeds 1 and 5: 0. The `sed` oracle (`test/sed-oracle/regex-diff.js`, real GNU sed 4.9) gives the identical result before and after the change (4 known deviations on the curated list, 2 on `fuzz 1 150`), so `sed` is not less faithful.

**Tests.** `test/search-engine.test.js` now has 79 checks (18 new in section 12, one rewritten: 5d expected `(?:x)` to be an error, which real grep contradicted): every rule above with the answers real grep gave, what must stay an error and with which message, the strict sed mode unchanged (`posix-regex` without `grep` still rejects `*a`, `a{1`, `)`, `a**`), and the new "Unmatched \{" wording in both modes. The new cases were red first. Suite: 49 test files, 4887 checks, 0 failing.

**Live (2026-10-31, after the restart).** `grep-file` with `extended:true`: `a{1` finds the line `a{1` as literal text, and `(?:x)` finds both `(?:x)` and `:x`; `\(ab` with `regex:true` still returns "Invalid regex: Unmatched ( or \(".

## The search tools compile `regex:true` as a POSIX regex like plain grep, with a new `extended` parameter (TODO R4g, first step: the regex layer) (2026-10-31; live-verified after a full Pulsar restart)

**BEHAVIOUR CHANGE for anyone passing `regex:true` to `grep-file`, `grep-project`, `find-text` or `search-functions`.** Until now their `regex:true` was a JavaScript regex. It is now a POSIX regex compiled by `lib/posix-regex.js`, the engine `sed` already uses, and the default flavour is the basic one (BRE), as in plain `grep` (decided by PJ, 2026-10-27). In a BRE the operators are `\|`, `\(` `\)`, `\+`, `\?` and `\{n\}`, and a bare `| + ? ( ) { }` is literal text, so a pattern written the JavaScript way (`foo|bar`, `(a|b)+`, `x{2}`) now looks for that literal text and usually finds nothing. The new boolean `extended` (the same parameter `sed` has) selects the extended flavour, like `grep -E`: `foo|bar`, `(a|b)+` and `x{2}` work without backslashes. `extended` does nothing without `regex:true`. Without `regex:true` nothing changes: the query is still an escaped literal in a plain `RegExp`, the fast path, equal to `grep -F`. POSIX, not JavaScript, also means: `\d` matches the letter d (use `[0-9]` or `[[:digit:]]`), a lazy `*?` is greedy, alternation is leftmost-longest (`a|ab` on `ab` gives `ab`), `(?:x)` is the group `(:x)` (as in grep, see the next entry), and a pattern that is too complex fails while matching with the same `invalidRegex` code. Case handling is unchanged (insensitive unless `caseSensitive:true`), and so are `occurrence`, the context options, `invertMatch`, `onlyMatching` (leftmost-longest, non-overlapping, empty matches skipped, like `grep -o`), `countOnly` and `maxMatches`.

**What changed in the code.** `lib/search-engine.js`: `compilePattern({ query, regex, extended, caseSensitive })` sends `regex:true` to a new POSIX matcher (an object with `test(line)` and `all(line)`); `scanLines` now works on a matcher, and wraps a plain `RegExp` (what `search-symbol` passes, and the literal path) so nothing changes for them, including the `/g` state reset. The four tools got the `extended` parameter, its description and the matcher; `search-functions` no longer builds its own JavaScript `RegExp` from the query (it also reports a bad regex as `invalidRegex` now, instead of an uncaught SyntaxError). New pure helper `regexHint`: when a regex search finds nothing, the reply carries a note if the pattern uses a bare `| + ? ( ) { }` without `extended:true` (pass `extended:true` or use a backslash), a backslash-d (POSIX has no digit shorthand), or a lazy quantifier in an ERE; backslash pairs and bracket expressions are not counted, and a literal query never gets a note. `find-text` and `search-functions` append it to their no-match text, `grep-file` and `grep-project` add a `hint` field to their JSON.

**Tests.** New `test/search-engine.test.js` (61 checks; `lib/search-engine.js` had no test file before): the literal path and the `scanLines` options pinned down first (they pass before and after), then the BRE and ERE behaviour, the POSIX traps, case, `onlyMatching`, errors, a speed check (5,000 lines in well under 2 seconds), RegExp pass-through, source checks that all four tools pass `extended` and use the note, and the eleven `regexHint` cases. The BRE and POSIX cases failed before the change (red first). Suite: 49 test files, 4869 checks, 0 failing.

**Not done yet (R4g continues).** The audit against GNU grep: the default case sensitivity (the engine is case-insensitive by default, while the `grep-file` description says case-sensitive), and the missing `-w`, `-x`, `--exclude` and `-L`. There is now a real GNU grep 3.11 to compare against (`test/grep-oracle/`, the Windows build from mbuilov/grep-windows that PJ placed on 2026-10-31; see its README.md and HASHES.txt; not part of the test suite). `node test/grep-oracle/grep-diff.js fuzz 1 150` (300 random patterns) gives 0 differences with grep on selected lines and `-o` output, and `curated` (264 patterns, 13 back-reference cases skipped because that build cannot run back-references) shows 26 differences of one kind: GNU grep is more lenient than `sed`, so some patterns that grep accepts (`*a`, `a**`, `a{1`, `)` in an ERE, a leading `\{` in a BRE, `(?:a)`) were `invalidRegex` here until the next entry (R4j) added grep's lenient syntax. Still to audit: the default case sensitivity (the engine is case-insensitive by default, real grep is case-sensitive, while the `grep-file` description says case-sensitive), and the missing `-w`, `-x`, `--exclude` and `-L`. Live-verified after the restart (2026-10-31): `grep-file` with `cat|dog` and `regex:true` finds nothing and returns the hint; with `extended:true`, or as `cat\|dog`, it finds both lines; `foo|bar` without `regex` is still a literal; `\(ab` returns "Invalid regex: Unmatched ( or \("; `grep-project` accepts `extended:true`, lists files and carries the hint on a miss; `search-functions` appends the hint. Not probed live: `find-text` and a positive `search-functions` match (both need the right active editor). The default case sensitivity is still undecided: `grep-file` found `Foo` for the query `foo` although its description says case-sensitive.

## The `sed` change report no longer lists the whole stretch between two far-apart edits as changed (TODO R4i) (2026-10-31; live-verified after a full Pulsar restart)

**The bug.** `diffLines` in `lib/sed-tool.js` trims the common first and last lines and then runs an exact LCS table on the block in between. When that block was bigger than 4,000,000 cells (old length times new length, which two edits more than about 2,000 rows apart are enough for) it gave up and reported the WHOLE block as changed. Only the preview was wrong (the `Changes:` text, also shown by `dryRun`); the write itself was always correct, which a line diff against a copy confirmed each time. Observed on `lib/mcp-registration.js` (about 7,500 lines): 4 edits on rows 2572 to 4759 were reported as 2188 lines removed, 2188 added and "4346 more changed line(s)", and 2 edits on rows 316 and 4753 as 4438. Reproduced first, with exactly those numbers, on a generated 7,500-line file.

**The fix.** New `myersDiff(a, b, maxD)` (Myers O(ND) diff, exported from `lib/sed-tool.js`). `diffLines` uses it for blocks over the LCS limit, where it finds the true minimal edit script whenever few lines changed: the 4 edits above are now 4 removed and 4 added at rows 2572, 2759, 3725 and 4759, the 2 edits are 2 and 2, and a 78-line deletion plus a far edit plus 2 inserted lines is 79 removed and 3 added. Blocks small enough for the LCS table still use it, unchanged, so every existing preview is identical. The search stops at an edit distance of 2,000 (`MYERS_MAX_D`, memory about that number squared ints, 16 MB at the cap); past that it returns null and the old fallback applies, so more than about 1,000 changed lines scattered across a big file are still reported as the whole stretch changed. Measured on a 7,500-line file: 44 to 49 ms at the cap, 12 MB heap, 0 ms for 3 far edits in a 20,000-line file.

**Tests.** `test/sed-tool.test.js` has 10 new checks (6j to 6s): the 4-edit and 2-edit cases with their rows and text, the deletion and insertion case, `buildSedPreview` counts and no "more changed" line on the big file, 2,000 seeded random pairs where the Myers result must be a valid edit script (the kept lines are equal) and minimal (the same distance as the LCS), the limit returning null, and empty or one-sided input. Negative control: against a copy of the pre-fix `lib/`, 6j, 6k, 6l, 6m, 6n and 6p fail. Suite: 48 test files, 4808 checks, 0 failing.

**Live verification (2026-10-31, after a full restart).** `sed` dry runs on `lib/mcp-registration.js` (about 7,500 lines): one changed line reports 1 removed and 1 added at the right row; two edits on rows 316 and 4753 report 2 removed and 2 added (the same pair was reported as 4438 changed lines before the fix); four edits plus a 78-line deletion report 82 removed or changed and 4 added, and the only truncation is the normal 30-line preview limit ("56 more changed line(s)"). The `read` refusal wording from the R2a entry below was live-verified in the same restart.

## Regex is removed from every edit tool and every anchor; it lives only in `sed` and the search tools (TODO R2a) (2026-10-28 to 2026-10-30; live-verified after a full Pulsar restart)

**What changed.** `regex:true` is now refused, with nothing changed, on `replace`, `str_replace`, `replace-all`, `replace-across-files`, `delete`, `insert`, `replace-block`, `replace-function-body` and `read` (the last one because it shares the anchor schema; being read-only it gets its own wording that points to `grep-file` and `startLine`/`endLine` instead of claiming that a change was undone). The refusal names the tool, says nothing was changed and points to the `sed` tool (GNU sed syntax, `extended:true` for `-E`) or to `replace` with `fragment:true` for a literal part-line edit. Regex still works, unchanged, on `find-text`, `grep-file`, `grep-project` and `search-functions`; `sed` has no `regex` flag because its script is the regex. The refusal is one central guard, the new pure module `lib/regex-guard.js` (`REGEX_KEEP` and `regexRefusal(name, args)`), called first thing in the `registerTool` wrapper in `lib/mcp-registration.js`, so it runs before any handler, the retry stash or a dry run. The parameter is still ACCEPTED by the tool schemas (`REGEX_REMOVED_PARAM` in `lib/schema.js`, described as REMOVED) only so that the value reaches the guard: if it were dropped from the schema, zod would strip it silently and the pattern would be matched as a plain literal, which is the failure the old `insert` regex bug had.

**Code removed.** The `str_replace` handler lost its whole regex matching block, the `!regex` conditions, the regex-only occurrence message, the `regexCommits` bump and the `regex` tag (78 lines). `regex` is gone from the argument lists and the matcher calls of `insert`, `delete`, `replace-block`, `replace`, `replace-across-files` and `replace-all`; `replace-all` and `replace-across-files` now always match their query as an escaped literal (to change a pattern across files, find the files with `grep-project` and `filesWithMatches:true`, then run `sed` once per file). `lib/match-engine.js`: `matchContent` lost its regex branch (about 70 lines) and the `invalidRegex` reason. `lib/edit-match.js` (`findHits` and its pattern builder), `lib/replace-all-plan.js` (the `regex` option and the `invalidRegex` result), `lib/mid-line-hint.js`, `lib/replace-fragment.js` (the flag-clash message and check) and `lib/str-replace-preview.js` (the `regex` tag) lost theirs too. The tool descriptions, `lib/tool-catalogue.js` and the failure advice in `lib/tool-hints.js` no longer offer `regex:true`; that advice now points to `sed`. Left on purpose: the `regexCommits` and regex-hint counters in `lib/edit-stats.js` (they hold historic lifetime data that is loaded from disk), and the `regex` flag filter inside the `sed` handler.

**Behaviour changes.** (1) `regex:true` on an edit tool is an error instead of a regex edit. (2) `regex:false`, or no `regex` at all, behaves exactly as before. (3) `replace-all` and `replace-across-files` can no longer take capture-group replacements; use `sed`.

**Tests.** New `test/regex-guard.test.js` (52 checks): the guard on every edit tool and `read`, pass-through for the four search tools, `regex:false`, no key, no arguments and non-boolean values, plus source checks that the wrapper calls the guard before the handler, that the schema stubs are in place and that only the four search handlers still destructure `regex`. `test/mcp-registration.insert-regex.test.js` was renamed `mcp-registration.insert-fuzzy.test.js` and keeps only its `fuzzyContent` checks (18). The source-marker strings in three mid-line tests no longer expect `regex`, and the flag-clash test in `match-engine.retry-nudge.test.js` now uses `fuzzyWhitespace`. The regex cases of the engine test files were deleted with the code they tested: `edit-match.test.js` (G3 to G6, G8, G9, C4 to C7, C12, Z1 to Z4, L2, P2b, P11, P14, H1 to H3, H6, H7), `match-engine.fail-response.test.js` (I5, I6, I9 and the whole J and K regex-row and anchor sections), `replace-all-plan.test.js` (the regex rows of the differential matrix and G3 to G5, G8, G9, P2), `mid-line-hint.test.js` (the regex-call check) and `str-replace-preview.test.js` (the regex tag check); the leftover `regex: true` options in `replace-fragment.*`, `match-engine.occurrence` and the retry-nudge flag-clash test were changed or removed. Two of these files, `mid-line-hint.test.js` and `replace-fragment.multiline.test.js`, were damaged on 2026-10-30 by a faulty one-off edit script (every `a`, respectively every `f`, was overwritten with `n`, respectively `l`) and no copy existed, so they were rebuilt from the damaged text and the identifiers of the rest of the repository and then run: 32 and 52 checks, all passing. They are reconstructions, not the original bytes: variable names that could not be recovered were chosen (the baseline result in M6 of the multiline test is now called `lf`), so a diff against any older copy may show cosmetic differences. Suite: 48 test files, 4798 checks, 0 failing (it was 47 files, 5019 checks before this work; the drop is the deleted regex cases, the added guard test has 52 checks).

**Live verification (2026-10-30, after a full restart).** Dry runs of all nine tools above with `regex:true` on a scratch file were refused with the new message and wrote nothing; `replace` with `regex:false` and `inFunction`, `replace` with `fragment:true`, `insert` without `regex` and a `sed` dry run with a `\(...\)` group all behaved normally; `grep-file` with `regex:true` returned both matches. `find-text` and `search-functions` were not probed live (the first needs an active editor); an offline harness that ran the real registration (72 tools) confirmed the nine refusals through the real wrapper.

## `CHANGELOG.md` repaired: 1051 garbled character sequences converted back (TODO H4, H10) (2026-10-25; documentation only)

Earlier edits of this file through Windows PowerShell 5.1 text cmdlets had read its UTF-8 text as Windows-1252 and written it back as UTF-8, which turned every non-ASCII character (em and en dashes, arrows, check and cross marks, emoji) into two to four garbled characters. The mechanism was reproduced on scratch files (TODO H10). The file was repaired with a node script that converts each garbled run back only when its bytes decode as strictly valid UTF-8: 1051 runs in 29 distinct forms, 428889 bytes down to 423924, no BOM, 2173 lines before and after, the 2 CRLF line endings kept, and no line that was pure ASCII before was touched. The standing rule (never edit repository files with PowerShell `Get-Content`, `Set-Content`, `Out-File` or `>`) is now in the README, TODO.md and the handoff. Only this file was affected: every other text file in the project was scanned and is clean. Suite unchanged: 47 test files, 5019 checks, 0 failing.

## `postinstall` no longer fails without `patch-package`, and the `match-engine.js` header is current (TODO H7 part, H3) (2026-10-25; install behaviour and a comment only, no restart needed)

`package.json` ran `patch-package` as `postinstall` while listing it only under `devDependencies`, so an install without dev dependencies could fail at that step (the command is "not recognized" and exits 1; reproduced on the main machine, where `patch-package` is not installed). The script is now `patch-package || exit 0`: `npm run postinstall` exits 0 there, and nothing else in `package.json` changed. The two existing patches (`merge-descriptors`, `router`) are already applied in the current `node_modules`; whether to retire them is still open in TODO H7 (Pulsar 1.132.1 runs Node 20.16.0, which does not need them). The header comment of `lib/match-engine.js` no longer says `str_replace` is "not yet refactored" onto the engine: it says `str_replace` is retired, lists the tools that run on the engine and says new edit tools should be built on it. Suite: 47 test files, 5019 checks, 0 failing.

## `replace-function-body` no longer advertises a `name` parameter it does not have (TODO R1m) (2026-10-25; live-verified after a full Pulsar restart)

The tool description in `lib/mcp-registration.js` said "name - the function name", but the input schema has only `newBody` plus the scope hints: the function is found from the name in the signature line of `newBody` (or from `inFunction` when `newBody` has no signature line). The description now says that, the unused `name` is gone from the handler's argument list, a stale comment names `_fnName`, and the `replace-function-body` entry of `lib/tool-catalogue.js` says how the function is found. No behaviour change. Suite: 47 test files, 5019 checks, 0 failing. Live-verified after a full restart: the reloaded tool description shows the new wording and the schema has no `name`; a dry-run call on `lib/sed-tool.js` found `diffLines` (lines 66-101) from the signature line of `newBody`.

## `replace-block` failures name `anchor` instead of `old_str` (TODO R1l) (2026-10-25; live-verified after a full Pulsar restart)

The content-failure messages of `replace-block` (not found anywhere, near miss, whitespace or comment difference, found outside the scope) said `old_str`, a parameter that tool does not have; its parameter is `anchor`. The tool's two `buildFailResponse` calls now pass `subject: 'anchor'`, and the `regex:true` example in the shared "NO HINTS USED" advice (`lib/tool-hints.js`) names `anchor` for `replace-block` too. `str_replace` keeps its wording byte for byte. `replace-function-body` and `insert-function-doc` needed no change: their `buildFailResponse` calls only reach the scope-error and out-of-range-occurrence messages, which never name the needle. Tests: `test/match-engine.subject-wording.test.js` now also drives `replace-block` with subject `anchor` through its seven failure scenarios (80 checks, was 66). Suite: 47 test files, 5019 checks, 0 failing. Live-verified after a full restart (dry runs on `lib/sed-tool.js`): a missing anchor says `anchor` in all four places, including the `regex:true` example; a found-outside-scope failure says `anchor`; `str_replace` (control) still says `old_str`.

## The `sed` documentation and tool catalogue describe the GNU sed tool (TODO R4d) (2026-10-25)

`README.md` (the `sed` row of the tool table, the module list and the match-engine paragraph) and the `sed` entry of `lib/tool-catalogue.js` still described the old JS-regex tool, `lib/sed-plan.js` and the "N match(es)" response. They now describe the GNU sed 4.9 tool (`lib/sed-tool.js`, `lib/sed-parse.js`, `lib/sed-run.js`, `lib/posix-regex.js`), the scope hints, `occurrence` as a hint picker only, the refused `w W r R e`, and the "N removed, M added" response. The hints `afterSymbol`, `beforeSymbol`, `afterFunction`, `beforeFunction`, `beforeString`, `beforeLine`, `fuzzyWhitespace`, `banner`, `ifdefBlock` and `ifdefEdge` were also checked live (dry runs on scratch files) with the expected result. The old `lib/sed-plan.js` and `test/sed-plan.test.js` were deleted the same day (nothing required them any more). No tool behaviour changed. Suite: 47 test files, 5005 checks, 0 failing.

## The `sed` tool runs real GNU sed 4.9 scripts (TODO R4d) (2026-10-24; live-verified after a full Pulsar restart)

**What changed.** The `sed` tool no longer uses the old JavaScript-regex subset (`lib/sed-plan.js`: `s`, `/addr/s`, `/a/,/b/d`, `/addr/d`). Its `expression` is now a complete GNU sed 4.9 script, parsed by `lib/sed-parse.js` (sandbox mode) and run by `lib/sed-run.js`: all commands (`s y d p P D n N g G h H x a i c = l q Q { } b t T :`), all address forms, POSIX basic regexes with the GNU extensions and leftmost-longest matching (`extended:true` is sed `-E`), `\1`..`\9`, `&` and the case escapes in replacements (a `$` stays literal), the `s` flags `g p N i I m M`, and `#n` as sed `-n`. The result of the script is the whole new file, written back the way `sed -i` would; a script that prints nothing (`#n` without `p`) empties the file, as `sed -n -i` does. `e r R w W` and the `s` flags `e` and `w` are refused (sandbox). `-z`, `-s` and `-l` are not offered.

**New `lib/sed-tool.js` (pure, no Pulsar).** `runSed({ expression, extended, text, scope, fileName })` parses and runs a script and returns `{ ok, output, exitCode, quit, silent }` or `{ ok:false, kind:'parse'|'run', message, ... }` with sed's own error text (for example `-e expression #1, char 5: unterminated \`s' command`). `diffLines` and `buildSedPreview` build the dry-run preview (changed lines with their rows; at most 30 entries, then a summary line).

**Scope hints.** All the hints of the other edit tools now work for `sed`, all optional: `inFunction`, `betweenHint`, `afterFunction`/`beforeFunction`, `afterSymbol`/`beforeSymbol`, `afterString`/`beforeString`, `afterLine`/`beforeLine`, `banner`, `ifdefBlock` (+ `ifdefEdge`), `hintRadius`, `fuzzyWhitespace` and `occurrence`. They are resolved by `resolveScope` in `lib/match-engine.js`, exactly as for `str_replace` and `insert` (so `inFunction`, `betweenHint`, `banner` and `ifdefBlock` give the whole region, and the `after*`/`before*` hints give a window of `hintRadius` lines, default 25, from the anchor). The resolved rows become the interpreter's `scope`: the script runs as if wrapped in `FROM,TO{ ... }`; line numbers and `$` inside the script keep their whole-file meaning. With no hint the whole file is the scope. `occurrence` now only picks which match of an ambiguous hint is meant; it is no longer the `s///N` count (write `s/a/b/2` in the script). A bad or ambiguous hint fails through `buildFailResponse`, as before.

**Behaviour changes.** (1) Patterns are POSIX, not JavaScript: `\(a\|b\)\+` in a basic regex, `(a|b)+` with `extended:true`. (2) A script whose output equals the file is reported as a failure ("sed made no changes"), counted like the old no-match case. (3) Parse errors and run errors carry sed's exact message and nothing is changed. (4) The result fields are `linesRemoved` / `linesAdded` (the old `matchCount` / `linesDeleted` are gone). (5) The old empty-pattern refusal is gone: an empty regex reuses the last regex, as in sed (with none yet it is the run error "no previous regular expression"). (6) The tool's input schema is the shared hint schema without `regex` and `fuzzyContent`, plus `banner`, `ifdefBlock`, `ifdefEdge`, `expression`, `extended` and `lint`.

**Stats.** `lib/edit-stats.js`: the `sed` block gets the failure keys `runError`, `anchorNotFound` and `anchorAmbiguous` (the last two were bumped by the old handler into keys that did not exist, so they counted nothing); `splitFails` counts `addressNotFound`, `badExpression`, `runError`, `anchorNotFound` and `anchorAmbiguous` as faults and `noMatch` as a miss.

**Tests.** New `test/sed-tool.test.js` (78 checks): scripts, `-E`, scope (`$` and line numbers keep their whole-file meaning), `q5` exit status, sed's error wording, sandbox refusals, the line diff and the preview, and source guards that the handler forwards every hint to `resolveScope`, turns rows into 1-based sed line numbers and uses `runSed` and `buildSedPreview`. Suite: 48 test files, 8973 checks, 0 failing. `lib/sed-plan.js` and `test/sed-plan.test.js` are no longer used by the tool.

**Live verification (2026-10-24, after a full Pulsar restart).** Dry runs on `README.md` (580 lines) and `lib/sed-tool.js`, plus one real commit on a scratch file outside the repo, all gave the expected result: a plain `s///g` on an address range; `-E` with `s/(MCP|Pulsar)/\L\1-\U\1/g`; a malformed `s/foo/bar` (sed's own error text, nothing changed); a script that changes nothing (reported as "sed made no changes"); an ambiguous `afterString` (refused, all 57 candidate lines listed) and the same hint with `occurrence:1` (only the first window edited); `2q5` (file cut to two lines, exit status 5 shown in the preview header); `#n` with `/^# /p` (silent mode, 578 of 580 lines removed in the preview); the scope hints `afterLine` with `hintRadius`, `betweenHint` and `inFunction` (only rows inside the resolved region changed, and the header names the region); and a committed script that combines an address block, an `s` with the `\u` escape, `a\` text and `$d`, read back row by row from the edited file.

## `delete` and `insert` failure messages name their own parameter (TODO R1k) (2026-10-22; takes effect after a full Pulsar restart)

**What changed.** `buildFailResponse` in `lib/match-engine.js` now forwards `subject` (the caller's own parameter name) to the diagnosis for EVERY content failure, not only for the mid-line ones added in R1j. The similarity, whitespace, comment and "found outside scope" lines and the "found at" line of a failed `delete` (`matchString`, `startContent`, `endContent`) or `insert` (`afterContent`, `beforeContent`) therefore say `matchString` / `afterContent` / ... instead of `old_str`, a parameter those tools do not have. Callers that pass no subject (`replace` and the other tools) are byte-for-byte unchanged.

**Tests.** New `test/match-engine.subject-wording.test.js` (66 checks): for `delete` and `insert`, seven kinds of failure (not found anywhere, found outside scope, indentation, near miss, `occurrence:1` alone, `occurrence:2`, trailing comment) never mention `old_str` and name the caller's parameter; without a subject the output equals the `old_str` wording; the mid-line wording of R1j is unchanged. Case 4 of `test/mid-line-hint.per-tool.test.js` now pins the new rule (a subject without a mid-line condition only renames the needle) instead of "byte-identical". Suite: 47 test files, 8895 checks, 0 failing. Not changed: the failures of `replace-block`, `replace-function-body` and `insert-function-doc` still say `old_str` (TODO R1l).

## `lib/sed-run.js`: the sed interpreter, byte-exact like GNU sed 4.9 (TODO R4c) (2026-10-22; not wired into any tool yet)

**What it is.** `runScript(parsed, input, opts)` runs the output of `lib/sed-parse.js` over the text of one file and returns `{ output, exitCode, quit }`. Options: `silent` (-n), `lineWrap` (-l, default 70), `fileName` (for `F`), `scope: { from, to }` (runs the script exactly as if it were wrapped in `from,to{ ... }`; this is how `inFunction` will be expressed), `maxSteps` (safety stop, exit code 4). `r R w W e` and the `w`/`e` flags of `s` throw `SedRunError`; the parser's sandbox already rejects them. Also exports `encodeBytes` / `decodeBytes`: GNU sed works on bytes, so bytes that are not valid UTF-8 travel through the JS string as lone surrogates U+DC80..U+DCFF and come back unchanged (the future tool must read files with `decodeBytes` and write with `encodeBytes`).

**Behaviour found by probing real sed (and pinned by tests).** A missing final newline stays missing (each space carries a "chomped" flag: `h g` copy it, `x` swaps it, `G H N` take it from the added text; `P` on an unterminated last line prints without newline, `q` always ends its output with a newline). `n`/`N` reset the `t` flag, `N` on the last line prints the pattern space, `c` in a range prints once at the end (once per line with `!`), `ADDR,~N` ends at `l + N - l%N`, a numeric range start fires at the first line seen at or after it (not only at the exact line), an `a` text is flushed when the next line is read, `D` restarts without reading. The empty regex means the last regex used at run time; reusing an ADDRESS regex in an `s` with a bad group reference is an error, a regex of an earlier `s` is not checked. `l` works on bytes (octal for everything outside printable ASCII) and wraps at `width - 1`. After an EMPTY match sed steps ONE BYTE, so `s/x*/-/g` on a multibyte character puts the replacement between its bytes (and writes invalid UTF-8): lib/sed-run.js does exactly that, but only for patterns that glibc treats as byte-safe (no I flag, `.`, range, class, negated bracket, `\w \W \s \S`, `\b \B \< \>` or back-reference). Stray bytes are invalid characters: `.`, brackets and classes do not match them, but for `\b \B \< \>` they count as word characters.

**Other files.** `lib/posix-regex.js` (3 small changes for stray bytes: sets and `.` refuse U+DC80..U+DCFF, `\b` family treats them as word characters), `lib/sed-parse.js` (only the header comment of the `y` map: keyed by character, not code point), `test/sed-run.test.js` (2241 checks) with `test/golden/sed-run-cases.js` (1852 + 312 cases recorded from a real sed by `test/sed-oracle/gen-run-test.js`), 12 new checks in `test/posix-regex.test.js`, and the differential tool `test/sed-oracle/run-diff.js` (+ oracle.js now passes bytes). Mutation check: 24 of 26 deliberate breaks of sed-run.js are caught; the other two are an equivalent mutant (scope block end) and one dead line, which was removed.

**Verified (2026-10-22, Windows sed 4.9 from mbuilov/sed-windows).** `run-diff.js`: `--quick` 3260 cases, `--rest` 3260 (28 known), `--mb` 1098 (23 known), 0 mismatches. Suite: 46 test files, 8829 checks, 0 failing. The "known" cases are differences of the Windows port, not of this module: `l` prints a tab as a real tab, characters outside the BMP (16-bit wchar_t), a lone stray byte has no word boundary; Linux sed 4.9 (checked in a sandbox) agrees with lib/sed-run.js in all of them. Known limitations: `\xHH` in a regex is a raw byte in GNU sed but a character here; `-s`, `-z` and `-E` handling is left to the tool layer.

## `sed-parse` `v` version check fixed, and the sed oracle tools added under `test/sed-oracle/` (2026-10-21; follow-up to R4a and R4b)

**What changed.** (1) `lib/sed-parse.js`: the `v` command now compares its version argument the way real sed does (glibc strverscmp: digit runs compare as numbers, everything else by character code). Before, `v {` was accepted; real GNU sed 4.9 rejects it. Found by the new corpus. (2) `test/sed-parse.test.js`: 5 new `v` cases, so 644 checks (was 639). (3) New folder `test/sed-oracle/` (consolidated differential tools that compare `lib/posix-regex.js` and `lib/sed-parse.js` with a REAL GNU sed 4.9): `oracle.js` (shared helpers), `corpus.js`, `seeds.json`, `regex-diff.js`, `parse-diff.js`, `parse-sem.js`, `parse-fuzz.js`, `gen-parse-test.js`, a `README.md`, `HASHES.txt` and `PLACE-THESE-FILES.txt`. They need `sed` on PATH and are NOT part of `npm test`.

**Windows.** The tools also run with the native Windows sed 4.9 (mbuilov/sed-windows, copied as `test/sed-oracle/sed.exe`, not part of the package). For that `oracle.js` got three small changes, Linux behaviour unchanged: `SED_ORACLE_LOCALE` overrides the locale (the Windows build rejects `C.UTF-8`; `.UTF-8` works), `-b` is added on Windows, and CRLF in sed's stderr becomes LF. The README has a "Running on Windows" section. Results: `parse-diff.js` 2529 cases, `parse-fuzz.js` 1174 scripts and `parse-sem.js` 9312 comparisons agree with sed (0 mismatches; the 80 scripts that use `w /dev/null` cannot run on Windows); `regex-diff.js curated` has 4 mismatches, all for the BRE pattern `\(a$\)`, where the Windows build differs from Linux sed 4.9, and Linux sed agrees with `lib/posix-regex.js`. Test suite after this change: 45 test files, 6576 checks, 0 failing.

## `lib/sed-parse.js`: a parser for GNU sed 4.9 scripts (TODO R4b) (2026-10-18; second step of R4)

**What changed.** New pure module `lib/sed-parse.js` (about 615 lines, no I/O, never opens the files a script names): `parseScript(script, { extended, sandbox })` returns `{ cmds, silent }`, a flat command vector like sed's own (`{` carries `blockEnd`, b / t / T carry `jump`, addresses are typed num / last / step / regex / plus / mod / null, `s` carries its compiled regex, GNU's replacement pieces and flags, `y` a character map, a / i / c their final text). Errors are `SedParseError` with sed's exact message and its "char N" position (counted in bytes). It uses `lib/posix-regex.js` (R4a) to compile every regex, so invalid patterns give glibc's message at sed's position. **Nothing requires the module yet**: the `sed` tool and `sed-plan.js` are untouched, so no restart and no behaviour change. It is the foundation for R4c (interpreter) and R4d (wiring).

**Behaviour reproduced (all checked against GNU sed 4.9).** The whole command set and address syntax (`first~step`, `addr,+N`, `addr,~N`, `0,/re/`, `0r file`, `+0` and `~0` as null addresses, `!`, ranges, `I` and `M` address flags); blocks, labels (ending at whitespace, `;`, `}` or `#`) and branches resolved at the end; `#n` silent mode; a / i / c in every text form (`a text`, `a\` newline, `a\text`, continuations, escapes); `s` with any delimiter, all flags (whitespace between flags is skipped, `w` takes the rest of the line), the delimiter scanner that is bracket-aware for patterns only, escape normalisation per context (`\x5e` raw in a regex but a literal `&` in a replacement, `\c` rules), and sed's replacement-piece algorithm for `\U \L \E \u \l`; `y` with its own unescaping; `v` version check; the sandbox option (e / r / R / w / W and the s flags e and w are refused with sed's message and position); the `-E` option; "[:alpha:] outside brackets" refusal.

**Tests.** New `test/sed-parse.test.js` (639 checks): 380 error-corpus verdicts (accept, or the exact message and char position), 204 single-command results (s, y, a, i, c applied to a line, output recorded from real sed, using a small evaluator in the test), and the shape of the parse result, sandbox, `-E` and error fields. Built with differential tests that are NOT part of the package: every prefix and single-character deletion of about 300 seed scripts (2511 cases), about 10000 semantic comparisons, and 7000+ random token-soup scripts, all at 0 differences once the harness artifacts were discounted. Facts that differed from what was expected and were fixed from probing: `//` with no earlier regex is a RUN-TIME error, address 0 is legal for `r`, `#` ends a label, whitespace does not end `s` flags, `\cA` is converted in a / i / c text. Mutation check: nine deliberate parser bugs, all caught (two as a crash). Suite: 45 test files, 0 failing, 6571 checks (was 44 and 5932), run on PJ's machine, stderr empty. Both new files were written with the Pulsar tools and checked byte for byte by SHA-256.

**Known deviations.** `L` is rejected as an unknown command (sed 4.9 accepts it and fails when run). sed opens `w` files while parsing, so a failed open hides later syntax errors; this pure parser cannot model that. "no previous regular expression" is left to the runner (R4c), as in sed.

**Not done.** The interpreter (R4c), the tool wiring and gating (R4d), the shared regex layer (R4e). `SED-R4-HANDOFF.md` in the project root records the design, every verified sed fact and how the verification was built.

## `lib/posix-regex.js`: a POSIX BRE / ERE regex engine that behaves like GNU sed's (TODO R4a) (2026-10-18; first step of R4, `sed` as a JS implementation of GNU sed)

**What changed.** New pure module `lib/posix-regex.js` (about 625 lines, no dependencies, no I/O): `compile(pattern, { extended, icase, multiline })` returns `{ nsub, exec(str, from) }`; `exec` gives `{ start, end }` Int32Arrays (index 0 is the whole match, -1 is a group that did not take part), or null. Errors are `RegexError` with the exact glibc message ("Unmatched ( or \\(", "Invalid preceding regular expression", "Invalid range end", and so on). **Nothing requires the module yet**: the `sed` tool, `sed-plan.js` and every other tool are untouched, so this change needs no restart and changes no behaviour. It is the foundation for R4b to R4e (script parser, interpreter, wiring, shared regex layer).

**Behaviour it reproduces (all checked against GNU sed 4.9).** Basic and extended syntax with the GNU extensions (`\+ \? \|` in BRE, `\w \W \s \S \b \B \< \> \` \'`, back-references `\1` to `\9`, intervals including `{,n}`, bracket expressions with `[:class:]`, `[=x=]` and `[.x.]`); leftmost match, then the longest overall match, then the first path in priority order for the groups (what glibc does); context rules for `*`, `^` and `$` in BRE; no repetition after an anchor; glibc's `X{m,n}` expansion and its "optional group that matches empty keeps its old capture" register rule; back-references that cannot see groups from an earlier alternative; the `I` flag; the `M` flag (`^` `$` at line breaks, `.` and `[^x]` stop at a newline, `\W` does not); `from` moves where the search starts while `^` and the word tests still look at the whole string; astral characters count as one character.

**Robustness.** State memoization makes catastrophic patterns such as `(a*)*b` or `(a|aa)+$` linear. One-character repeats (`.*`, `[a-z]+`, `a{2,9}`) run iteratively, so `.*foo` on a 30000-character line needs no recursion. A stack overflow in a deep group loop, or a runaway back-reference pattern (step limit 60 million), becomes `RegexError` "Regular expression too complex" instead of a hang or a raw RangeError.

**Tests.** New `test/posix-regex.test.js` (367 checks): 259 golden cases recorded from a real GNU sed 4.9 (`test/golden/posix-regex.golden.json`: s///g output on 10 fixed lines, group captures, and the exact error message for 48 invalid patterns), the search API, 18 `M`-flag results, and the robustness cases. No sed is needed to run it; `test/golden/gen-golden.js` and `curated-lists.js` regenerate the golden file on a machine that has sed. Built with a differential harness (the same pattern through real sed and through the engine, 245 curated patterns plus several thousand random ones): the curated set is at 0 differences and the random runs found and fixed back-reference scoping, bracket `-` handling, empty-iteration captures and the bounded-interval shape. Mutation check: five deliberate engine bugs, all caught; one (later equal-length path overwrites the earlier one) first survived, which exposed a gap in the golden set, so equal-length-path and back-reference cases were added. Suite: 44 test files, 0 failing, 5932 checks (was 43 and 5565), run on PJ's machine (Node 24, no sed installed), stderr empty. All five new files were written with the Pulsar tools and checked byte for byte against the sandbox copies by SHA-256.

**Known deviations (not asserted, listed in TODO R4a-dev).** (1) Assertions (`\< \> \b \B`, a mid-pattern `^` or `$`) inside repeated groups: GNU sed sometimes matches what no matcher can justify, for example `(^[Ab](b)){0,2}` on `abab` finds no match at all and `(\w\>a){,2}a` matches `ba` although `\>` cannot hold between `b` and `a`. (2) Capture assignment for three-level nested optional groups that match empty, for example `((.{0,2}){0,1}){2}`. About 0.5 percent of random patterns hit these; ordinary patterns do not.

**Not done.** `sed`'s own script-level rules (R4b), the interpreter (R4c), the tool wiring and gating (R4d), and the shared regex layer (R4e). sed-level escapes such as `\t`, `\xHH` and `s///` delimiter handling around brackets (`s/[/]/x/`) belong to R4b, not to this module.

## Mid-line failure wording for `delete` `startContent` / `endContent` (TODO R1j, part 2) (2026-10-18; live-verified after a restart)

**What changed.** A `delete` in Mode 4 (`startContent` + `endContent`) whose start or end anchor starts or ends mid-line now says what is actually wrong instead of blaming a typo or whitespace. The anchors match whole lines, so a fragment of a line cannot match. The failure now (1) names the anchor that failed ("startContent starts and ends in the middle of a line, and delete startContent only matches complete lines.", and the same for `endContent`); (2) drops the similarity line and the "close but not identical" blame; (3) ends with a NOTE as its last line: "<anchor> only matches whole lines. Extend <anchor> to cover the whole line (a range delete has no part-line mode). To remove just a span inside one line, use replace with fragment:true and new_str:''." Mode 4 has no retry nudge and no stash by design, so there was nothing to drop.

**Where.** `lib/mid-line-hint.js`: new kind `deleteAnchor` in `midLineWhat` (gives "delete " plus the anchor name, default `startContent`) and in `midLineAdvice`; `midLineKind('delete')` is still `deleteMatch`, because the two anchor paths pass `deleteAnchor` explicitly. `lib/mcp-registration.js`: imports `midLineHint`, `midLineAdvice` and `midLineWhat`; the start-anchor not-found path (about L2001) computes `_startMid` and the end-anchor path (about L2054) computes `_endMid`, each passed to `diagnoseFailure` as `midLine` plus `midLineWhatText`; each wrapper appends the NOTE after `_sd.post` / `_ed.post`, so the NOTE is the last line. The end-anchor hint is computed against the end scope (the lines after the start anchor), so text that exists only before the start anchor still says FOUND OUTSIDE SCOPE. The match engine needed no change: `diagnoseFailure` already supported `midLine` and `midLineWhatText` since part 1.

**Behaviour that did not change.** An absent anchor keeps the old wording (similarity line, "NOT FOUND ANYWHERE"). Mode 4 keeps its own "Closest area" block, the "Lines after startContent" list and the Tip on the end-anchor failure. A valid whole-line pair still gives a normal dry-run preview. `delete` `matchString`, `insert` and `replace` are unchanged from part 1.

**Tests.** New `test/mid-line-hint.delete-anchors.test.js` (25 checks), written first and seen red (14 failing; the engine-side checks already passed because the engine already supported the hint, so the red checks were the new kind and the wiring). Suite: 43 test files, 0 failing, 5565 checks (was 42 and 5540), re-run after the live probes with empty stderr.

**Live-verified.** After a full restart, on a scratch file in Windows TEMP (disk read back unchanged, 9 lines), delete dry runs only: (1) a mid-line `startContent` ("const total") with a valid `endContent` failed with the sentence naming `startContent`, no similarity line, and the NOTE as the last line; (2) a valid `startContent` with a mid-line `endContent` ("return total") failed with the sentence naming `endContent`, the "Lines after startContent" list and the Tip still present, and the NOTE as the last line (the consecutive-failure banner sits above the match-location line, and the NOTE is still last). Controls: an absent `startContent` and an absent `endContent` each kept the old wording (similarity 94 and 93 percent, NOT FOUND ANYWHERE, no NOTE); a valid whole-line pair previewed "will delete 2 line(s) (lines 2-3)" as before.

**Not done.** `delete` and `insert` failures that are not mid-line still say `old_str`; passing `subject` for all failures is a possible later change (TODO R1k).

## Mid-line failure wording for `delete` `matchString` and `insert` `afterContent` / `beforeContent` (TODO R1j, part 1) (2026-10-18; live-verified after a restart)

**What changed.** A `delete` whose `matchString`, or an `insert` whose `afterContent` / `beforeContent`, starts or ends mid-line now says what is actually wrong instead of blaming a typo or whitespace. These parameters match whole lines, like `replace`'s `old_str`, so a fragment of a line cannot match. The failure now (1) names the caller's own parameter ("matchString starts and ends in the middle of a line, and delete matchString only matches complete lines.", and the same for `afterContent` / `beforeContent` with "insert"), never `old_str`; (2) ends with a NOTE that gives the right alternative per tool; (3) prints no "Retry cheaply" nudge and returns `retryStash:false` (R1i, for free).

**The NOTE per tool.** `delete` `matchString`: use `replace` with `fragment:true` and `new_str:''` to remove just that span, or extend `matchString` to whole lines if those lines are to be deleted. `insert` `afterContent`: use `afterString` (it matches text inside a line and inserts after that line) or extend to whole lines; `beforeContent` says `beforeString` and "before". `replace` is unchanged: same sentence, same NOTE.

**Where.** `lib/mid-line-hint.js`: new `midLineKind(tool)`, `midLineWhat(kind, subject)`, and `midLineAdvice(kind, subject)`; `midLineAdvice()` with no argument is still the `replace` wording. `lib/fail-diagnostics.js` `diagnose()`: new `midLineWhat` parameter replaces the hard-coded "a whole-line replace". `lib/match-engine.js`: `diagnoseFailure()` forwards it (`midLineWhatText`); `buildFailResponse()` takes an optional `subject`, used ONLY when `midLine` is set, so every other failure keeps its old text byte-for-byte (pinned by a test). `lib/mcp-registration.js`: the `insert` content-anchor failure path and the `delete` `matchString` failure path compute `midLineHint` (skipped for an ambiguous match, scope bounds as in `replace`) and pass `midLine` and `subject` (`hintName` for `insert`, `'matchString'` for `delete`).

**Tests.** New `test/mid-line-hint.per-tool.test.js` (42 checks), written first and seen red (28 failing) before the code: the three helpers, the engine wording per tool, `replace` unchanged, a subject without a midLine changes nothing, and a source scan that the two handlers are wired (the ambiguity check is anchored to the `_midLine` assignment so it cannot pass vacuously). Suite: 42 test files, 0 failing, 5540 checks (was 41 and 5498). `insert` has no retry nudge (it is not in `RETRY_FIELD_BY_TOOL`), so the nudge is asserted for `delete` only.

**Live-verified.** After a full restart, on `lib/mid-line-hint.js`, dry runs only (disk not written): a mid-line `delete` `matchString`, `insert` `afterContent` and `insert` `beforeContent` each failed with the new sentence naming their own parameter, the right NOTE as the last line, no `old_str`, and no "Retry cheaply" line. Controls: an absent `matchString` and an absent `afterContent` kept the old wording (`old_str`, similarity, and for `delete` the retry nudge); a valid whole-line `delete` and `insert` still gave a normal dry-run preview; and the NOTE's own alternatives ran as dry runs (`replace` with `fragment:true` and an empty `new_str`, and `insert` with `afterString`).

**Not done in part 1 (R1j part 2, since done: see the entry above).** `delete` `startContent` / `endContent` showed the old wording; they call `diagnoseFailure` directly (the not-found paths at about L2001 and L2050 of `lib/mcp-registration.js`; the `buildFailResponse` call at about L1979 only handles `occurrenceOutOfRange`) with their own `subject`, so they already name their own parameter but have no mid-line hint. Their messages are hand-written wrappers around `_sd.pre` / `_ed.pre` and keep Mode 4's own context preview, so part 2 needs `midLine` computed against each anchor's own scope, a `midLineAdvice` kind of its own (extend the anchor to whole lines), and the wrapper to append that NOTE. Non-mid-line failures of `delete` and `insert` still say `old_str`; passing `subject` for all failures is a possible later change.

## `replace` description and refusal mention the mid-line case, docs follow-up to R1i (2026-10-17; live-verified after a restart)

**What changed.** Text only, no logic. `lib/mcp-registration.js`, `replace` registration: the description sentence about the bare-retry shortcut now also says it is not offered when `old_str` starts or ends mid-line (resend the whole call with `fragment:true`; a mid-line failure is not kept for the shortcut and also clears any older stashed failure), and the "new_str is required" refusal now says the failed-call shortcut is "not possible after a mid-line failure". Docs: the README `lib/fragment-match.js` entry now mentions `matchSpan()`; the "Not covered" line about `matchSpan` in the 2026-10-16 multi-line entry is marked fixed (R1f); the "Last audited" blockquote is updated for the 2026-10-16 and 2026-10-17 `replace` work and says the 2026-10-14 to 2026-10-16 entries were not re-audited. No test pinned either string. Suite: 41 test files, 0 failing, 5498 checks.

**Live-verified.** After a full restart: the `replace` description shown by the client includes the new mid-line sentence; and on a scratch file in Windows TEMP (disk not written), a mid-line `replace` dry run failed with the NOTE as its last line, and a bare `{ filePath, old_str }` call straight after it was refused with "new_str is required ..., unless you are retrying a failed call (not possible after a mid-line failure) with just ...".

## Mid-line failure: wording said once, no retry nudge, not stashed (TODO R1i) (2026-10-17; live-verified after a restart)

**What changed.** A `replace` failure whose `old_str` starts or ends mid-line (R1e, R1h) now (1) explains itself once: the NOTE is cut to the action only and no longer repeats the "a whole-line replace only matches complete lines" sentence the diagnosis already has, and it now says to resend the whole call (`new_str` included) with `fragment:true`, or to extend `old_str` to whole lines and make `new_str` the full replacement lines; (2) prints no "Retry cheaply" nudge; (3) is not stashed for the bare `{ filePath, old_str }` retry, and clears any older stashed failure for that tool.

**Why (data-loss hazard).** The bare-retry merge fires for exactly `{ filePath, old_str }` and carries the old `new_str`. After a mid-line failure, extending `old_str` to the whole line and bare-resending would have replaced the whole line with the partial `new_str` (for example `const sum = 1` replacing `  const total = 1;`, dropping the indent and the semicolon). The new framework test reproduced exactly this before the fix.

**Where.** `lib/match-engine.js` `buildFailResponse`: no nudge when `midLine` is set, and the result carries `retryStash:false`. `lib/tool-framework.js`: a result with `retryStash:false` is not stashed and deletes this tool's pending failure (`matched:true` still clears it as before). `lib/mid-line-hint.js`: `midLineAdvice()` takes no argument and returns the action-only NOTE; `midLineEdge` is now used by the diagnosis only.

**Tests.** New `test/match-engine.mid-line-nudge.test.js` (18 checks) and `test/tool-framework.retry-stash.test.js` (5 checks, stubbed `atom`, scripted handler); `test/mid-line-hint.test.js` and `test/fail-diagnostics.mid-line.test.js` updated for the new wording. Suite: 41 test files, 0 failing, 5498 checks (was 39 and 5476).

**Live-verified.** After a full restart, on a scratch file in Windows TEMP (disk read back unchanged): (1) a mid-line `replace` dry run showed the explanation once, ended with the NOTE, and had no "Retry cheaply" line; (2) a bare `{ filePath, old_str }` call straight after it was not merged and was refused with "new_str is required" (the new framework test shows the old code merged the stashed `new_str` and `dryRun` here; the pre-fix behaviour was not run live); (3) a normal not-found failure still showed the nudge, and a bare retry after it still merged (it returned the dry-run preview); (4) a normal failure, then a mid-line failure, then a bare resend: the older stashed call was NOT merged (refused with "new_str is required"), so the mid-line failure cleared it. The "new_str is required" refusal still says "unless you are retrying a failed call", which is slightly off after a mid-line failure but harmless.

## `matchSpan` moved into `lib/fragment-match.js` (TODO R1f) (2026-10-17; live-verified after a restart)

**What changed.** Refactor only, no behaviour change. The multi-line span matcher `matchSpan()` and its identifier-guard helper `spanGlued()` moved from `lib/replace-fragment.js` into `lib/fragment-match.js`, which now exports `{ matchFragment, matchSpan }`. Other tools (for example a future port of the retired `str_replace` fragment path) can reuse the span matcher without importing the `replace` planner.

**Where.**
- `lib/fragment-match.js`: requires `findHits` from `lib/edit-match.js` (no circular require: `edit-match.js` does not require `fragment-match.js`); gains `spanGlued` and `matchSpan`, copied verbatim; the SCOPE header comment now says `matchSpan` handles multi-line needles while `matchFragment` stays single-line.
- `lib/replace-fragment.js`: imports `matchSpan` from `./fragment-match`; the `findHits` and `checkIdentifierBoundary` requires were dropped because nothing else in the file used them; `spanText` (used by the drift check) stays. Two stale comments reworded.

**Behaviour that did not change.** Same inputs give the same refusals, messages and stat keys. `node --check` clean on both files. Suite before and after: 39 test files, 0 failing, 5476 checks.

**Live-verified.** After a full restart, six `replace` dry runs on a scratch file in Windows TEMP, nothing written (disk read back unchanged): (1) a multi-line `fragment:true` span that starts mid-line on one row and ends mid-line on the next matched at the right line and the diff kept the text around the span; (2) a span whose first edge is glued to a longer identifier (`ount` inside `count`) was refused with the identifier-glued message; (3) the same span with `guardIdentifier:false` matched; (4) `occurrence:2` with one candidate gave the out-of-range refusal; (5) a multi-line needle that is not in the file gave "fragment not found in scope"; (6) a single-line `fragment:true` dry run still matched (the unchanged `matchFragment` path).

## A mid-line whole-line `replace` failure no longer blames a typo or whitespace (TODO R1h) (2026-10-17; live-verified after a restart)

**What changed.** The R1e NOTE (retry with `fragment:true`) used to sit underneath older lines that pointed at the wrong cause: "Content is close but not identical ... a typo, a wrong character, or an invisible/Unicode difference", "MATCH LOCATION FOUND ... but exact text comparison failed (see whitespace/partial match detail above)" and "Re-read lines around N ... rebuild old_str". When the mid-line hint fires, the failure now reads: the Closest area block, then "The text itself exists in the file, so the problem is not the text: old_str starts and ends in the middle of a line (or just starts / just ends), and a whole-line replace only matches complete lines", then the match location ending "the text is there; it only fails as a whole-line match." (no re-read advice), then the NOTE, then the retry nudge last. The PARTIAL MATCH "likely trailing whitespace" blame is suppressed in the same case.

**Where.**
- `lib/fail-diagnostics.js`: `diagnose()` takes an optional `midLine`. It adds one similarity-line branch (after the exact-outside-scope and found-elsewhere branches, which keep priority, and before the comment-led branch), changes the two location messages (scoped and unscoped), and gates the PARTIAL MATCH line. It requires `lib/mid-line-hint.js` for the shared edge wording.
- `lib/mid-line-hint.js`: new export `midLineEdge()`, used by both the diagnosis and the NOTE.
- `lib/match-engine.js`: `diagnoseFailure` and `buildFailResponse` take `midLine`; `buildFailResponse` passes it to the diagnosis and now adds the NOTE itself, so the wording lives in one place.
- `lib/mcp-registration.js`: the `replace` whole-line failure path passes `midLine` instead of building the advice.
