- `test/fail-diagnostics.test.js`: the negative-control copy list gained `mid-line-hint.js` (13 controls failed without it, because the temp copy of `fail-diagnostics.js` could not require the new module).

**Behaviour that did not change.** Without `midLine` every old string is identical (a test pins it). Text that exists only outside the scope still says FOUND OUTSIDE SCOPE with no mid-line wording, and text found only ignoring whitespace still says FOUND ELSEWHERE. Only `replace` receives the hint; `delete`, `replace-block`, `replace-function-body`, `insert` and the retired `str_replace` keep their old wording for mid-line needles.

**Tests.** New `test/fail-diagnostics.mid-line.test.js`, 39 checks, seen red first (21 failing). Suite: 39 test files, 0 failing, 5476 checks (was 38 files, 5437).

**Live-verified (scratch file in Windows TEMP, dry runs).** Mid-line `old_str` with `inFunction:'alpha'`: new sentence, location ending "the text is there", NOTE, retry nudge last, and none of the old typo, "exact text comparison failed" or "Re-read lines" lines. The same without a scope hint: the same shape with "old_str found at L8 (inside beta())". Mid-line text that exists only in `beta`, searched in `alpha`: FOUND OUTSIDE SCOPE, no mid-line wording. A whole-line `old_str`: matches. Absent text: the old NOT FOUND ANYWHERE wording, unchanged.

**Known leftover.** The NOTE and the new similarity-line sentence both say the text starts/ends mid-line, and the retry nudge after them still reads as if the content were wrong. It is repetitive but no longer contradictory.

## A mid-line whole-line `replace` failure says to retry with `fragment:true` (TODO R1e) (2026-10-17; live-verified after a restart)

**What changed.** When a whole-line `replace` fails because `old_str` starts or ends in the middle of a line, the failure now carries one extra line before the retry nudge: `NOTE: old_str exists in the file but starts and ends in the middle of a line (or just starts / just ends), and a whole-line replace only matches complete lines. Retry with fragment:true to replace just that span (the rest of the line is left untouched), or extend old_str to cover the whole line(s).` Before, the caller got a generic "not found" and no hint that fragment mode existed.

**Where.**
- New `lib/mid-line-hint.js` (pure): `midLineHint()` returns which edge is mid-line for the first literal hit that is inside the searched scope rows, else null. It stays silent for `regex`, `fuzzyWhitespace` and `fuzzyContent` calls, for an empty or whitespace-only needle, and for hits outside the scope (that case is a FOUND OUTSIDE SCOPE failure and a fragment retry would fail the same way). CRLF text is treated like LF. `midLineAdvice()` builds the ASCII wording.
- `lib/match-engine.js`, `buildFailResponse`: new optional `extraAdvice` array. The lines go after the diagnosis and before the retry nudge, which stays last. Without it nothing changes.
- `lib/mcp-registration.js`: the `replace` whole-line failure path computes the hint (skipped for an ambiguous match) and passes it as `extraAdvice`. Only `replace` is wired; `delete`, `replace-block`, `replace-function-body`, `insert` and the retired `str_replace` are untouched.

**Tests.** New `test/mid-line-hint.test.js`, 34 checks (detection, scope rows, CRLF, wording, ordering before the nudge, wiring), seen red first. Suite: 38 test files, 0 failing, 5437 checks (was 37 files, 5403).

**Live-verified (scratch file in Windows TEMP, dry runs).** Mid-line `old_str` in `inFunction:'alpha'`: the NOTE appears above the retry line. The same text as a whole line matches normally. Absent text: no NOTE. The mid-line text with `fragment:true`: matches. Mid-line text that exists only in another function: FOUND OUTSIDE SCOPE and no NOTE.

**Known gap (fixed the same day, see the R1h entry above).** The older lines of the same failure still blamed whitespace or a typo ("close but not identical", "exact text comparison failed", "re-read lines around N"), so the NOTE sat under text that pointed at the wrong cause.

## `replace` description and title no longer say EXPERIMENTAL (TODO R1d) (2026-10-17; live-verified after a restart and a new MCP session)

**What changed.** Wording only, no behaviour change. The `replace` tool description, title and catalogue entry now present it as the default edit tool that takes over from the retired `str_replace`, instead of an experimental sibling "for comparison" with `str_replace` as the "reference tool for everyday use". This was the last open item of TODO R1 (replace parity): R1a and R1b (retry), R1c (rescue-path audit, closed) and R1g (no nudge after an outside-scope failure) are done, and the multi-line fragment gap closed earlier on 2026-10-17.

**Where.**
- `lib/mcp-registration.js`, `replace` registration: title `Replace (engine-native)` is now `Replace`; the first description line says "a PART of a line or a multi-line span" (it said "a single line", which has been wrong since multi-line fragments landed); the "EXPERIMENTAL / FOR COMPARISON" sentence is replaced by a statement that this is the default edit tool, that `str_replace` is retired (still registered, not extended), and how the bare retry works, including that it is not offered when the text exists outside the scope hint.
- `lib/tool-catalogue.js`, the `replace` entry: the same change. It also said "no mid-line fragment matching ... for a mid-line edit use str_replace instead", which has been wrong since `fragment:true`, so it now describes fragment mode and the retry.

**Behaviour that did not change.** `str_replace` is registered with its old description and is untouched. Schemas, handlers and stats are unchanged. Suite: 37 test files, 0 failing, 5403 checks, same as before; `node --check` clean on both files.

**Live-verified.** After a full restart and a new MCP session the `replace` tool description starts with the new first line (a PART of a line or a multi-line span), says it is the default edit tool that takes over from the retired `str_replace`, explains the bare retry, and contains no EXPERIMENTAL or FOR COMPARISON text. The title is not shown in the tool schema, so only the description was checked.

## No retry nudge after a FOUND OUTSIDE SCOPE failure (TODO R1g) (2026-10-17; live-verified after a restart)

**What changed.** When `old_str` exists in the file but not inside the resolved scope (for example `inFunction:'alpha'` while the text lives in `beta`), the failure still prints the FOUND OUTSIDE SCOPE diagnosis but no longer ends with the "Retry cheaply ... just { filePath, old_str }" line. That advice was wrong for this failure: the text is right and the scope hint is what needs changing, and a bare resend merges the stashed call, which keeps the wrong scope, so following the nudge failed again with the same message.

**Where.** `lib/match-engine.js`, `buildFailResponse` default framing: `diagnoseFailure` already returned `diag`; the nudge line is now `if (!diag.foundOutsideScope) parts.push(retryNudge(tool))`. It applies to every tool that gets the nudge (`replace`, `delete`, `replace-block`, `replace-function-body`).

**Behaviour that did not change.** The failure still returns `matched:false` (so it still stashes; nothing advertises a bare resend any more). Every other failure keeps the nudge exactly as before: not found anywhere, with or without a scope hint, and a near miss inside the scope. `insert` never had a nudge. `str_replace` is retired and untouched (it keeps the same wording).

**Tests.** New `test/match-engine.outside-scope-nudge.test.js`, 20 checks, seen red first (4 failures, one per tool), then green. Suite: 37 test files, 0 failing, 5403 checks (was 36 files, 5383).

**Live-verified (scratch file in Windows TEMP, dry runs).** `replace` with `inFunction:'alpha'` on text that exists only in `beta` shows FOUND OUTSIDE SCOPE (hit at L7 inside beta()) and no retry line. The same call with text that exists nowhere still ends with the retry nudge.

## `replace` supports the cheap retry: bare resend and retry nudge (TODO R1a, R1b) (2026-10-17; live-verified after a restart)

**What changed.** When a `replace` call fails in a way the caller can fix by correcting `old_str`, the refusal now ends with the same retry nudge `str_replace` gives, and the failed call is stashed. The caller can then resend just `{ filePath, old_str: '<corrected>' }`: the stashed `new_str` and flags are merged in. Before this, `replace` had no retry support at all, which was one of the three remaining gaps before it could take over from `str_replace` (the other two are the rescue-path audit R1c and the description reword R1d).

**Where.**
- `lib/match-engine.js`: `RETRY_FIELD_BY_TOOL` gets `replace: 'old_str'`.
- `lib/replace-fragment.js`: new exported `isRetryableRefusal(reason)`. It is true only for `noMatch`, `ambiguous` and `onlyIdentifierGlued`.
- `lib/mcp-registration.js`, `replace` registration: `features.retryOnFail { field: 'old_str' }`. The schema now declares `new_str` as optional (`z.string().optional()`). A guard at the very top of the handler refuses a call with no `new_str` and returns `found:false` with no `matched` key, so a refused bare call stashes nothing. `_fragRefuse` returns `matched:false` plus `retryNudge(curTool)` for the retryable reasons only. Whole-line failures already carried `matched:false` through `buildFailResponse`, so they stash with no further change.

**Behaviour that did not change.** Flag-clash refusals (`fragment:true` with `regex`, `fuzzyWhitespace` or `fuzzyContent`) and out-of-range `occurrence` refusals carry no nudge, because correcting `old_str` cannot fix them. `delete`, `replace-block` and `replace-function-body` are in `RETRY_FIELD_BY_TOOL` but still register no `retryOnFail` (TODO I4). `str_replace` is untouched.

**Tests.** New `test/match-engine.retry-nudge.test.js`, 12 checks: nudge text; the four existing tools unchanged; a source scan that every `retryOnFail` registration has a matching nudge field; `new_str` optional with the guard first and without a `matched:` key; `isRetryableRefusal` against real planner refusals. Suite: 36 test files, 0 failing, 5383 checks (was 35 files, 5371).

**Live-verified (after a full restart, on a scratch file; dry runs except where stated).**
- A whole-line not-found refusal shows the nudge, and a bare `{ filePath, old_str }` resend merges the stashed `new_str` and `dryRun` and matches the right line.
- Fragment not-found, glued-identifier and ambiguous refusals each carry the nudge and stash; a bare resend merged `fragment:true` and `new_str` every time.
- A call with no `new_str` and no pending failure is refused with the text `new_str is required (pass '' to delete the matched text), unless you are retrying a failed call ...`, and a pending failure on another path is not disturbed.
- Flag-clash and out-of-range `occurrence` refusals carry no nudge.
- After a successful commit on the same file, a bare resend is refused (the stale pending failure is swept). A successful dry run sweeps it as well.
- Disk read back after the probes: only the one intended commit had landed.

## `replace` with `fragment:true` now matches multi-line spans (2026-10-16 to 2026-10-17; live-verified after a restart)

**What changed.** A multi-line `old_str` is no longer refused in part-line mode. It may start and end mid-line, and only the matched span is replaced; the text before the first line's match and after the last line's match is untouched. Before this, only a single-line fragment worked, so a span such as the end of one line plus the start of the next had no `replace` equivalent (the gap that mattered most for retiring `str_replace`, which has always matched exact spans).

**Where.**
- `lib/replace-fragment.js`: `MSG_MULTI_LINE` removed. New `matchSpan()` matches a multi-line needle with `findHits` from `lib/edit-match.js` (scope `wholeText`, rows joined with LF) and returns the matchFragment shape plus `endRow`. `spanGlued()` applies the identifier guard at both ends: the character before the first row's match and the character after the last row's match. The planner returns `endRow` on the edit.
- `lib/match-engine.js`: `applyEditSpan` has a multi-row branch (used when `match.endRow` differs from `match.row`). It re-verifies the covered text byte for byte before writing and refuses on drift; it does not relocate, because guessing a new location for text that crosses lines is not safe. Single-row behaviour is unchanged.
- `lib/mcp-registration.js`: the `replace` fragment branch builds the dry-run preview over the whole row range, reports `linesChanged` as new lines minus old rows, and returns `matchEndLine` as the last row. The tool description now says a fragment may span several lines.

**Behaviour that did not change.** `fragment:true` still refuses `regex`, `fuzzyWhitespace` and `fuzzyContent`; ambiguity, `occurrence` (out of range is refused, never clamped) and `guardIdentifier:false` work as for a single line. `str_replace` is untouched.

**Tests.** New `test/replace-fragment.multiline.test.js`, 52 checks. Suite: 35 test files, 0 failing, 5371 checks (was 34 files, 5319).

**Live-verified (after a full restart, on a scratch file).** Dry runs: a two-line span starting and ending mid-line (diff shows only the matched span changed); a span whose first edge is glued to a longer identifier (`count` inside `discount`) is refused; a span whose last edge is glued (`func` inside `function`) is refused; three identical call lines give an ambiguity refusal listing lines 6 and 7 (overlapping spans count separately, as overlapping single-line hits already did); `occurrence:2` picks the second span; `fragment:true` with `regex:true` is refused with the flag-clash text; `guardIdentifier:false` lets the glued span through. Commits: a two-row span replaced by three rows reported `+1 line`, and the revert reported `-1 line`; the file on disk was byte-identical to the original afterwards.

**Not covered.**
- A drifted multi-row span is covered by unit tests only; it cannot be triggered live.
- CRLF multi-line needles were not probed live.
- `matchSpan` lives in `replace-fragment.js`, not `fragment-match.js`, so other tools would have to import it from the `replace` planner. (Fixed 2026-10-17: moved into `lib/fragment-match.js`, TODO R1f, see the entry near the top.)

## Function ranges survive braces in comments, strings, templates and regex literals (2026-10-16)

**Bug.** `inFunction:'matchContent'` on `lib/match-engine.js` resolved the function to its signature line only (394 to 394 instead of 394 to 567). `bodyEndRow` in `lib/tree-sitter-symbols.js` counted every brace in the body, including braces inside comments and strings. `matchContent` has exactly one stray brace, in a comment that quotes the text `function alpha() {`, so the depth never returned to 0, `bodyEndRow` returned -1 and `getSymbolsFromText` turned that into "end row = signature row". Every tool that resolves a function range (`inFunction`, `afterFunction`, `beforeFunction`, `replace-function-body`, `read`, `delete`) inherited the one-line range. A scan of `lib`, `test` and the package root found 9 real functions in this state: `findFunctionInBuffer`, `ChatPanel`, `matchContent`, `matchBlock`, `mcpRegistration`, `checkLines`, `makeRegisterMcpTool`, `bodyEndRow` itself and `getSymbolsFromText`. This is the open-work item "braces inside strings, comments and regex literals are still counted".

**Fix.** New `makeLineMasker()` in `lib/tree-sitter-symbols.js`: a stateful function that returns each line with comment text, string and char literal contents, template literal text and regex literals blanked to spaces (same length, so columns still line up). It carries block comments and multi-line template literals (with nested `${ }` expressions) from row to row, and decides regex versus division from the previous significant character or keyword. `bodyEndRow` now runs its signature scan (parens) and its body scan (braces) on the masked rows, lazily and in order, so only rows up to the closing brace are scanned. Heuristic, not a parser.

**Not changed.**
- `braceEndRow` is untouched (the `U` tests pin it, and `lib/mcp-registration.js` imports it as `_tssBraceEndRow`). The no-body fallback inside `bodyEndRow` and the `registerTool` path still count every brace.
- Strings and regex literals end at the end of the line; a backslash line continuation inside a string is not followed.
- The tree-sitter path (`getSymbolsFromEditor`) takes ranges from the syntax tree and was never affected.

**Tests.** New `test/tree-sitter-symbols.code-mask.test.js`, 25 checks: 13 `M` cases (line and block comments, single, double and escaped quotes, multi-line template with a nested expression, regex literals, division, `//` and `/*` inside strings and regexes, a paren in a signature default), 6 `N` cases that must not change, and 6 `G` cases including the real `matchContent`. 12 failed before the fix (the real-file check reproduced `[393, 393]`). In `test/tree-sitter-symbols.test.js`, `B13` used to pin the old limitation ("} inside a string ends the body early", expected 1) and now expects 2. Suite: 34 test files, 0 failing, 5319 checks (was 33 files, 5294). Rescan after the fix: only brace-less one-line declarations still return -1, which is correct for them.

**Live status.** Live-verified after a restart (2026-10-16): `read` with `inFunction:'matchContent'` on `lib/match-engine.js` returned lines 394 to 567 (174 lines, last line the closing brace); before the fix it returned line 394 only. `inFunction:'bodyEndRow'` on `lib/tree-sitter-symbols.js` returned lines 215 to 247.

## Drift nudge prints the window the caller used, not always the default (2026-10-15)

**Bug.** A failed edit that used `afterLine` or `beforeLine` printed "window (25 lines)" even when the caller passed `hintRadius`. The real window was right all along: `resolveScope` hands the caller's radius to the line, string and symbol resolvers. Only the label was wrong, because `buildFailResponse` gave `diagnose()` the constant `HINT_RADIUS`. Reproduced end to end before the fix with `{afterLine:1, hintRadius:5}` (and 7, 40).

**Fix.** Two small edits in `lib/match-engine.js`. `resolveScope` records the effective radius (`params.hintRadius || HINT_RADIUS`, the same expression the resolvers use) on the scope as `scope.hintRadius`, and `buildFailResponse` passes that to `diagnose()`. It is deliberately NOT added to `scope.hints`, because hints feed `classify()` and the hint names in the fault log. `lib/fail-diagnostics.js` already printed whatever radius it was given and is unchanged.

**Not changed.**
- The retired `str_replace` still prints the constant, on purpose: `lib/mcp-registration.js` (lines 383 and 953 at the time of writing) reproduces the old label.
- The comment on `hintRadius` in `lib/schema.js` still says "+/-25 window" although the windows are one-sided (after: anchor to anchor+radius, before: anchor-radius to anchor). Fixed on 2026-10-16: the comment now describes the one-sided window (comment-only change).

**Tests.** New `test/match-engine.hint-radius-label.test.js`, 8 checks, 3 failing before the fix, all passing after. They also check that the real window honours the radius, that no label is printed for other hints, that a hand-built scope falls back to the default, and that `scope.hints` gains no `hintRadius` key. Suite: 33 test files, 0 failing, 5294 checks (was 32 files, 5286).

**Live status.** Live-verified after a restart (2026-10-15), on a scratch file: `replace` with `dryRun:true`, `afterLine:1`, `hintRadius:5` and an old_str that is not in the file printed "afterLine:1 window (5 lines)"; the same call without `hintRadius` still printed "window (25 lines)".

## autoRescue no longer guesses when occurrence is above 1 (2026-10-15)

**Bug.** `matchContent` runs the combined whitespace + unicode rescue (`recover.js` `profiledMatch`) only when the exact and fuzzy waterfall found nothing. The rescue ignores `occurrence` and reduces its answer to one hit, so `occurrence:K` could never mean "the Kth rescued match". Reproduced in `test/match-engine.rescue-occurrence.test.js` before the fix: with one fuzzy-only candidate, `occurrence:2` (or 9) failed as "has only 1 match" although nothing had matched exactly; with two fuzzy-only candidates, `occurrence:2` failed as `ambiguous` and told the caller to "use occurrence:N", which they had just done.

**Fix.** One condition in `lib/match-engine.js`: the rescue is skipped when `occurrence` is a whole number above 1. The result is a plain `noMatch`, which the existing diagnosis explains. Unchanged: no occurrence and `occurrence:1` still rescue; `autoRescue:false` is unchanged; `0`, `-1` and `1.5` are still refused as out of range; exact matches never reach the rescue (two exact matches with `occurrence:2` still pick the second).

**Tests.** New `test/match-engine.rescue-occurrence.test.js`, 11 checks, 3 failing before the fix, all passing after. Suite: 32 test files, 0 failing, 5286 checks (was 31 files, 5275).

**Not done.** No wording was added saying why the rescue was skipped; what the caller sees is the existing noMatch diagnosis (seen live: it reports the whitespace mismatch and suggests `fuzzyWhitespace:true`). Option (c), a rescue that lists every candidate and honours the occurrence, was not attempted.

**Live status.** Live-verified after a restart (2026-10-15), on a scratch file whose line differed from old_str by indentation only (the curly-quote variant is covered by the unit test): `replace` with `dryRun:true` and `occurrence:2` gave "content not found" with the whitespace-mismatch diagnosis; `occurrence:1` still previewed the rescued match.

## Read pair anchors: out-of-range occurrence refused the way the engine refuses it (2026-10-15)

**Bug.** `read` with `startContent` + `endContent` (or `betweenHint`) falls back to a substring-per-line match when the engine finds no whole-line match. That fallback, `findSubstringInLines`, mishandled `occurrence`. A whole number past the matches came back as plain "start anchor not found", with no count and no line list. `0`, a negative number or a fraction came back as `matched:true` with an undefined `matchLine` and a NaN `matchEndLine`. (The open-work note called this "clamping"; nothing was clamped.)

**Fix.**
- `findSubstringInLines` moved, unchanged, out of the read handler (it was a closure, so it could not be unit tested) into `lib/substring-lines.js`; `lib/mcp-registration.js` requires it.
- It now refuses an occurrence that is not a whole number from 1 to the number of matches, using `occurrenceFail(...)`: the exact result shape `lib/match-engine.js` gives. The read handler already had a branch for it, so the message is: "start anchor: occurrence:K requested but only N matches found (lines ...). Use an occurrence between 1 and N."
- `occurrenceOutOfRange` and `occurrenceFail` are now exported from `lib/match-engine.js`, so there is one definition of "out of range" and no second copy to drift.
- Unchanged and pinned by the test: no occurrence with one match, several matches (still ambiguous), `fromRow`, nothing found (still noMatch, even when an occurrence is given).
- Counting: the failure still counts under `read` `fails.startNotFound`, as before. No new stats key.
- Tests: new `test/substring-lines.test.js`, 18 checks. Seen red first (7 failing against the old logic), then green. Suite: 31 test files, 0 failing, 5275 checks (was 30 files, 5257).
- Side effect: `lib/substring-lines.js` requires `match-engine.js`, so running its test outside Pulsar prints the harmless `[edit-stats] _flushSync failed` line described in the session notes.

**Live status.** Live-verified after a restart (2026-10-15), on a scratch file with three `call foo(` lines: `read` with `startContent`, `endContent` and `occurrence:5` gave "start anchor: occurrence:5 requested but only 3 matches found (lines 4, 6, 8). Use an occurrence between 1 and 3."; `occurrence:2` still read from the second match. The `read` schema declares `occurrence` as an integer with a minimum of 1, so `0` and `1.5` never reach the handler and are covered by the unit test only.

## Out-of-range occurrence: right failure bucket and right wording (2026-10-14)

**Bug.** An `occurrence` past the matches was misfiled and mis-worded. `insert` and `replace-block` counted it under `fails.anchorNotFound`, and `replace-function-body` under `fails.ambiguous` / `fails.ambiguousHint`. For `inFunction`, `afterFunction`, `beforeFunction`, `afterSymbol`, `beforeSymbol`, `afterString` and `beforeString` the message said "Use occurrence:N to disambiguate" (or "use a more unique anchor") although `occurrence` had been passed.

**Fix.**
- Buckets: `scopeErrorIsOutOfRange(error, occurrence)` in `lib/match-engine.js` tells the three tools when a scope error is really an out-of-range request, and they count it under `fails.outOfRange`.
- Wording: `resolveScope` tags an ambiguous error with the occurrence the caller passed, and `buildFailResponse` says "has only N matches (lines ...) but occurrence:K was requested. Nothing was changed -- use an occurrence between 1 and N.", the same wording banner and ifdefBlock already used. With no occurrence the original advice is unchanged.
- The `ambiguityCheck` text no longer says an unscoped duplicate "would target occurrence 1 blindly"; it says match 1 would be a blind pick.
- Occurrence guard: `occurrence` counts occurrences, so it must be a whole number from 1. `0`, negative numbers and fractions such as `1.5` used to slip past `occurrence <= matches` and make `resolveScope` throw `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'startRow')` for `inFunction`, `afterFunction`, `beforeFunction`, `afterSymbol`, `beforeSymbol`, `afterString` and `beforeString`. They are now refused and counted under `fails.outOfRange`, with a message that says why: "has N matches (lines ...) but occurrence:K was requested. Nothing was changed -- occurrence must be a whole number between 1 and N (it counts matches from 1)." A whole number past the end keeps the plain "use an occurrence between 1 and N" advice.

**Tests.** `test/match-engine.scope-error-bucket.test.js` (10 checks; its `ifdefBlock` fixture needed `#endif /* FOO */` lines, because the anchor only pairs an `#endif` that names the macro) and `test/match-engine.scope-error-message.test.js` (20 checks, written first and seen failing, including the whole-number guard and its wording). Suite: 30 files, 5257 checks, 0 failing.

**Live status.** Live and probed after a full Pulsar restart (2026-10-14). `replace-function-body` with `inFunction` on a name defined twice and `occurrence:5` says "has only 2 matches (lines 2, 10) but occurrence:5 was requested" and counts one `fails.outOfRange`; with no `occurrence` it still says "Use occurrence:N to disambiguate" and counts one `fails.ambiguousHint`. `insert` and `replace-block` were not probed live; they share the helper and are covered by the unit tests. Probed again after the 2026-10-15 restart (0.24.0 code loaded): `occurrence:5` and the no-occurrence case behave exactly as above. The tool schemas (`occurrence` is an integer, minimum 1) refuse `0` and `1.5` before the engine sees them, so the whole-number wording is covered by the unit test only.

## Diagnosis module renamed: `lib/elsewhere-diagnosis.js` is now `lib/outside-scope-diagnosis.js` (2026-10-14)

**What changed.** The file name only. No behaviour changed: 28 test files, 0 failing, 5224 checks, the same before and after. The exported function names (`diagnoseElsewhere`, `formatElsewhereAdvice`) and the `elsewhere` field on the `diagnose()` result were deliberately left alone, so no caller and no message text changed.

No reference was left in `lib/`, `test/` or TEMP.
- **Not live until a full Pulsar restart.** `lib/*.js` is not hot-reloaded and the old file name no longer exists on disk, so the running server keeps its already-loaded copy until the restart. Verified on disk only (`node --check` on both lib files, `node test/run-all.js`); the live probe is still to do.
- **History.** Entries below this one still use the old name; they describe work done under it.
