## Engine failure messages tightened: comment-led diagnosis, no percentage without a located row, one-letter function names (2026-10-12; live-verified after a restart)

**What changed.** Three behaviour fixes and two tidy-ups, all on the failure side of the shared engine; none changes what is committed.
- **Comment mismatch no longer contradicts itself (`lib/fail-diagnostics.js`).** When COMMENT MISMATCH is the lead diagnosis (a comment issue exists, the text is not exact outside the scope and not found elsewhere ignoring whitespace), the generic `Similarity: N%` line and the `NOT FOUND ANYWHERE` block are replaced by "The code on the line(s) flagged under COMMENT MISMATCH matches the buffer; only the trailing comment differs." and "Fix the trailing comment(s) shown under COMMENT MISMATCH and retry; re-read the target area only if it still fails." (plus the `Hint used:` line when a hint was active). The claim is limited to the flagged lines on purpose, because the rest of `old_str` can still differ. FOUND OUTSIDE SCOPE and FOUND ELSEWHERE keep priority; with an AMBIGUOUS elsewhere match the comment-led wording is not used.
- **No percentage when there is no plausible row (PJ chose "option 1", precision edits).** The percentage is ONE Levenshtein call on ONE needle-sized slice. `closestArea` picks the slice by word overlap over the diagnosis window (the scoped region, or the whole file when unscoped); with no plausible row it used to fall back to the first rows of the window, so the number described unrelated text. Now, with no plausible row and no located diagnosis (whitespace, encoding, partial match), the line reads "No similar location found in the search window: no line shares a distinctive word with old_str. Verify scope hints and re-read the target area." and no percentage is printed. A plausible row (row 0 included) keeps `Closest area found` and `Similarity: N%`. `similarity` and `similarityTier` stay on the result object.
- **One-letter function names (`lib/tree-sitter-symbols.js`).** `getSymbolsFromText` dropped every name shorter than two characters, so `inFunction:'g'` on `function g()` failed with "No function/symbol-like patterns". The filter exists because the regex also matches bare `const a = 1` and `let i = 0`. A short name is now allowed for an explicit `function x` or `class X`, and for a `const`/`let`/`var` only when the line is visibly a function or arrow expression. C files keep the old rule.
- **Step 2c (dead code, no behaviour change).** The `salvagedComment` variable, the `autoStripComment` transform push and the `salvagedComment` result field are gone from `matchContent` in `lib/match-engine.js`; nothing outside the engine read them.
- **Step 2d (comments only).** The `matchContent` doc comment now describes the waterfall as it is: the rescue stays inside the scope, never strips a needle's extra trailing comment, and a partial, outside-scope or comment-only match returns noMatch and is explained by `diagnose()`.

**Tests added or updated.** `test/symbols_short_names_test.js` 16 checks (8 of 8 mutants killed); `test/comment_led_diagnosis_test.js` 29 (9 of 10; the survivor, dropping `!_exactOnlyOutside`, is equivalent because exact-only-outside implies the scan total is above zero); `test/no_row_similarity_test.js` 14 (8 of 8). Four older assertions that pinned `Similarity:` or "Low match" for no-row cases were updated: `W3c` in `elsewhere_diagnosis_test.js`, two in `s4_diagnostics_test.js` (the 249-character test now has a second token so a row is found and its point, a rounded 100% is neither exact nor drift, is kept) and `C9b`. All 32 `test/*_test.js` files exit 0 with no FAIL lines.

**Live-verified (one full restart, REPLACE dry runs on a scratch file).** The comment-led refusal (no `Similarity:`, no `NOT FOUND ANYWHERE`); `inFunction:'g'` matches at line 7; a needle with no distinctive word gets the "No similar location" line and no percentage; a typo on a real line still shows `Closest area found` and `Similarity: 53%`. The stats panel afterwards showed `replace` fails as `noMatch` and `comment` only, with no new keys.

**KNOWN, NOT FIXED / NOT VERIFIED.**
- A needle whose only distinctive token is the typo itself (one long identifier with one wrong character) shares no word with any line and now gets the "No similar location" line; the old output for that case was only right by accident of the first-row fallback.
- `NOT FOUND ANYWHERE` is still printed after the "No similar location" line. That is true there, since the full scan found nothing.
- `lib/elsewhere-diagnosis.js` still has its original name (PJ called it a dumb name; the rename is not done).
- README and the package version line (0.23.0) are not updated; whether this and the work below earn a bump is PJ's call.

## Engine stops committing guesses: a comment mismatch and a partial match are now failures with exact advice (2026-10-11; live-verified after restarts)

**Decision (PJ, 2026-10-11).** `str_replace` is retired and is not touched or ported; every feature worth keeping goes into the engine (`match-engine.js`, `fail-diagnostics.js`, `recover.js`, `edit-stats.js`) so the tools can become thin shims. The partial-match commit is dumped (a partial match becomes a failure with a diagnosis), and the trailing-comment auto-strip becomes a failure that names the lines to fix. Whitespace and unicode auto-rescue stay.

**What changed.**
- **Step 1: comment mismatch as a failure reason.** `lib/fail-diagnostics.js` builds on `lib/comment-diagnosis.js` (kinds extra, missing and different, on any needle line) and adds a COMMENT MISMATCH section with one "It should read: ..." line per issue; it suppresses the generic PARTIAL MATCH guess when comment issues exist. The engine maps it to the stat key `comment` (`fails.comment`); `lib/edit-stats.js` gained `comment: 0` in the seven `fails` blocks that carry `partialMatch` and in `SHARED_DIAG_KEYS`.
- **Step 2a: the engine no longer strips an extra trailing comment.** `profiledMatch` in `lib/recover.js` takes `opts.stripComment` (default true, so every other caller is identical) and `matchContent` passes `false`. The old rescue only ever fired for an extra comment on the needle's LAST line with enough text left over; those needles used to commit silently and now fail with the advice.
- **Step 2b: stage 2 of `matchContent` removed.** The partial-match rescue differed from stage 1 only for a target OUTSIDE a narrowed scope, and it committed there silently, including exact text that existed only outside the scope. Both cases are now noMatch.
- **Where-is-it advice (`lib/elsewhere-diagnosis.js` wired into `diagnose()`).** For a narrowed-scope edit whose text exists elsewhere ignoring whitespace, the caller now gets FOUND ELSEWHERE with the lines, the whitespace differences and the fix, instead of "NOT FOUND ANYWHERE" and a misleading low percentage. An ambiguous match says so. Stats keys are unchanged.
- **`replace` refusals counted.** The flag-clash and multi-line refusals of `fragment:true` now also bump `fails.noMatch` (`statKeys` in `lib/replace-fragment.js`), so the eight golden refusals read as eight fails, not four. Messages and the consecutive-failure rule are unchanged.
- **`inFunction` scope on functions with destructured parameters.** `braceEndRow` returned the signature row for `function f({ a, b }) {`, so `inFunction`, `afterFunction` and `beforeFunction` collapsed to one line for every such function (at least seven in `match-engine.js`); that is what let the old rescue commit out of scope. The new `bodyEndRow` in `lib/tree-sitter-symbols.js` finds the body brace at paren depth 0 and counts braces from there. `braceEndRow` is unchanged because the `registerTool` caller needs the old behaviour. `diagnoseFailure` now reads lines 1041 to 1095 live (it was 1041 to 1041).
- **Stats panel.** `lib/pulsar-edit-mcp-server.js` now draws `regex`, `fragmentCommits` and `fragmentFails` for `replace` (three rows after the `fuzzyContent` row).

**Tests added.** `fail_diagnostics_comment_test.js` 39 checks (12 of 12 mutants killed), `recover_optout_test.js` 18 (7 of 8; the survivor is equivalent), `match_content_comment_test.js` 13 (6 of 6), `match_content_scope_test.js` 22 (6 of 6), `elsewhere_diagnosis_test.js` 38 (24 of 27; the three survivors are equivalent by construction), `tree_sitter_symbols_test.js` 29 (16 of 16); the `replace_fragment_plan_test.js` pins were updated (114 checks, 17 of 17). `s4_diagnostics_test.js` (88) copies the new dependencies in its negative controls.

**Live-verified (after restarts).** Five `replace` flag-clash and multi-line dry runs gave the golden texts and `fails.noMatch:5`; `delete` dry runs gave the COMMENT MISMATCH section for missing, extra and different comments and `comment:3`; the `inFunction` read returned 1041 to 1095; on REPLACE dry runs the extra-comment case is refused with the advice (no `[autoStripComment]` commit), exact text outside the scope is refused with FOUND OUTSIDE SCOPE (it used to commit there), the whitespace-different case is refused with FOUND ELSEWHERE, and a plain indentation drift still dry-runs as a `fuzzyWhitespace` match.

**KNOWN, NOT FIXED / NOT VERIFIED.**
- `matchBlock` and `matchContent` still get one-line `inFunction` ranges, because their bodies contain braces in strings and comments; a string- and comment-aware counter is not written.
- The new stats panel rows were edited on 2026-10-11 and a live look at them is not recorded. A `hintsFailed` row is not shown, and `dryRuns` did not match the number of probes run.
- `str_replace` keeps its own inline partial-match and comment-strip code, untouched on purpose.
- Who wrote `lib/comment-diagnosis.js` and its test (timestamped 2026-10-11 10:00) was never confirmed.
- The fault-log `reason` names differ from the stats keys for the same failure (cause not investigated).

## Shared span pathway: Stages 4 and 5, `replace-all` and `sed` now run on pure planners (2026-10-10 to 2026-10-11; live-verified after restarts)

**What changed.** Both handlers keep their scope resolution, response text, dry-run previews, buffer writes and stats; only matching and planning moved into pure, unit-tested modules.
- **Stage 4, `replace-all`.** `replace-all` now runs on `lib/replace-all-plan.js` (Stage 4a built it, Stage 4b wired the handler). Live-verified 2026-10-10: dry runs (7 occurrences with locations, case-sensitive 6, `^foo` no matches, a `foo\nbar` regex with an empty location list, `(` giving the golden invalid-regex error) and four commits read back from disk (plain, named group, `$<length>`, a CRLF file keeping its CRs).
- **Stage 5, `sed`.** `lib/sed-plan.js` (`planSed`, `parseSed`) replaced the handler's inline parse-and-execute section; `lib/mcp-registration.js` went from 7670 to 7607 lines. The handler builds all failure text from the planner's reason: `badExpression`, `badAddress` (no counter bump, as before), `badSubstitution`, `badRange`, `rangeEndNotFound`, `emptyPattern` and `noMatch`. Live-verified 2026-10-11: 19 dry runs on the golden fixture and five commits read back from disk.
- **Deliberate behaviour changes** (all recorded in `test/golden_replace_all_sed.md`): `replace-all` refuses an empty query (it used to insert the replacement between every character) and counts it as a noMatch miss; `sed` refuses an empty substitution pattern, `s//X/` and `/a/s//X/` (it used to insert at the start of every row, and real sed reuses the last regex), with the text "Empty substitution pattern in sed expression: <expr> - nothing changed. An empty pattern is not supported (real sed would reuse the last regex)." Everything else is preserved on purpose: case defaults, the scope-wide `N` counter, reverse-ordered delete previews, only the first range deleted, and the per-line trimmed `replace-all` location list.

**Tests added.** `replace_all_plan_test.js` 452 checks (20 of 20 mutants killed); `sed_plan_test.js` 3968 checks (24 of 24), of which 3936 are differential cases against a verbatim copy of the old handler logic.

**KNOWN, NOT FIXED / NOT VERIFIED.**
- The `replace_all` and `sed` stats rows were never read after the live checks. That the empty-query and empty-pattern refusals count as noMatch misses rests on code reading, and the "bad address reported before empty pattern" order rests on a unit test, not a live probe.
- The `replace-across-files` confirm path, which shares the named-group fix, has never been run live.
- The `replace-all` and `sed` handlers are inline closures that need `atom`, so they are covered by live golden replays rather than node tests.

## Shared span pathway: Stages 2 and 3 added, and the named-group replacement bug fixed (2026-10-09 to 2026-10-10; the bug fix live-verified after a restart, Stages 2 and 3 are tests and a pure module only)

**What changed.** Three things, in the order they were done.
- **Stage 2 (tests only, no code change).** `replace-all` and `sed` keep their logic inline in `lib/mcp-registration.js`, which requires `atom`, so plain `node` cannot load their handlers. Only `applyReplacement` and `escapeRegex` in `lib/string-utils.js` are shared and pure. So `test/string_utils_replace_test.js` pins those two (29 checks, 13 of 13 mutants killed) and `test/golden_replace_all_sed.md` records the handlers' behaviour, captured LIVE on scratch files: the fixtures, every probed call with its exact output, and a 7-item list of behaviours to preserve or change on purpose in Stages 4 and 5. A driver harness with a fake `atom`, and extracting pure `sedPlan` / `replaceAllPlan` cores, were weighed and left for Stages 4 and 5.
- **Stage 3 (pure module, not wired).** New `lib/edit-match.js` exports `findHits`, `planEdit` and `expandHit`. Nothing requires it, so no tool behaves differently. It was written on 2026-10-09 as `regex-match.js`, then renamed and reworked Edit-style on 2026-10-10 (modelled on Claude Code's Edit tool): `oldString` matches exactly (literal, case-sensitive) unless `regex:true` or `caseSensitive:false`; it must match once or the plan refuses as `ambiguous`; `replaceAll:true` accepts every hit; `occurrence:N` picks the Nth hit (an extension, the live tools already have it). Refusals come back as `{ ok:false, reason }` with no message text, and an invalid regex is returned as `invalidRegex`, not thrown. A hit is a span with both rows because `replace-all`'s regex crosses lines; the scope is `'wholeText'` (replace-all) or `'perLine'` (sed). Hits come from `RegExp#exec`, so the slot bug below cannot happen in it. Test: `test/edit_match_test.js`, 60 checks passing, including the PARITY block that ties it to the golden file's live numbers.
- **The bug (fixed, live-verified).** `replace-all`, `sed` (both branches) and `replace-across-files` (confirm path) read the `String#replace` callback arguments by fixed position from the end. With a NAMED group the callback gets an extra groups object, so the offset slot held the whole string: `` $` `` came out empty and `$'` was the whole text (the whole file in `replace-all`, the whole line in `sed`). Found while fixing it: with NO named group the groups slot held the whole text, so `$<length>` gave the text length (a test file became `const [32] = 1;`) and `$<0>` its first character. A new pure helper, `unpackReplaceArgs` in `lib/string-utils.js`, detects the trailing groups object first. It replaces the six-line slot-reading block at exactly four sites in `lib/mcp-registration.js` (about L3731, L4743, L5047 and L5055; the import is at L13). A search of `lib/*.js` for the old slot reads now finds none.

**Behaviour change.** Only this, and only for regexes with capture groups: with a named group, `` $` `` and `$'` now give the text before and after the match; `$<name>` in a pattern with no named group now gives empty. Everything else is meant to be identical.

**Tests added.** `test/string_utils_replace_test.js` grew to 44 checks. U1-U10 feed REAL `String#replace` callbacks through the helper (no groups, numbered, a group that did not take part, named, numbered plus named, plain `arguments` objects, a null last argument); E1-E5 are end-to-end cases including the `$<length>` and `$<0>` regressions. Mutation-checked: 20 of 20 mutants killed (13 for the older helpers plus 7 for `unpackReplaceArgs`; throwaway harness in the Windows TEMP folder, the real files were never written). Every suite that prints a summary line passed after the edits.

**Live-verified (one full restart).** On scratch files: the `sed` dry run `s/(?<w>foo)/[$`|$<w>|$']/` previews `const [const |foo| = 2;] = 2;` (before: `const [|foo|const foo = 2;] = 2;`); a `replace-all` commit with a named group read back from disk exactly as expected (exact string comparison, LF endings); `replace-all` of `(foo)` with `[$<length>]` read back `const [] = 1;` (before: `const [32] = 1;`).

**Pinned in the golden file and deliberately NOT changed.** `replace-all` is case-insensitive by default and matches over the whole text with no `m` flag, so `^` anchors to the file start; `$&` is expanded even with `regex:false`; `$$` stays literal, `$0` is dropped and `$10` reads both digits (all unit tested); `sed`'s `N` flag counts across the whole scope, not per line; `sed` cannot take an escaped separator (`s/a\/b/c/` fails to parse); `replace-all` throws on an invalid regex while `sed` returns text; `replace-all`'s dry-run location list is per line and trimmed, so it can disagree with the match count.

**KNOWN, NOT FIXED / NOT VERIFIED.**
- The `replace-across-files` confirm path (one of the four fixed sites) has never been run live, before or after the fix. It makes the same helper call as `replace-all`.
- `sed` was verified through a dry run only, not a committed edit.
- The header of `lib/edit-match.js` still describes the named-group slot bug in the present tense (about L40-43); it is stale since this fix.
- The reworked matcher test (`edit_match_test.js`) was not mutation-checked after the rework; the first version of the matcher (30 of 30 killed) was.
- Stage 3 is not wired: no tool uses `lib/edit-match.js`. Stages 4 and 5 (moving `replace-all` and `sed` onto one pathway) have not started, and `applyEditAll`'s hit shape was not read. `replace` with `regex:true` on a fragment pattern still replaces the whole line with no warning (plan item, untouched).
- `wholeText` joins rows with `\n`. Rows from `buffer.getLines()` carry no CRs, while the live `replace-all` runs on text that keeps them, so a `\r\n` pattern behaves differently on CRLF files. A Stage 4 adapter concern.
- README not updated. The package version line still says 0.23.0; whether this earns a bump is PJ's call.
- Backups of the pre-fix `mcp-registration.js` and `string-utils.js` are in `session/backup-2026-10-10-named-group-fix/`.
## `replace` fragment branch extracted into a pure planner, `lib/replace-fragment.js` (Stage 1 of the shared span pathway plan; 2026-10-09, live-verified after three restarts)

**What changed.** New `lib/replace-fragment.js` (pure: no Pulsar objects, no I/O) exports `planFragmentReplace` and `describeApplyError`. The `replace` handler's fragment branch (`lib/mcp-registration.js`, about L2869-2923) no longer matches, builds refusal text or checks drift itself. It makes ONE `planFragmentReplace` call and then:
- on a refusal: increments the consecutive-failure counter when the plan's `consecBump` is true, bumps the plan's `statKeys` (apart from `fragmentFails`, which the unchanged `_fragRefuse` helper bumps together with `hintsFailed.*` and the fault-log write) and returns the plan's message;
- on success: reads the span and the tags (`fragment`, plus `guardedOut:N`) from the plan, then does the dry-run preview, the `applyEditSpan` write, the lint/style/struct/nudge suffixes and the success stat bumps exactly as before; a write error goes through `describeApplyError`.

The planner keeps the old order: flag clash (`regex`/`fuzzyWhitespace`/`fuzzyContent`), multi-line `old_str` (LF or CR), the matcher, then, on a commit only (`liveLines: buffer.getLines()` is passed; a dry run omits it), the drift pre-check. The handler's now-dead `matchFragment` import was removed.

**Behaviour is meant to be unchanged.** Same refusal texts, reasons and stat keys, same consecutive-failure rule:
- `fragmentUnsupportedFlag`, `fragmentMultiLine`, `fragmentDrift`: `fragmentFails` only, no consecutive-failure bump.
- `ambiguous`: `fails.anchorAmbiguous` + `fragmentFails`, no consecutive-failure bump.
- `noMatch`, `invalidInput` (empty needle), `onlyIdentifierGlued`, `occurrenceOutOfRange`: `fails.noMatch` + `fragmentFails`, consecutive-failure bump.
- write error (`applyEditSpan` returns an error): `fails.noMatch` + `fragmentFails`, not fault-logged, no bump.
- every refusal also bumps `hintsFailed.<hint>` for each hint the call passed (still done by `_fragRefuse`).

**Test added (plain `node`, no Pulsar).** `test/replace_fragment_plan_test.js`, 114 checks: pins the golden refusal messages (A-F captured live, G and H from source), the stat keys, the 10-line cap on the ambiguity list, the scope window, the guard default (only an explicit `false` turns it off), refusal ordering, `new_str` carried verbatim (`$&`, `$1`, tab, newline, empty), that the input lines are never mutated, and parity with `matchFragment`. Mutation-checked: 17 of 17 mutants killed (throwaway harness in the Windows TEMP folder; the real files were never written). Writing it exposed two test defects, both fixed: an assertion that threw instead of failing when a mutant turned a refusal into a success, and a wrong guess that `process.exit()` was truncating piped output (the real cause was the throw; the test now sets `process.exitCode`).

**Live-verified (three full restarts: flag clash + multi-line first, then all matcher refusals, then drift + success path + write error together).** After each restart, on a scratch file: the eight refusals (flag clash with each of the three flags, multi-line, ambiguous, every hit glued, occurrence out of range, not found) and the dry-run preview were byte-identical to the golden strings; the last eight fault-log records were those refusals in order; the stats read `fragmentFails` 8 with `fails` `anchorAmbiguous` 1 and `noMatch` 3 and nothing else; real commits (a plain edit, `occurrence:2` skipping a glued hit with the response tag `guardedOut:1`, a mid-identifier edit with `guardIdentifier:false`, and a fragment edit whose `new_str` spans two lines, response `line 2, +1 line`) were read back from disk and reverted, leaving the file byte-identical.

**KNOWN, NOT FIXED / NOT VERIFIED.**
- The consecutive-failure counter is not observable from the fragment path (`_fragRefuse` returns no `consecFailures` or suggestion text). The rule rests on `consecBump`, pinned by the planner test and read in the handler code; it was not checked live.
- `fragmentDrift` and the `applyEditSpan` error path cannot be triggered live; they are covered by the planner test and code read-back only. The plan takes `liveLines` in the same call as the lines it matches, so a drift refusal is probably unreachable in practice (as before).
- The `onAmbiguous` option sketched in the plan was NOT added: the planner only refuses. It belongs with Stage 3, when an `'all'` policy exists.
- `_fragHintBump` still duplicates the whole-line `hintsSucceeded`/`hintsFailed` blocks (plan item 1(c) not done).
- Flag-clash and multi-line refusals still bump no `fails.*` key, so eight refusals read as four fails in the headline.
- `replace` counts `fuzzyWhitespace` but not `regex` or `fuzzyContent` in `hintsUsed` (pre-existing; those bumps sit above the fragment branch).
- `applyEditAll` and `commitFragmentEdit` still have no callers (pre-existing lint warnings).
- README not updated. The package version line still says 0.23.0; whether this and the fragment work earn a bump is PJ's call.
## `replace` gains opt-in part-line mode (`fragment:true`); two unit test files added and mutation-checked (2026-10-08, live-verified after restarts)

**Decision (PJ, 2026-10-08).** The 2026-09-27 ruling in `## B32 closed` (replace stays whole-line permanently, part-line matching rejected) is SUPERSEDED. PJ asked for fragment matching in the shared engine for `replace` ("one of the few things not ported"). It is opt-in: without `fragment:true` `replace` is unchanged and whole-line. `str_replace` is untouched. The older entries below still describe `replace` as whole-line only; they are history, not edited.

**What was built (`lib/mcp-registration.js`, fragment branch ~L2850-2924, unless noted).**
- `fragment:true` and `guardIdentifier` (default true) in the `replace` schema, handler and description. The branch sits before the whole-line `matchContentEngine` call. It runs `matchFragment` (`lib/fragment-match.js`) over the resolved scope rows and writes only the matched span with `applyEditSpan` (`lib/match-engine.js`). `applyEditSpan` relocates to the first other occurrence if the span drifted, so the branch pre-checks the live span and refuses on drift instead.
- Refusals, all "Nothing was written", fault-logged and counted: fragment combined with `regex`/`fuzzyWhitespace`/`fuzzyContent`; a multi-line `old_str`; ambiguous (the message lists the first 10 lines, then "... and N more"); every hit glued to a longer identifier (`count` inside `discount`, escape hatch `guardIdentifier:false`); `occurrence` out of range (refused, never clamped); drift; an `applyEditSpan` error.
- Stats (`lib/edit-stats.js`): `fragmentCommits` and `fragmentFails` added to the `replace` counter block and to its TOOL_REGISTRY extras. Successful commits also bump `hits` and `hintsSucceeded.*`; refusals bump `fragmentFails` and `hintsFailed.*`. Ambiguous refusals bump `fails.anchorAmbiguous` (`replace`'s own schema name: the first version bumped `fails.ambiguous`, which `bump()` silently dropped).
- Parity with `str_replace`: struct-balance suffix and `successNudge` on BOTH the fragment and whole-line paths of `replace` (helper `_replNudge`), plus `features.styleCheck`.

**Live-verified (after full restarts).** Dry runs and commits on `package.json` (an em dash on the line survived, edit reverted); ambiguous, `occurrence:2`, out-of-range, regex clash, multi-line and identifier guard on and off; the struct warning on both paths; `successNudge` on both paths; on a 320-hit scratch file the ambiguous refusal listed 10 lines then "... and 310 more" and the stats read `fails.anchorAmbiguous` 1, `fragmentFails` 1.

**Tests added (both run with plain `node`, no Pulsar).**
- `test/replace_fragment_test.js`, 47 checks: drives `matchFragment` and `applyEditSpan` the way the fragment branch does (match, live-span drift pre-check, span edit) on a fake buffer. Covers an em dash line, an astral character (UTF-16 columns), a trailing CR, a BOM, a scope window across rows, ambiguity with 1-based rows, `occurrence` 0/-1/1.5/out of range, two hits on a line, literal `new_str` (`$&`, `$1`, backslash, tab), empty `new_str`, the identifier guard, overlapping hits, the drift guard and a split/join negative control. Mutation check: 6 of 6 injected breaks caught (drift pre-check dropped, occurrence clamped, replace-all, scope window ignored, guard always off, span end off by one). The span-end break is caught only by one check, because `applyEditSpan`'s own drift fallback hides most wrong columns.
- `test/replace_wholeline_crlf_bom_test.js`, 22 checks: pins how the whole-line engine (`matchContent`, `applyEdit`) treats a BOM, a trailing CR and CRLF, as probed on 2026-10-08. Strict matching treats them as real characters (mismatch is a `noMatch`); the default auto-rescue matches across them but is always flagged with a transform; `fuzzyContent` handles the BOM case; `applyEdit` writes `new_str` verbatim, so a CR or BOM on the replaced row survives only if `new_str` carries it; two identical CR rows are ambiguous without `occurrence`. Mutation check against a COPY of `lib/`: 8 of 8 injected engine breaks caught. This closes the open "CRLF/BOM regression test" item for the whole-line path.
- Generic matcher and `applyEditSpan` cases were already in `test/s4b_fragment_match_test.js`.

**KNOWN, NOT FIXED / NOT VERIFIED.**
- Neither new test calls the `replace` handler (it needs Pulsar). Untested: refusal message text, `bump()` keys, fault logging, the struct and nudge suffixes, the dry-run preview, a `new_str` containing a newline. Covering them means extracting the fragment branch into a pure module (like `lib/str-replace-fragment.js`), a refactor not started.
- The fake buffer in the CRLF/BOM test does not normalise line endings the way Pulsar's `setTextInRange` does, so what Pulsar writes for a `new_str` containing CRLF is untested, and the test does not show whether Pulsar's buffer ever holds a BOM or CRs.
- The flag-clash and multi-line refusals bump `fragmentFails` but no `fails.*` key.
- `_fragHintBump` duplicates the whole-line `hintsSucceeded`/`hintsFailed` blocks.
- Fragment mode is a single-line plain literal: no regex, no fuzzy, no auto-rescue. The older footgun is unchanged: `replace` with `regex:true` on a fragment pattern matches, then replaces the WHOLE line, with no warning.
- Whole-line `replace` drops a CR or BOM on the replaced row unless `new_str` includes it (pinned by the new test); whether that deserves a warning is undecided.
- Still different from `str_replace`: `retryOnFail` (blocked on the `_sameFile` relative-path fix), the auto-rescue cascade (not fully audited), the `occurrence` default.
- A failing `replace` dry run counts as a fault but is not tallied in the `dryRuns` counter.
- README and the plan were not updated for this work.

## B84 closed: edit tools wait for their own lint pass, files with no open tab are linted, and the fix needs a patched linter-bundle (2026-10-04 to 2026-10-06, live-verified)

**Symptom.** The lint suffix on an edit response showed the PREVIOUS edit's state (a fix still reported the error it had just removed), and a file edited with no open tab got no lint at all.

**Root causes.** (1) The change-driven lint pass is debounced and not awaitable, and linter-bundle's MessageRegistry applies results on a further 100ms trailing debounce; `GetLinterMessages` reads `registryMessages.messages`, which is only assigned when that debounce fires. (2) The forced-lint step in `lint-helpers.js` never ran at all: linter-bundle keeps `instance` module-private, so `mainModule.registryLinters` and `registryEditors` were always undefined and the old gate never opened (a debug log showed `hasEditorLinter:false, hasLint:false`). (3) With no open tab `ctx.editor` is null (write tools work on a bare TextBuffer from `bufferForPath()`), and `lintSnapshot` returned early on `!editor`.

**Fix in this repo.** `lib/lint-helpers.js`: `lintSnapshot` awaits linter-bundle's exported `lintEditor(editor)` inside a 3000ms `Promise.race`. On timeout it returns "lint (pending): still running after 3s, results not yet current - call get-diagnostics on this file shortly." instead of printing a result that may belong to an earlier edit. For a null editor it uses the current call's buffer: `lib/tool-framework.js` runs each handler inside `runWithLintTarget({ buffer, filePath }, ...)` (Node AsyncLocalStorage, so parallel calls on different files cannot see each other's buffer), and `lintSnapshot` calls linter-bundle's `lintBuffer(buffer)`, or, if that export is missing, lints through its own hidden `TextEditor` cached per buffer in a WeakMap. The hidden editor is never destroyed by us (destroying the last holder of a project buffer has crashed the renderer: B68/B69/B70). None of the ~19 `maybeLintSuffix` call sites changed.

**Fix in linter-bundle (separate repo; MERGED upstream as PR #4 and PR #5 on asiloisad-pulsar/linter-bundle).** PR #4 gives the four provider bookkeeping keys a package-specific prefix (the clash fix). PR #5 adds the `lintEditor` and `lintBuffer` exports and the debounce flush in `GetLinterMessages`. The maintainer reworked `lintBuffer` before merging (commit 2f2b43a): instead of our cached hidden editor on the caller's buffer, it lints a temporary editor holding a snapshot of the buffer's text, path and grammar, waits for Tree-sitter to initialise, and destroys that editor after every pass; the caller's buffer is never retained or destroyed, and results stay attributed to the original buffer (`registryLinters.lint` gained a `buffer` argument). It also added a README section and `spec/main-spec.js`. Upstream's `package.json` still says 2.6.1 (no release yet), so a normal Pulsar package update does NOT bring these in: the installed `~/.pulsar/packages/linter-bundle` was replaced by hand with merged upstream (lib/, spec/ and README identical to commit 1a1c70b; backups of the previous patched copy are in `session/linter-bundle-installed-backup-2026-10-07/`). An update from an older release would silently remove the fix, and lint then falls back to the old stale or missing behaviour.

**linter-bundle 2.6.1 breaks next to the original `linter` package.** Both store bookkeeping on the provider object under the same `__$sb_linter_*` keys. 2.6.1 keeps a per-buffer WeakMap in `request_last_received`; the original `linter` writes a number there, so `linter[$requestLastReceived].get is not a function` throws before `did-update-messages` and linter-bundle's registry stays empty (`get-diagnostics` returns 0 while another UI shows the error). Keep `linter` and `linter-ui-default` disabled; a restart is needed after changing that, because the clobbered value stays on the provider object until linter-bundle consumes it again. The key rename in `fix-shared-registry-keys` makes it work even with both enabled.

**Live-verified (after full restarts).** Break and fix edits report their own lint state in the same response, with and without an open tab, including a 6,003-line file (the cold first edit finished inside the 3s timeout). `get-diagnostics` with a `filePath` returns a tab-less file's messages. With `linter` and `linter-ui-default` enabled again, the registry receives ESLint results and the dev console stays free of the TypeError.

**KNOWN, NOT FIXED / NOT VERIFIED.**
- Edits fired genuinely in parallel were never tested (the client ran the calls one at a time); "newest pass wins" rests on the registry's `requestLastReceived` check.
- The timeout message path fired live exactly once (2026-10-07, the first lint of a freshly created tab-less file took more than 3s and the response carried "lint (pending)..."); it has not been tested deliberately. No latency figure exists.
- Upstream's merged `lintBuffer` creates and destroys one temporary editor (and Tree-sitter parser) per lint of a tab-less file and parses the whole file each time; its per-edit cost on large files, and the stability of that create/destroy cycle on this Pulsar build, have not been measured (burst test pending). Our own fallback (used only when `lintBuffer` is missing) keeps one hidden editor per buffer alive for the session.
- `get-diagnostics` with `scope:'project'` lists only open files' messages, by design.
- `ctx.commit()`'s own lint gate (`features.lint && editor` in `tool-framework.js`) still skips tab-less files; only handlers that call `maybeLintSuffix` directly are covered.
- Separate finding, not investigated: `str_replace` fails with "commit-time verification failed" on CRLF files (reproduced on a 160-line all-CRLF file; the same edit on an LF copy succeeds). The message comes from `mcp-registration.js` around L1179.
- Upstream now ships `spec/main-spec.js` covering `lintEditor` and `lintBuffer`; there is no test for `runWithLintTarget` or for this repo's own hidden-editor fallback.

## B32 closed: `str_replace` will NOT be ported onto the engine, `replace` is grown instead; engine work from the 2026-09-25 research pass recorded (2026-09-25 to 2026-09-27, plan closed 2026-10-01)

**Status of B32.** Stages S1, S2/S2b/S2c/S2d, S3 (helper), S4-1, S4a, S4b, S4c, S4d and S4e Sections 1-3 (plus the `allSymbols` follow-up fix) are all done and live-verified; each has its own entry further down this file. Nothing in B32 is still in progress.

**Decision (PJ, 2026-09-27): do NOT port `str_replace` onto the engine.** Instead the new `replace` tool is grown until it has `str_replace`'s features. This answers open decision A (a parallel tool wins over growing in place) and CANCELS the rest of S4e: non-plain (multi-line, regex, fuzzy) matching and `str_replace`'s own failure builder stay hand-rolled inside `str_replace`, which remains the reference and the control. The commit half of S4e Section 3 (`commitFragmentEdit`/`applyEditSpan`) stays unwired by design (decided 2026-09-25: the existing block already does the B39/B40 drift re-check and write-verify, and a second commit path is needed for fuzzy/regex/multi-line either way).

**Decision (PJ, 2026-09-27): `replace` stays whole-line only, permanently.** No fragment or mid-line matching in any new tool (this restates the S3 ruling); sub-line editing stays inside `str_replace`, guarded by `guardIdentifier`. Also decided 2026-09-27: `delete` needs no `fuzzyWhitespace` reindent fix, because it never writes a caller-supplied replacement.

**Engine steps done 2026-09-25** (smoke-tested): `sed`'s `inFunction` now resolves through `resolveScope()` (it used to call `findFunctionInBuffer` directly and silently take the first match on duplicate names; `occurrence` added to its schema, `buildFailResponse` wired in). `matchContent()` gained an `allOccurrences:true` mode (regex and literal) that returns `{matched, totalMatches, hits[]}` instead of refusing an ambiguous match. `applyEditAll(buffer, hits, newStrFor)` is the batch-write sibling of `applyEdit`. Gotcha kept: `newStrFor(hit, index)` must receive the hit's ORIGINAL occurrence index (ascending row order), not its position in the highest-row-first write order, or replacement text is silently inverted on multi-hit edits. Investigated and NOT needed: `replace-all` and `replace-across-files` already work through a different valid pattern (`String.replace` plus the shared `applyReplacement()` and `buffer.setTextViaDiff()`), and `ghidraFindFn` needs no ambiguity detection because Ghidra's `FUN_xxxxxxxx` names are unique by construction.

**`replace` tool live tests and fixes (2026-09-25).** Two bugs found and fixed, both live-verified after a restart: a duplicate `[regex]` tag in the dry-run header (the handler re-pushed a tag the engine had already set), and genuine ambiguity refusals counted as failures, which tripped the "N consecutive failures, consider switching tools" nudge exactly when the tool was refusing to guess. The counter now skips ambiguity refusals, and `fails.anchorAmbiguous` is split from `fails.anchorNotFound`. The lint/style suffix behaves like `str_replace` (the scratch file produced no lint output, so end-to-end rendering against a file with real lint output is still unverified), and the partial-match and similarity diagnostics were checked and are sensible.

**Research pass: what to take from Cline, Aider and opencode (2026-09-25).** Done and verified 2026-09-26/27: (1) `diffVsBuffer` is now populated on match failures and the "Closest area found" block has a plausibility floor. The false "2% similarity" readings had one root cause: `fuzzyRow`, a word-overlap scorer, locks onto the WRONG row on files with repetitive vocabulary or very long lines, and every downstream field inherited it; `calculateSimilarity` itself was correct. (2) Disproportionate-match guard in `match-engine.js` (`profiled.disproportionate`): a fuzzy rescue whose rebuilt span is far larger than the needle is refused as ambiguous. Only `profiledMatch`'s needsEncoding/_norm rebuild branch needed it (the other two rebuild paths are line-bounded by construction); the threshold was checked on both sides of the boundary (508 characters for an 8-character needle). (3) The `fuzzyWhitespace` indentation audit found real mangled indentation, fixed by `reindentForFuzzyWhitespace()` (entry below). REJECTED, do not reopen without new evidence: block-anchor similarity matching (known-harmful upstream and against refuse-don't-guess), escape-normalised matching (3 of 200 sampled failures), Aider's `...` elision (a chat-format feature), Aider's fuzzy edit-distance fallback (dead code upstream), Aider's "did you mean" (weaker than `lib/fail-diagnostics.js`).

**Housekeeping.** `README.md` mojibake fixed 2026-09-27 (89 stray U+009D characters, mangled arrows, ellipses, plus-minus signs and emoji). The verbatim B32 stage text, the research section and the old session notes were archived before the 2026-10-01 cleanup as `session/refactor-plan-2026-10-01-pre-cleanup.md`, `session/CHANGELOG-2026-10-01-pre-cleanup.md` and `session/notes-archive-2026-10-01-pre-cleanup.ndjson`.

## Stats: silent-counting defects fixed, every bumping tool now in the schema, stats panel completed (2026-09-30, stats live-verified)

**Silent-counting fixes.** `bump()` only increments a key that already exists as a number in the schema, so a bump to a missing key does nothing and says nothing (this had already bitten S4-0, `replace`'s `anchorAmbiguous` and `banner`/`ifdefBlock`). Four more instances found and fixed, each verified on a fresh process:
- `insert` `fails.onLineContentMismatch` did not exist in `edit-stats.js`, so the onLine+expectedContent mismatch was never counted. Key added; counts.
- `delete` Mode 4: an ambiguous `startContent` was counted as `startNotFound`. Now `anchorAmbiguous` (`occurrenceOutOfRange` too). The end-anchor bump got the same mapping but is probably unreachable, see the fault-log entry below.
- `insert` positional hints: `afterLine`, `beforeLine`, `onLine` (and `expectedContent` for onLine+expectedContent) are now counted in `hintsUsed` at attempt time (`mcp-registration.js`, ~L1649). `startOfFile` was added to `FULL_HINT_KEYS`; its bump had no key, so `startOfFile` inserts had never been counted. Verified: each of the five reads 1 after one dry run apiece.
- Dry runs are now counted centrally in `tool-framework.js`: a handler that returns `dryRun:true` without going through `ctx.dryRunReturn` is counted once (`ctx._dryRunCounted` stops a double count). Restart test #1 found three defects, all fixed: `str_replace` had no `dryRuns` key in its schema, and `replace` and `replace_function_body` had direct bumps that were counted twice. Side effect: `replace_block`, `sed` and `apply_patch` (whose `dryRun` defaults to true, so every preview counts) now count dry runs too; earlier history is undercounted and is not backfilled. Dry runs deliberately do not count toward hits or edit ops. PJ reports the dry-run stats fixed, and lifetime `str_replace.dryRuns` now reads non-zero after the restart; the full seven-tool restart checklist was not recorded.

**Audit.** `test/_audit_bumps_0930.js` (read-only) checks every literal `bump(tool, path)` against the live schema, and `test/_audit_ctxfail_0930.js` does the same for `ctx.fail(reason)`. It found 19 tools calling `bump(<tool>, 'hits')` with no schema entry at all, so their counts were silently dropped, plus `get_linter_messages` (2 sites). Limit learned the hard way: the first run missed `str_replace`'s absent `dryRuns` key because it only checked literal call sites; dynamic `statsKey` values and `TOOL_REGISTRY` extras need checking too.

**Schema additions** (`edit-stats.js`: the `editStats` initialiser plus `TOOL_REGISTRY`, all `countAs: 'none'`, hits only). Search group: `get_linter_messages`, `get_document`, `read_file`, `get_file_summary`, `get_includes_and_defines`, `list_project_functions`, `file_line_count`, `get_line_count`. Nav group: `get_filename`, `get_full_path`, `get_project_files`, `open_file`, `goto_line`, `list_open_files`, `get_active_editor_info`, `undo`, `redo`, `save_file`, `save_all`. Kernel group: `check_struct`. The `check-struct` handler's `bump('check-struct', ...)` is now `bump('check_struct', ...)`; the hyphenated key could never have matched. Audit after the change: 242 literal bump sites resolve (was 223) and definite silent no-ops fell from 20 to 1, the one left being a false positive inside a comment near L2129. Counts start from the restart; nothing is backfilled.

**Stats panel** (`lib/pulsar-edit-mcp-server.js`, `GROUP_DEFS`). The panel draws only the keys listed for each group and silently skips everything else. The Edit group still listed the pre-rename names `delete_line_range` and `delete_block` and never listed `replace` or `delete`, so neither tool appeared. Fixed. New "Kernel tools" group (`_kernelGroup`) with `namingcheck`, `check_function_docs`, `insert_function_doc`, `checkpatch` and `check_struct`. The Search list gained the 7 read tools and the Nav / file list the 11 file tools. Verified: after a restart `get-edit-stats` shows all 20 new keys in both session and lifetime, and one live `read-file` plus one `list-project-functions` call moved `read_file` and `list_project_functions` to 1 hit each (session and lifetime). A screenshot of the panel confirms `replace` and `delete` under Edit tools. The new Search, Nav and Kernel rows were not yet confirmed by eye.

**KNOWN, NOT FIXED:**
- The Shell group looks for `_cmdGroup` but the stats key is `_shellGroup`, and the Nav group has no key although `_navGroup` exists, so both group badges never render (one-word fixes).
- A tool that never calls `bump()` is still invisible in the stats and the panel; this was not audited tool by tool. Central counting in `tool-framework.js` would cover them if they go through the framework.
- `ctx.fail` reasons with no `fails` key: `find-text` `invalidRegex`, `search-symbol` `noRoot`, and `goto-line` (which now has a schema entry but no `fails` block).
- `insert` still does not count `fuzzyWhitespace`, `fuzzyContent`, `regex`, `ifdefEdge` or `hintRadius` in `hintsUsed`, and has no `hintsSucceeded` counting on its positional branch.
- No catch-all "Other" group in the panel, so the next added or renamed tool can vanish the same way.
- Scratch files still to delete by hand: `test/_dryrun_scratch.txt` and `test/_verify_0930_scratch.js`.

## Fault log: `replace`, `insert` and `delete` failures now record every active hint, the closest buffer area, a diff and the proposed text (2026-09-28 to 2026-09-30, live-verified)

**Requirement (PJ, 2026-09-28):** every hint used is logged, and every match failure saves the diff against the buffer, the buffer text ("before") and the proposed text. Before this, `replace` wrote no fault-log entries at all, `insert` and `delete` logged only the one hint that failed with `diffVsBuffer` always null, and no tool recorded the proposed `new_str`.

**New helper `logMatchFailure()`** in `lib/mcp-registration.js`, next to `logHintFailure`. Takes `{tool, filePath, reason, needle, proposedNewStr, allLines, hints}` and records: `hintsSet` (every active hint, not just the failing one), a closest-area row (same word-overlap and plausibility floor as `str_replace`'s own no-match branch), a `diffLines()` diff (existing `diff` package, no new dependency), a +/-5-line `bufferPreview`, and `proposedNewStr` (the first tool anywhere to log it).
- **`replace`:** wired into both failure branches (scope error, match failed). `hintsSucceeded` is now written on commit and `hintsFailed` on both failure branches; the buckets existed but nothing ever wrote to them. Side fix: the match-failure branch had no `bump()` at all, so `fails.noMatch` and `fails.ambiguous` were never counted there. Verified 2026-09-28 (scope failures and a content-not-found): full `hintsSet`, populated `proposedNewStr`/`diffVsBuffer`/`bufferPreview`, and `hintSuccessRate` matching the real attempts (`ifdefBlock` ok 1; `inFunction`, `afterString`, `banner`, `occurrence` fail 1).
- **`insert` (4 sites) and `delete` (6 sites):** all 7 `logHintFailure` calls replaced. Three `delete` sites had no fault-log entry before and now do: Mode 1/2 `banner`/`ifdefBlock` scope error, Mode 4 `startContent` not found, Mode 4 `endContent` not found. `delete`'s `proposedNewStr` is always null (destructive, there is no replacement text). `insert`'s onLine+expectedContent branch now diffs against the known target line rather than a fuzzy search. The two purely numeric `outOfRange` checks stay bump-only (no needle to diff against). `logHintFailure()` is no longer called from `insert` or `delete`, but it is NOT unused: `str_replace`'s scope resolver (`lib/_s4e_section2_scope.js`, four call sites) still calls it, so `str_replace` hint failures still log in the older thinner shape (`hintFault:<hint>:<variant>`, one hint, no proposed text). Verified live 2026-09-29 by triggering five failures (insert afterString bad, insert onLine+expectedContent mismatch, delete banner ambiguous, delete startContent/endContent missing, delete matchString missing); the `hintsUsed` counters incremented exactly as expected, so nothing regressed.
- **`delete` Mode 4 ambiguous branches** (start and end early returns) now log reasons `startAmbiguous` and `endAmbiguous`. `startAmbiguous` verified on a fresh process 2026-09-30 (full `hintsSet`, `bufferPreview`, `diffVsBuffer`, `proposedNewStr` null; the caller-facing text is unchanged). The first attempt showed no row because the server process pre-dated the edit, not because the code was wrong. Lesson: after a restart, confirm the process is fresh with `get-debug-log` (near-empty) and session counters at zero before testing.
- **Unwired plumbing:** `bumpHint()` (creates missing keys on demand) and an expanded `FULL_HINT_KEYS`/`ALL_HINT_NAMES` are in `edit-stats.js` and threaded into `tool-framework.js`'s deps, but nothing calls them. The planned framework-level auto-counting (wrap `ctx.commit`/`ctx.fail`) does not work because `str_replace`, `insert` and `delete` mostly return raw result objects; manual `bump()` calls remain the only hint counting.

**KNOWN, NOT FIXED:**
- `replace_block` and `replace_function_body` still use `buildFailResponse`/`logFailure` with the thinner data.
- `delete` Mode 4's `occurrenceOutOfRange` branch delegates to `buildFailResponse`; whether that path fault-logs has not been audited.
- The end-anchor `anchorAmbiguous` bump and the `endAmbiguous` log are probably dead code for plain matches, because Mode 4's end match is pinned to `occurrence:1` (S2c). Untested whether `regex` or fuzzy matching can reach them. Decide: keep as harmless, or remove.
- 52 malformed entries in `session/session-faults.ndjson` (no `tool` field; a string spread into an object in a `find_text` `logFailure` call) were found and left unfixed.

## Structural-anchor parameters renamed (HARD rename, no aliases); `replace` gains them (2026-09-28)

**Renamed everywhere, old names no longer accepted:** `sectionHint` -> `banner`, `preprocBlock` -> `ifdefBlock`, `preprocSide` -> `ifdefEdge`. The old names described neither what is matched (a `/* === NAME === */` banner comment, an `#ifdef..#endif` block) nor what the third one selects (the `'open'` or `'close'` line of that block). Any caller, saved prompt or note that still passes the old names will have them silently ignored by the schema, so grep for them before assuming a tool call is scoped. Applied across `lib/` (7 files: schemas, handlers, `resolveScope` params, stats keys, descriptions, failure messages), the two tests that name them, `README.md` and `LLM-FAILURE-MODES.md`. Earlier entries in this file and in `mcp-server-refactor-plan.md` keep the old names deliberately: they record what was true when written. The frozen `test/_tmp_buggy/` copy was restored from backup and NOT renamed.

**Lifetime stats are migrated, not reset.** `edit-stats.js` now folds counts stored under the old key names into the new ones when the stats file is loaded (`_migrateRenamedHintKeys`, idempotent, safe to delete after a few releases). Checked against the real `session/session-stats.json` in memory before shipping: e.g. `insert.sectionHint` 30 -> `insert.banner` 30, `delete.preprocBlock` 28 -> `delete.ifdefBlock` 28, nothing lost. Note: importing `edit-stats.js` in a throwaway script is NOT safe -- its process-exit hook overwrites the stats file with empty in-memory data.

**`replace` now takes `banner`, `ifdefBlock`, `ifdefEdge`** (parity with the other tools). Also added `anchorAmbiguous` to `replace`'s `fails` and `banner`/`ifdefBlock` to `FULL_HINT_KEYS`, without which their counters silently did nothing. Ambiguous structural anchors are now recognised via `scope.error.ambiguous === true` (they report `kind:'anchorError'`, not `kind:'ambiguous'`), so they no longer raise the consecutive-failure counter or get logged as not-found.

**KNOWN, NOT FIXED:** `banner` resolves to the banner comment only, not the code beneath it, so it cannot scope a `replace` search to a section's contents (`ifdefBlock` is unaffected and works). Awaiting a decision: widen `banner` to run from the banner's end to the next banner/EOF for `replace`, or drop it from `replace`.

## `fuzzyWhitespace` reindent-on-commit fix for `replace`; scope audited and found NOT engine-wide (2026-09-27, live-verified)

Context: an earlier audit (2026-09-26) found that `fuzzyWhitespace` tolerated indentation
differences only when *matching* -- on commit, the replacement text was spliced in with
whatever indentation the caller's `new_str` happened to have, not the buffer's real
indentation. Fixed the same day in `lib/match-engine.js` (`reindentForFuzzyWhitespace()`,
wired into `applyEdit`/`applyEditAll`, and into `replace`'s call site passing `old_str` as
the needle) but not yet restarted or live-tested at the time that entry was written.

Live-verified this session: confirmed a restart had picked up the change, then ran a real
`replace` call against a fresh scratch file with a genuine indentation mismatch (buffer at
8-space indent, `old_str`/`new_str` both written at 2-space indent, `fuzzyWhitespace:true`).
Read the committed result back from disk directly (not the buffer, not a cached tool
result) and confirmed the write landed at 8-space indent -- the buffer's real indentation,
not the caller's. Dry-run preview correctly still shows the diff at the caller's original
indentation (expected -- `buildReplacePreview` does not reindent, only the commit path does).

SCOPE CORRECTION (found by grepping every `applyEdit`/`applyEditAll`/`applyEditSpan`/
`commitFragmentEdit` call site in `mcp-registration.js`, not assumed from the design intent):
this fix is **not** shared across all engine-backed tools. Breakdown:
- `replace` -- opted in, passes `old_str` as the needle. This is the only tool that gets
  the reindent behavior today.
- `replace_block` and `replace_function_body` -- both call `applyEdit`, but without a
  needle, so the reindent step no-ops for them by design (whole-block/whole-function
  replacement doesn't have a single "line indentation" the way a line-level replace does).
- `applyEditAll` -- has the reindent logic wired in, gated on the caller supplying
  `opts.needle` and the hit carrying `fuzzyWhitespace` in its transforms; not confirmed
  whether any current caller actually supplies that.
- `applyEditSpan` (the row/column committer used by Section 3's fragment-match path) --
  does not call `reindentForFuzzyWhitespace` at all. Nothing committed through that path
  gets reindented.
- `str_replace` -- its own hand-rolled commit path was deliberately left untouched (per PJ,
  `str_replace` is being retired in favor of `replace`), so it still has the original bug:
  `fuzzyWhitespace` tolerates the match but writes `new_str` verbatim, unreindented.
- `sed` -- does not go through `applyEdit`/`applyEditAll` for its substitutions at all, so
  this fix does not apply to it.

NOT YET DONE: no standing regression test file for this fix (only the one live case above
was exercised end-to-end); `delete`'s Mode 4 (`startContent`/`endContent`) and any other
`fuzzyWhitespace`-capable tool have not been audited for whether they should also opt in;
the negative/edge cases from the original unit tests (no-op when `transforms` lacks
`fuzzyWhitespace`, no-op when `needle` is omitted, negative-delta clamp-at-0, blank-line
handling) were verified as pure function calls but not re-exercised through a live buffer.


## S4e closed match-only; stats double-count fixed and live-verified; `_s4e_section3_fragment.js` header, README and this file brought up to date (2026-09-24)

**Context.** Final S4e pass. Two separate things were settled: the last open S4e decision, and a stats bug that had made every lifetime counter read exactly 2x. Nothing in this entry changes how `str_replace` matches or commits.

**S4e is CLOSED as match-only (PJ's decision, 2026-09-24).** Section 3's commit half is deliberately not wired. `commitFragmentEdit()` is exported from `lib/_s4e_section3_fragment.js` and imported at `lib/mcp-registration.js` L28 but never called (verified by grep 2026-09-24: 1 occurrence, the import); `applyEditSpan()` has 0 occurrences in `mcp-registration.js` and no live tool calls it. `str_replace` keeps its own character-index splice (`buffer.setTextInRange`), which already performs the B39 drift-relocate and B40 byte-for-byte verify, so swapping it would replace proven code with equivalent never-run code, and fuzzy/regex/multi-line matches would still need the old block. If it is ever wired: dry run, full restart, live test, standing suite; every earlier S4e wiring step shipped a bug the standing suites could not see (the missing wiring, the `const` reassignment crash, the `allSymbols` ReferenceError). All three S4e sections (1 dry-run preview, 2 scope resolution, 3 plain single-line match) are wired and live-verified. `str_replace` is still a hybrid, not fully engine-backed: multi-line, regex and fuzzy matching, its failure builder and its commit remain its own code.

**Section 3's two behaviour changes are now documented and probed.** Both refuse instead of silently committing, both covered by `lib/test_s4e_section3.js` (10/10). (1) Identifier guard: `old_str:"count"` no longer matches inside `discount`; `guardIdentifier:false` is the escape hatch. Probed 2026-09-24 with a MIXED case (`let discount = 5;` plus `let count = 1;`): the glued hit is excluded (`guardedOut:1`, `totalMatches:1`) and the whole-word hit matches on its own; with `guardIdentifier:false` both count and the call is ambiguous. (2) Same-line double hit: a needle twice on one line is now 2 occurrences (probe: `totalMatches:2`, `matchRows:[1,1]`), so an unscoped call is refused as ambiguous and `occurrence:1`/`occurrence:2` pick the first/second hit. Previously the second hit was invisible and the edit silently went to the first. Known cosmetic wart, NOT fixed: the ambiguity message lists the same row twice ("Found at line(s): 1, 1").

**Stats double-count FIXED and live-verified.** Symptom: every lifetime counter was exactly 2x its true value (ratios stayed valid, absolutes did not). Root cause, confirmed by reproduction: `bump()` writes both `editStats` and `lifetimeStats`, and `syncToLifetime()` then added `(session - _lastSynced)` to lifetime a second time. That function runs on a 5-second timer and on every `get-edit-stats` read, reset, unload and process exit, which is why the factor was exactly 2 and not something messier. Fix (`lib/edit-stats.js`): `syncToLifetime()` is now an exported no-op with an explanatory comment; `bump()`/`bumpStyle()` are the only lifetime writers. `resetLastSynced()`, `_lastSynced` and the 6 call sites in `mcp-registration.js` are untouched and harmless. `styleStats` were never affected: every style increment already writes session and lifetime as a matched pair, and no direct `editStats` increments exist outside `bump()`. Verified: new `test/test_stats_no_double_count.js` passes 8/8 on the fixed code and FAILS 4 checks against a copy with the old body restored, with the exact bug numbers (10 bumps -> lifetime 20; interleaved 30 vs 15; nested `fails.*` 8 vs 4); `node --check` clean on `edit-stats.js` and `mcp-registration.js`; the 7 standing suites unchanged. Live after a Pulsar restart: 3 real `str_replace` commits raised lifetime `str_replace.hits` by exactly 3 (2022 -> 2025) and edit ops by exactly 3, and a 12-second idle wait added nothing, where the old code would have moved by 6. That test cannot touch real stats (`STATS_PATH` is null outside Pulsar).

**Lifetime totals recorded BEFORE the fix are not recoverable and must not be quoted as absolutes.** They are doubled AND incomplete. `session/session-history.ndjson` shows why (re-derived from the file 2026-09-24): a session record is only written when `get-edit-stats` is called with `reset:true`, nothing told the assistant to do that, and so the file holds only 37 records: 35 between 2026-06-16 and 2026-07-18, then a 48-day gap, then 2 records (2026-09-03 and 2026-09-17), and none since. Together they log 576 edits against 2,990 lifetime edit ops, and the session counter has restarted from 1 twice (46,47,48 then 1..32, then 1,2). PJ's diagnosis, confirmed: the standing notes never said to flush, so it was never done. Fix applied to the session notes (standing-rules note [0]): an END OF SESSION rule (write the handoff, then call `get-edit-stats` with `reset:true` once, never mid-session) plus a corrected STATS paragraph. Not done, still PJ's call: what to do with the inflated `session/session-stats.json` (recommendation: dated backup, then zero and re-baseline; halving is NOT safe because the history shows the file is not just doubled).

**Housekeeping in this pass.** `lib/_s4e_section3_fragment.js` header rewritten: it said "DRAFT, not wired in" and carried a "NOT YET VERIFIED / FLAG FOR REVIEW" block about same-line counting that was resolved long ago (PJ confirmed the two-hits-per-line behaviour on 2026-09-23); it now states the wired/not-wired split, the reasons, and both behaviour changes. `README.md` corrected in four places: the `lib/match-engine.js` entry contradicted itself (it still said Section 2 was "not yet wired in" two sentences before saying Section 3 was live); it now states that the commit half is not on the engine; the "ambiguity guard" section gained Section 3's two refusals; and the `lib/edit-stats.js` entry notes `syncToLifetime()` is now a no-op.

**Not done / needs PJ.** (1) Delete by hand, because the destructive-command guard blocks `Remove-Item -Recurse`/`rmSync` and `confirm:true` was not authorised: `test/_tmp_buggy/` (contains a deliberately buggy copy of `edit-stats.js`; never run or require it from real code), `test/_stats_live_scratch.txt`, `test/_save_fix_scratch.js`, and this pass's throwaway probes and their `.out`/`.err` captures in `test/` (`_s4e_close_probe.*`, `_s4e_mixed*`, `_readme_*`, `_cl_facts*`, `_cl_history*`, `_docs_scan.out`, `_chk*.out`, `_chk.err`, `_s3out.txt`, `_s3err.txt`). (2) Still stale and unresolved: the `s4e/` drafts (`_s4e_section1_preview.js`, `_s4e_section2_scope.js` v2) and `lib/mcp-registration.js.bac`. (3) The inflated `session-stats.json` decision above. (4) Not investigated: the `get-edit-stats` reset handler's zeroing loop only resets counters it names, so any counter not named there is never zeroed on reset; possibly a separate source of drift. (5) The bigger stage after S4e (porting `str_replace`'s multi-line/regex/fuzzy matching, failure builder and commit onto the engine, plus `sed`/`replace-all` and a second start/end tool) is undecided.

## S4e: Section 2 (scope resolution) confirmed wired + live-verified; `str_replace` allSymbols ReferenceError (edits landing in the buffer but never saved) fixed and live-verified (2026-09-24)

**Context.** Closing out the three planned S4e sections (this is NOT a claim that `str_replace` is fully ported to the engine -- see the last paragraph). Two things were settled this pass: (1) Section 2's real status, which several docs still described as "not wired", and (2) the cause of a long-running buffer-vs-disk confusion in which `str_replace` edits appeared to succeed but never reached disk.

**Section 2 status -- wired and live-verified (correcting the stale "Sections 2/3 still not wired" text in the entries below and in `mcp-server-refactor-plan.md`).** `resolveStrReplaceScope()` in `lib/_s4e_section2_scope.js` (the live `lib/` copy is v3, 322 lines; the `s4e/` copy is the older v2 draft) has been wired into `lib/mcp-registration.js` since 2026-09-23: the require sits at ~L27 and the call at ~L302, replacing the ~145-line hand-rolled scope if/else chain. v3 differs from v2 in one important way: v2 only had failure-path wording verified against live source, so wiring it as-is would have silently changed every SUCCESS `scopeLabel`; v3 rebuilds each hint type's success wording to match `str_replace`'s exact prior output. The caller deliberately passes `resolvedRadius: HINT_RADIUS` (the bare constant) to reproduce a pre-existing label quirk (the afterString/beforeString label prints the constant, not the caller's `hintRadius`), per the standing "reproduce quirks first, fix later" rule.

**Live bug found and fixed in Section 2 (2026-09-23).** `str_replace`'s handler defaults `occurrence = 1`, but `resolveScope()` treats any non-null `occurrence` (including 1) as an explicit disambiguation pick, so a genuinely ambiguous `inFunction`/`afterFunction`/`afterSymbol` hint (e.g. two functions named `beta`) was silently resolved to the first match instead of being refused. Fix in `lib/_s4e_section2_scope.js` (~L113): only forward `occurrence` into `resolveScope()` when it is greater than 1 (`scopeOccurrence`). Live-verified after a restart: `inFunction:"beta"` now refuses as ambiguous and lists both lines, `occurrence:2` narrows to the second `beta`, and a unique `inFunction:"alpha"` still resolves. Section 3 received the same treatment (`_fragOccurrence`). Note: no dedicated Section 2 test file exists; the "213/213" figure is the sum of the 7 standing suites, not a Section 2-specific count.

**Bug found and fixed: `allSymbols is not defined` in `str_replace` (2026-09-24).** The Section 2 rewire removed the hand-rolled scope block, which also contained the only `const allSymbols = getSymbols(...)` declaration in `str_replace`'s handler. The success path still passes `symbols: allSymbols` to `successNudge()` (~L1139), so every committed (non-dry-run) `str_replace` threw a `ReferenceError` AFTER `buffer.setTextInRange()` (~L1105) and BEFORE `buildEditResponse()`, which is the call that saves the buffer. Result: the buffer was edited, disk was never written, and buffer-first read tools kept agreeing with the unsaved buffer. This was the real mechanism behind the README buffer/disk loop of 2026-09-23/24. Dry-run paths return earlier, which is why Section 2 and Section 3 dry-run verification never hit it. The "allSymbols is not defined" error previously logged as benign was NOT benign.

**Correction to an earlier conclusion: `str_replace` was never missing an auto-save.** A session note claimed `str_replace` had no save because a grep found no `.save()` inside its handler. That was wrong: `str_replace` passes `buffer` to `buildEditResponse()` (B26), which calls `buffer.save()` when `buffer` is set and `dryRun` is false (`lib/edit-response.js` ~L93-95), exactly as documented in the 2026-09-16 "Auto-save architecture confirmed universal" entry below. No save call should be added to `str_replace`. The only defect was the exception thrown before that save was reached.

**Fix.** Added `const allSymbols = getSymbols(editor, text, ctx.filePath);` in `str_replace`'s handler directly after the `isCodeFile` line (~L298). Applied with `insert` (dry-run previewed, then committed), so it was saved to disk; `node --check` clean and the line confirmed on disk via a raw PowerShell read.

**Live-verified after a restart.** Two real non-dry-run `str_replace` commits on a scratch fixture, one scoped (`inFunction:"alpha"`) and one with no hints, both returned a clean success line with no `allSymbols` error, and a separate raw disk read showed the new content each time. Standing suite re-run clean, all 7: `s4_diagnostics_test` 88/88, `s1_occurrence_test` 23/23, `s3_identifier_boundary_test` 23/23, `s4_engine_probe` 0 failures, `insert_regex_wiring_test` 34/34, `s4b_fragment_match_test` 29/29, `s4d_replace_preview_test` 16/16 (213 total), plus `lib/test_s4e_section1.js` 10/10 and `lib/test_s4e_section3.js` 10/10.

**Not done.** `str_replace` is still a hybrid, not fully ported to the engine. Verified by grep of its handler (2026-09-24): the engine now backs its scope resolution (Section 2), dry-run preview (Section 1), plain single-line match (Section 3) and rescue paths (S4a, `recover.js`), but the commit itself is still a hand-rolled character-index splice via `buffer.setTextInRange`, multi-line/regex/fuzzy matching is still its own code, and its failure/diagnostic builder is still its own (no `buildFailResponse`/`matchContentEngine` calls inside its handler). S4 as a whole is therefore not complete; `sed`/`replace-all` and the second start/end pair tool were not re-audited. `test/_save_fix_scratch.js` (the live-test fixture) still needs deleting by hand -- the destructive-command guard blocked `Remove-Item` and `confirm:true` was not authorised. Optional hardening not applied: wrap the post-`setTextInRange` bookkeeping in `str_replace` (decorate, nudge, lint, style) in try/finally so a late throw can never again leave an unsaved buffer. `README.md` is not yet updated for this fix. Still-outstanding housekeeping: the stale `s4e/_s4e_section1_preview.js` and `s4e/_s4e_section2_scope.js` (v2) drafts and `lib/mcp-registration.js.bac`.

## S4e: `str_replace` switch-over — Section 1 (dry-run preview) wired in and live-verified after a restart; Sections 2/3 still not wired (2026-09-23)

**Context.** The user authored a design doc (`s4e/S4E_DESIGN.md`) and three draft modules splitting the S4e switch-over into three independent, separately-revertible pieces: Section 1 (dry-run preview → `buildReplacePreview`), Section 2 (scope resolution → `resolveScope`), Section 3 (plain single-line match+commit → `matchFragment`+`applyEditSpan`, closing the S3 count/discount hazard). All three were marked DRAFT, not applied. This session's job was to verify, fix, and test them — not to design them.

**Bugs found and fixed**, both by checking the drafts directly against live source rather than trusting their own comments:
- Section 3 imported `matchFragment` from `./match-engine`, but it's actually exported from `lib/fragment-match.js` (confirmed via both files' real `module.exports`) — would have thrown `MODULE_NOT_FOUND` at load time. Fixed: split into two correct `require()`s.
- Section 2 (v1 → v2, larger rewrite): (a) used one generic message builder for all 5 hint types, but str_replace's real wording is NOT uniform — header text, `[kind]` tag presence, and ambiguous-message shape all differ across `inFunction` / `afterFunction`+`beforeFunction` / `afterSymbol`+`beforeSymbol` / `afterString`+`beforeString`, transcribed and fixed per-group with live line citations; (b) destructured a `candidates` field off `scope.error` that doesn't exist anywhere in `match-engine.js` — the real field is `raw` (`raw.matches` for symbol-based hints, `raw.rows` for the string-based hints), a *quiet* bug that would have silently rendered empty "(none)" listings rather than crashing.

**Policy questions closed.** Both open items flagged in the design doc were answered explicitly by the user this session: Section 1's dry-run output byte-change (`?`→`❯` marker, header text) is approved. Section 3's intended behavior change — `matchFragment` counts two overlapping hits on the same line as two separate matches, where str_replace's old `.includes()`-based check only ever counted one hit per line regardless of repeats — is confirmed as the correct behavior going forward.

**Unit-tested this session** (all scratch test/stub files, deleted after each run — none left in the tree): Section 2 33/33 (mock `resolveScope` via a temporary stub, covering every hint-group's exact wording plus bump()/logHintFailure() side effects); Section 3 10/10 (real `lib/match-engine.js`+`lib/fragment-match.js`, fake buffer, covers the count/discount guard, the escape hatch, the confirmed two-hits-per-line counting, and cross-line ambiguity with/without an explicit `occurrence`); Section 1 10/10 (real `buildReplacePreview`, covers the marker/header change, all four tags, and the trailer appearing exactly once).

**Standing regression suite** re-run clean after cleanup, all 7: `s4_diagnostics_test` 88/88, `s1_occurrence_test` 23/23, `s3_identifier_boundary_test` 23/23, `s4_engine_probe` 0 failures, `insert_regex_wiring_test` 34/34, `s4b_fragment_match_test` 29/29, `s4d_replace_preview_test` 16/16.

**Section 1 wired in and live-verified (2026-09-23, continuation of the same push).** Re-grepped `mcp-registration.js` for current line numbers (design doc's citations had drifted, as expected — `str_replace` now spans ~222–1320, dry-run block at 1121–1142). Added `require('./_s4e_section1_preview')` after the existing `recover.js` require, then replaced the hand-rolled dry-run block with a call to `buildDryRunResult({...})`. Found along the way: the draft module only existed at `s4e/_s4e_section1_preview.js` in the project root, not at `lib/` where the require path needed it — fixed via `copy-file` into `lib/` (the `s4e/` original is now a stale duplicate, not yet deleted). `node --check` clean; a whole-file `check-struct` brace-imbalance flag (+1) was traced and dismissed as the same known heuristic noise documented under S4a (isolating the edited region confirmed it balances). After a full restart: `get-diagnostics` on `mcp-registration.js` returned 0/0/0; all 7 standing tests re-run clean (88/23/23/0-fail/34/29/16, no regressions); a real live `dryRun:true` call against a scratch fixture produced the expected `🔍 DRY RUN` / `❯` output end-to-end through the actual edited code, and a real non-dry-run commit on the same fixture succeeded normally. This closes Section 1 as fully live-verified, not just syntax-checked.

**Not done.** Sections 2 and 3 are still not wired into `lib/mcp-registration.js`. The design doc's line-number citations for their target blocks will have drifted further since Section 1's own re-grep already found drift; re-confirm current line numbers before drafting either swap. The stale `s4e/_s4e_section1_preview.js` duplicate and `lib/mcp-registration.js.bac` (now stale across many sessions) are still outstanding cleanup/housekeeping items, not blocking further work.

## S4e: Section 3 (plain single-line match) wired in and live-verified after multiple restarts (2026-09-24)

**Context.** Continuation of the S4e switch-over. A prior session had Section 3's wiring drop out of `lib/mcp-registration.js` entirely before it was ever live-verified -- the standalone `lib/_s4e_section3_fragment.js` module and its unit test (10/10) survived untouched, but the registration-side `require`, the `guardIdentifier` schema/handler param, and the `tryPlainFragmentMatch` call were all absent from the live file (confirmed via `grep-file` returning 0 matches for all four). This session re-applied that wiring from the design doc and the fragment module's own header comment, then live-verified it end-to-end.

**Bug found and fixed this session (new, distinct from the earlier-session import-path/Section 2 bugs already logged above).** The first re-wire attempt assigned into `allOccurrenceLines`, which is declared `const allOccurrenceLines = []` a few lines above and is meant to be `.push()`ed into everywhere else in the surrounding code. `allOccurrenceLines = [...]` in the matched branch and `allOccurrenceLines = _frag.matchRows` in the ambiguous branch both throw `Assignment to constant variable` -- and because the buggy branch runs on *every* plain-text `str_replace` match, not just the identifier-guard edge case, this crashed `str_replace` globally (any file, any call) once loaded, not just against the specific test case. Root-caused by testing a completely unrelated trivial dry-run on a scratch file and getting the identical crash. Fixed by changing both sites to `.push(...)` (with a spread for the array case). Also cost real time this session: two edit attempts made via `str_replace` itself reported apparent success (no thrown error visible in the response, `grep-file` appeared to confirm the change) but never actually wrote to disk -- root cause unconfirmed, but consistent with the handler crashing before reaching its own write step. The fix that actually landed was applied via `run-command` + PowerShell + `[System.IO.File]::WriteAllText`, bypassing `str_replace` entirely, and was independently verified via raw `Get-Content`/regex checks and `LastWriteTime`, not via `grep-file` or the tool's own response.

**Live-verified after the corrective restart**, all 5 of the design doc's scenarios against a scratch fixture: (a) `old_str:"count"` vs `"let discount = 5;"` -> correct `onlyIdentifierGlued` refusal; (b) same + `guardIdentifier:false` -> correct clean match (escape hatch works); (c) `old_str:"total"` vs an unrelated line -> correct plain match, unaffected by the guard; (d) `old_str:"dup"` with two matching lines and no `occurrence` -> correct ambiguous refusal listing both lines (not a silent first-match pick -- the exact bug class Section 2 already had fixed once); (e) same + `occurrence:2` -> correctly resolves to the second line. Standing regression suite re-run clean at every stage (before the re-wire, after the re-wire, after the `.push()` fix, and after the final restart): `s4_diagnostics_test` 88/88, `s1_occurrence_test` 23/23, `s3_identifier_boundary_test` 23/23, `s4_engine_probe` 0 failures, `insert_regex_wiring_test` 34/34, `lib/test_s4e_section1.js` 10/10, `lib/test_s4e_section3.js` 10/10 -- unsurprising, since none of these exercise the wired code path in `mcp-registration.js` directly (they test the standalone modules), so neither the missing-wiring state nor the `const`-reassignment crash was ever caught by the standing suite. Only the live probe against a real scratch file caught both bugs. Scratch fixture `test/_s4e_section3_live_scratch.js` deleted after the final clean run.


## S4d: `buildReplacePreview` — shared dry-run preview builder for replace-style tools (2026-09-23, live-verified after a restart)

**Context.** Per the plan doc's own ordering (S4d, "a shared match-preview builder for every tool"): `str_replace`'s own dry-run block hand-rolls a context window with matched rows marked plus a `- old`/`+ new` diff (~20 lines), and `insert` already had its own equivalent, `buildInsertPreview` (T2, 2026-07-20). `buildReplacePreview` is the replace-side sibling, placed directly after `buildInsertPreview` in `match-engine.js`. The function itself was already written and exported before this session's work began — no session note describes that authorship, so it is recorded here as found rather than as done-this-session; what this entry covers is verification.

**Shape.** `buildReplacePreview({ allLines, lineCount, matchLine, matchLines, new_str, scopeLabel = '', tags = [] })`. Takes `matchLine`/`matchLines` directly from what `matchContent`/`matchBlock`/`matchFunction` already return, so a caller can pass its `match` result through with no reshaping. Context window uses the same `radius = 3` convention as `buildInsertPreview`, marking matched rows with `❯` and leaving other rows plain. Diff is `matchLines.map(l => '- '+l)` concatenated with `new_str.split('\n').map(l => '+ '+l)`. `tags` is a `string[]` of bracketed annotations (e.g. `['fuzzyWhitespace']`) rendered as `[fuzzyWhitespace]` in the header, replacing the hand-built ternary chain `str_replace` uses today.
