Returns `{content, matched:true, dryRun:true, matchLine}` (1-based), the same shape `buildMatchResponse`'s dry branch produces, so a caller can adopt it without reshaping its return. Unlike `buildInsertPreview`, its trailer does NOT offer `commitLastPreview` — confirmed by direct source read, a real behavioral difference, not an oversight.

**Verified this session (live, after a user-initiated restart).** Before restart: `node --check` clean; `grep-project` (glob `lib/*.js`) confirmed the function had zero callers anywhere in `lib/` — only its own definition and `module.exports` entry — matching its header comment's claim that nothing calls it yet. All 6 standing tests (the original 5 plus `s4b_fragment_match_test.js`) passed with the function present but unreachable. After the restart: `get-diagnostics` on `match-engine.js` confirmed 0/0/0 (linter active, file loaded clean); all 6 standing tests re-run with identical clean results. A new functional test was written that `require`s the real live module (not a re-implementation) and calls `buildReplacePreview` directly with two fixtures — a single-line replace inside `function beta` with a `fuzzyWhitespace` tag, and a multi-line replace with no `scopeLabel`/`tags` — checking the raw rendered output line-by-line, not just return-shape booleans. 16/16 passed, confirming: the `❯` marker lands on exactly the matched row(s); the header renders scopeLabel and tags verbatim; the diff produces correct `- `/`+ ` lines for both single- and multi-line matches; empty `scopeLabel`/`tags` produce no stray `undefined` or `[]` artifacts.

**Tests:** `test/s4d_replace_preview_test.js` (new, 16/16) — promoted from a one-off manual probe (`test/_s4d_manual_probe.js`, now a stale duplicate needing manual deletion) after live verification succeeded.

**Not done:** no negative-control check yet (unlike S4b's fragment-match test, which deliberately breaks its own guard to prove the test can fail) — this test currently only proves the happy path renders correctly. `buildReplacePreview` is still not wired into any tool; `str_replace`'s own hand-rolled ~20-line preview block is unchanged. Whether to wire it into `str_replace` now (a smaller, separable step) or leave it unwired until the full S4e switch-over is an open question, not decided here.

## S4c: audit found the scope-hint gap mostly already closed; `fuzzyWhitespace` forwarding fixed (2026-09-23, live)

**Context.** Per the plan doc's own ordering, S4c was next: "extend `resolveScope` with what `str_replace` has and it lacks." Before designing anything, read `str_replace`'s actual hint-resolution block in `mcp-registration.js` (L220-1267) directly, rather than trusting the plan doc's "not started" framing — the same correction pattern as the S4-1 finding above. That block's own comment at L247 already read "S4c (2026-09-22): sectionHint/preprocBlock/preprocSide are new to str_replace" — meaning `str_replace` had already gained `sectionHint`/`preprocBlock`/`preprocSide` (schema L253-255), `logHintFailure` calls in every hint branch, `fails.outOfScope`/`fails.afterNotFound` stat naming, and occurrence-on-hint handling per branch, in an earlier session than the notes trail showed. None of this had previously been marked done anywhere.

**Change.** Compared `str_replace`'s hint block against `match-engine.js`'s `resolveScope`/`_resolveScopeCore` directly. Found exactly one real remaining gap: `str_replace`'s `afterString`/`beforeString` call to `resolveStringPosition` (`mcp-registration.js` L407) omitted the `{ fuzzyWhitespace }` options object that the engine's `resolveScope` forwards (`match-engine.js` L236) — so `str_replace`'s anchor-*scoping* step lacked fuzzy-whitespace tolerance even though its later content-match step (via S4a's `profiledMatch`) already has it. Fixed: `mcp-registration.js` L407 now reads `resolveStringPosition(allLines, hintVal, dir, _radius, { fuzzyWhitespace })`. One-line parity patch, not a redesign; `fuzzyWhitespace` was already in scope at that point (handler destructure L265).

**Remaining gap, deliberately not touched.** `str_replace` still does not call `resolveScope()` itself — its L294-435 block is a complete parallel hand-rolled scope-resolution implementation, not a delegate, so the "Known functions:"/"Known symbols:" listings and ambiguity messages are still duplicated by hand rather than shared with the engine. This is structural enough that it's recommended to fold into S4e's switch-over (`str_replace` moves onto the engine last) rather than remain its own stage — S4c as originally scoped is now essentially closed.

**Verified.** `mcp-registration.js` L407 change committed via `str_replace` with an `afterString` anchor, confirmed by the tool's own line-number echo. The standing regression suite (`s4_diagnostics_test.js`, `s1_occurrence_test.js`, `s3_identifier_boundary_test.js`, `s4_engine_probe.js`, `insert_regex_wiring_test.js`) was **not** re-run this session — only a `resolveStringPosition` call site gained one argument, and `mcp-registration.js` changes are picked up by a new MCP session rather than requiring a full restart — but re-running them is recommended before treating this as fully regression-clean.

**Not done.** The structural `resolveScope()`-delegation gap itself; a README.md staleness check for S4c (S4a and S4b both needed one); PJ's confirmation that folding the remaining work into S4e is the right call.

## S4b: character-span fragment matcher + `applyEdit` span mode (2026-09-22, pure additions, no live tool wired in yet)

**Context:** the identifier-boundary hazard (`str_replace`'s plain single-line branch matches `count` inside `discount`; a single such hit commits silently) has had a safety-guard helper since S3 (`lib/identifier-boundary.js`), but nothing could wire it in because the engine had no sub-line (character-span) match shape — `matchContent`/`scanBlock` only ever compare whole lines.

**Change:**
- New pure module `lib/fragment-match.js` — `matchFragment({ lines, needle, startRow, endRow, occurrence, guardIdentifier })`. Single-line by design (fragments don't span lines; that's `matchContent`'s job). Returns a row+column span shape: `{ matched, row, startCol, endCol, actualText, totalMatches, guardedOut, boundary }`. Uses `checkIdentifierBoundary` from S3's helper on every raw hit. **DECIDED 2026-09-22 (open question 4): refuse, not warn** — `guardIdentifier:true` (the default) EXCLUDES glued hits from the candidate set; when only some hits on a line are glued they're filtered out (not refuse-and-list), with `guardedOut` on the result reporting how many were excluded so a caller isn't left guessing. `guardIdentifier:false` is the named escape hatch for intentional mid-identifier edits — it returns glued hits too, each still carrying its own `boundary`, for a caller that wants warn-not-refuse semantics to inspect directly.
- New `applyEditSpan(buffer, match, newStr)` in `lib/match-engine.js`, added directly after `applyEdit` and exported alongside it. Span-mode sibling of `applyEdit`: keeps the same B39 (drift relocate) / B40 (byte-for-byte re-verify immediately before write) safety shape, expressed in row+column terms against `match.actualText` instead of whole-row `match.actualLines`. Tries a same-row rescan first (cheap common case: an edit on the same row shifted columns only), then a full-buffer search for `actualText`, before refusing with `driftUnrecoverable`.
- **Shape choice (row + column, not a global buffer-text character offset):** rows are what every other part of the engine already keys on, and a row+column span verifies/relocates against `buffer.getLines()` directly with no text-vs-lines offset conversion needed. This does NOT require the plan doc's "open question 1" (rows vs. characters in `resolveScope`) to be answered first — that question is about the scope-WINDOW layer; `matchFragment` still takes row bounds from a resolved scope and searches within them line by line.

**Additive, no existing tool changed:** neither `matchFragment` nor `applyEditSpan` is called by any registered tool yet. `str_replace`'s plain single-line branch, and every other tool, behaves exactly as before this session.

**Tests:** `test/s4b_fragment_match_test.js` (new, 29/29) — plain hits, the count-inside-discount hazard refused by default and exposed via `guardIdentifier:false`, a both-edges-protected fragment correctly NOT flagged (contrasted with the glued case), a negative control (`split().join()`, never `String.replace`, per the standing testing rule), ambiguity/occurrence-out-of-range refusal, scoped `startRow`/`endRow`, bad-input handling, and `applyEditSpan` against a fake buffer (plain object: `getLines`/`setTextInRange`/`getLineCount`) covering no-drift, same-row drift, full-buffer (row-shifted) drift, unrecoverable drift, and `matchFragment` → `applyEditSpan` integration. All 5 standing regression tests re-run clean afterward: `s4_diagnostics_test.js` 88/88, `s1_occurrence_test.js` 23/23, `s3_identifier_boundary_test.js` 23/23, `s4_engine_probe.js` 0 failures, `insert_regex_wiring_test.js` 34/34.

**Not done:** S3's guard is still not wired into any live tool (that needs PJ's answer to open question 4, plus S4c's scope work per the plan doc); `node --check` was run on both new/changed files but there has been NO live Pulsar restart or in-editor exercise of these two modules (unlike S4a, nothing here is reachable from a registered tool yet, so a restart isn't required before this is "real" — but it also means there is no live-integration test the way S4a got one). Test-output capture files from this session (see CLEANUP CHECKLIST) still need manual deletion — `run-command`'s destructive-command guard blocks the assistant from doing it directly.

## S4a: `str_replace`'s own inline rescue now delegates to `lib/recover.js` (2026-09-22, live-verified after a restart)

**Context.** `str_replace` had its own ~260-line hand-rolled copy of the profiling/rescue logic (`mcp-registration.js` L743-1000) that `lib/recover.js` already exposed as `profiledMatch()`/`partialMatchRescue()` for the shared engine (see S4). Per standing direction, `str_replace` was deliberately left on its own code as the control until the shared module could be proven to reproduce it exactly. This entry swaps `str_replace` onto `recover.js`.

**Change.** `mcp-registration.js`: added `const { profiledMatch, partialMatchRescue } = require('./recover');`. Replaced the inline profiling head (needle/haystack line-splitting, the local `_norm` unicode map, the local `_profileScan` needle classifier, and the fuzzy-rebuild scan) with a single `profiledMatch(old_str, searchText)` call. Replaced the inline partial-match block (2-line leader search across the full buffer) with a single `partialMatchRescue(old_str, text, searchStart, searchEnd)` call. `recover.js`'s functions are pure — no `bump()` calls, no message strings — so every existing stat counter and every user-facing message string was preserved verbatim in the caller, unchanged in wording.

**Bug introduced and fixed during the edit, both caught before commit reached PJ.** (1) The inline block's replacement left 4 orphaned closing braces from the deleted code's deeper nesting; `node --check` failed with a syntax error, `check-struct` confirmed a net brace imbalance, and the exact original closing sequence was recovered from `lib/mcp-registration.js.bac` (the pre-edit backup) to fix it precisely rather than by guesswork. (2) The fix overshot by one brace; isolating just `str_replace`'s own object literal (grepped boundary: L221-1262) into a scratch file and running `check-struct` on it alone confirmed it was actually balanced — the whole-file heuristic's "+1" was noise from elsewhere in the 7000+ line file, not a real defect in the edited region.

**Live-verified, 2026-09-22, after PJ restarted Pulsar.** All five of `profiledMatch`'s/`partialMatchRescue`'s branches were exercised through the real edited `mcp-registration.js` on a scratch file, content re-read via `read-file` immediately before each test (not assumed from memory):
1. Comment-strip: `old_str` without a trailing comment matched a buffer line that had one; correct rebuild, correct diff.
2. `fuzzyWhitespace`-only: a differently-indented `old_str` (no comment involved) matched the buffer's real indentation.
3. `partialMatchRescue`: search scoped away from the target (`beforeLine` pointed at unrelated lines) still found a 2-line needle at its real location elsewhere in the file — confirms the full-buffer fallback actually searches beyond the caller's window, not a coincidental in-window hit.
4. `fuzzyContent`/encoding: `old_str` with smart/curly quotes matched a buffer line with straight ASCII quotes.
5. Ambiguity guard: two identical buffer lines, one differently-indented `old_str` that fuzzy-matches both — refused with the same "found similar text in more than one place" message as before, not a silent first-pick.

An earlier apparent failure on path 1 was a false alarm: a test string one character under `recover.js`'s `STRIP_MIN_TOTAL=10` anchor-length guard, and a second test where the buffer content assumed to exist was never actually written — not a code defect. Traced fully before concluding so.

**Verified.** Offline: `test/_s4a_parity_probe.js` 13/13 (tests `recover.js` against a separately re-implemented copy of the old inline logic — confirms `recover.js` itself, not the live integration). `test/s4_diagnostics_test.js` 88/88 (including all 21 `source-identical` wording-pin checks, re-run against the post-swap source), `test/s1_occurrence_test.js` 23/23, `test/s4_engine_probe.js` 0 failures, `test/insert_regex_wiring_test.js` 34/34 — no regressions in any of the four standing suites. `node --check` and an isolated `check-struct` on `str_replace`'s own object literal both clean.

**Not done.** PJ has not reviewed the literal diff line-by-line; the edit proceeded on his general go-ahead plus a restart, not a turn-by-turn sign-off on the exact patch text. `lib/mcp-registration.js.bac` is now stale (pre-S4a) and was not refreshed. `str_replace`'s own object-literal boundary (`mcp-registration.js` L221-1262 as of this entry) will drift on the next edit to this file — re-grep for `name: 'str_replace'` / `name: 'find-text'` rather than trusting these line numbers on a later pass.

## Engine failure diagnosis (S4): `insert`, `delete` and `replace-block` now explain a miss the way `str_replace` does; regex and partial-match handling in the engine tightened (2026-09-21; live-verified across five restarts, the final regex/wording round on 2026-09-22)

**Context.** After S1 to S3 the engine was safe but mute. When a content match failed, `insert`, `delete` and `replace-block` printed two or three lines, while `str_replace` (which has its own inline code) explained *why*: a whitespace mismatch, a partial match, the closest area, a similarity score, and where else the text lives. S4 moves that diagnosis into one pure module that every engine tool shares, together with the same `fails.*` counters. `str_replace` itself is deliberately NOT moved yet (S4e, last): its hand-rolled branch stays as the live control until a live diff shows the engine reproduces it.

**Shared diagnosis (live-verified).**
- `lib/fail-diagnostics.js` (new, pure, no Pulsar or I/O): `diagnose()` returns message pieces plus raw counts. It reports a **whitespace mismatch** (a needle line equals a buffer line after trimming but not raw), a **partial match** (the first N of M needle lines matched consecutively, then diverged; multi-line needles only), the **closest area** (±4 lines), a **similarity** score in three tiers, a **drift nudge** when `afterLine`/`beforeLine` is used, and **where else the text exists** (nowhere / inside the scope / outside the scope). An optional `subject` names the input in the messages (`startContent`, `endContent`; default `old_str`), and `fuzzyAdvice:false` drops the "retry with `fuzzyWhitespace:true`" recommendation for calls that do not honour it.
- `lib/match-engine.js`: `resolveScope()` is now a thin wrapper that attaches the raw hint values as `scope.hints` (no call site changed); a new exported `diagnoseFailure()` runs `diagnose()` and does the shared counter bumps; `buildFailResponse()`'s not-found branch calls it and keeps the engine's own `❌ <tool>: content not found` header. Ambiguous, out-of-range-occurrence, hint-resolution and scope-error failures are untouched.
- **Tools sharing it now:** `insert` (`afterContent`/`beforeContent`), `delete` `matchString`, `delete` Mode 4 (`startContent`/`endContent`), `replace-block` (`anchor`).
- **Counters.** `whitespace`, `encoding`, `partialMatch` and `foundOutsideScope` (plus `noMatch` where absent) were added to the `fails` block of `insert`, `delete`, `replace_function_body`, `replace_block`, `replace_all` and `sed`, and `encoding` to `str_replace`. `bump()` ignores a missing leaf silently, so `str_replace`'s `fails.encoding` had never counted anything. Defaults overlay the on-disk lifetime stats, so the new keys start at 0 with no migration. The tally is PARALLEL: the engine bumps the shared key and the tool's own call site still bumps its specific one (`anchorNotFound`, `startNotFound`, ...), so one failure counts in two places on purpose. Confirmed for `delete`, `insert` and `replace_block`.

**Four fidelity fixes, each found by running the SAME failing call through the engine tool and through `str_replace` and diffing the output (all live-verified).**
1. **Diagnosis window.** `str_replace` diagnoses only inside the function or region for `inFunction`/`betweenHint`. The first engine cut diagnosed the whole file, so one scoped miss scored 89% with a whitespace hit at L7 in the engine and 61% with no hit in `str_replace`. The engine now narrows for the same two hints, and for `delete` Mode 4's anchors, which search from a start row to the end and so must not be diagnosed above it. Every other hint stays whole-file, as in `str_replace`.
2. **What counts as a scope.** `str_replace` counts `occurrence` toward "has a scope" only when it is greater than 1. The engine counted `occurrence:1` too, so `delete ... occurrence:1` printed "inside scope" and suppressed the NO HINTS USED nudge. Aligned.
3. **`delete` Mode 4 messages.** A `startContent`/`endContent` miss now gets the whole diagnosis, names the input that missed, and no longer prints "(occurrence undefined)" when no occurrence was passed. Mode 4 keeps its own 10-line preview and tip. It gets no retry nudge, because that would advertise `matchString` and silently switch the call to Mode 5.
4. **Text that exists exactly, just outside the search.** An `endContent` that exists only before `startContent` was reported as "Similarity: 100% — likely whitespace/indentation drift". Nothing had drifted; the search looked in the wrong place. The message now says so. It is keyed on the scan result (exact text found only outside the scope), not on the percentage, because the score is rounded: a 250-character needle with one wrong character also scores 100%.

**Partial and fuzzy matching in the engine.** The failure message now names a partial match ("first N of M lines matched consecutively, then diverged") and counts it under `fails.partialMatch` only when 0 < N < M, otherwise under `noMatch`, which is `str_replace`'s own classification. The automatic partial-match rescue from B32 is unchanged, and `delete` still refuses to use it. **`delete` Mode 4 now honours `fuzzyWhitespace`, `fuzzyContent` and `regex` on both anchors.** It is opt-in: with none set, behaviour is unchanged and `autoRescue` stays false. Live-verified as dry runs: a whitespace-only start anchor fails without the flag (and now recommends it) and previews "delete 2 line(s) (lines 2–3) ... matched via fuzzyWhitespace" with it; with the flag already set the advice is not repeated; a fuzzy anchor that matches two lines differing only in indentation is refused as ambiguous and deletes nothing, and `occurrence:2` then selects the second; `hintsUsed.fuzzyWhitespace` counts each call. The `delete` tool description now says the flags apply to both anchors.

**Regex matching in the engine — LIVE-VERIFIED (2026-09-22, after the fifth restart).** Found by dry-running the newly enabled Mode 4 regex, and already present in `delete` `matchString`:
1. **Rows came from the raw match edges.** A pattern with a leading `\s*` swallowed the newline before the line, so `delete {matchString:'\s*const total = \d;', regex:true, dryRun:true}` previewed "delete 2 line(s) (lines 1–2)" and a real call would have removed `function alpha() {` too; Mode 4 previewed lines 1–3. Rows now come from the first and last NON-WHITESPACE character of the match (a whitespace-only match keeps its raw edges). Everything that takes whole rows from a regex match is affected: `delete` (both modes) and `replace-block`. Live-verified: `\s*const total = \d;` now previews lines 2–2, and Mode 4 with a leading `^\s*` start anchor previews lines 2–3. **`insert` is NOT verifiable here:** its handler (`mcp-registration.js` ~L1564) destructures `fuzzyWhitespace` but not `regex` or `fuzzyContent`, so `insert` `afterContent`/`beforeContent` never reach the engine's regex branch (see "Not covered yet").
2. **`^` and `$` were whole-text anchors.** The engine compiled with `'g'`; `str_replace` compiles with `'gm'` (`mcp-registration.js` ~L575). So `^\s*const ...` never matched, and `^function` silently took the FIRST of two matching lines. The engine now uses `'gm'`: `^` and `$` are line anchors, `^function` reports both lines as ambiguous, and `occurrence:N` chooses. Match counts equal `str_replace`'s for the 8 patterns compared. **Behaviour change:** a pattern that relied on `^`/`$` as a whole-text anchor can now match more places and report "ambiguous" where it used to pick one. `str_replace`'s own regex path is separate code and is unchanged; it replaces the exact matched characters, so it never had the over-delete.
3. **Wording, same round.** "Likely whitespace/indentation drift" is now said only when a whitespace mismatch was actually found: a typo such as `totl` for `total` scored 83% and was blamed on drift, and now reads "close but not identical ... a typo, a wrong character, or an invisible/Unicode difference". In the exact-text-outside-the-scope case the similarity percentage is hidden (it is still returned on the result object).

**Verified.** Offline: `test/s4_diagnostics_test.js` 88/88 (13 negative controls), `test/s4_engine_probe.js` 107 checks with 0 failures (it drives the real engine in plain Node), `test/s1_occurrence_test.js` 23/23, `test/s3_identifier_boundary_test.js` 23/23, and `node --check` clean on `match-engine.js`, `fail-diagnostics.js`, `mcp-registration.js` and `edit-stats.js`. Live, after five restarts, all dry runs on scratch files: everything above, including the regex round on 2026-09-22 (`\s*const total = \d;` previews lines 2–2, `^\s*const total = \d;` matches, Mode 4 regex anchors preview lines 2–3, `^function` is refused as ambiguous with `occurrence:2` selecting the second line, a typo anchor reads "close but not identical" with no drift claim, an end anchor that exists only before the start anchor says the text exists exactly and prints no similarity line, and a real whitespace-only mismatch still says "likely whitespace/indentation drift"). Stats read from `session/session-stats.json` after its ~70 s async flush: one whitespace-only `delete` `startContent` failure moved `startNotFound` and `whitespace` (+2 each, the known doubling) and nothing else, re-confirmed on 2026-09-22 after a 75 s stable baseline; `hintsUsed.fuzzyWhitespace` moved once per fuzzy call. The offline suites were re-run on 2026-09-22 with the same results. **Not done:** a real, non-dry-run commit was never exercised for the new Mode 4 flags; `replace-block` with `regex:true` was not live-tested; `insert` with `regex:true` cannot be tested until the gap below is closed.

**Not covered yet.** `sed`, `replace-all`, `replace-function-body` (its needle is a function name, so a similarity score would be meaningless) and the second start/end pair tool (~L2475) do not use the shared diagnosis; `str_replace` is S4e. **`insert` `afterContent`/`beforeContent` now honour `regex` and `fuzzyContent` (written 2026-09-22, live-verified 2026-09-22 after a restart).** The gap found earlier the same day: the tool's Zod schema advertised `regex`/`fuzzyContent` (via `STRUCTURAL_ANCHOR_SCHEMA` → `ANCHOR_SCHEMA`) but the handler (`mcp-registration.js` L1564) destructured only `fuzzyWhitespace`, and its call into `matchContentEngine` (~L1744) forwarded only `{ fuzzyWhitespace, occurrence }`. Both now include `fuzzyContent` and `regex`. This is opt-in and additive — neither flag defaults to true, so a plain `afterContent`/`beforeContent` call with no flags is unaffected. `autoRescue` was already on by default for `insert` (a non-destructive caller), which is unchanged. Offline: `node test/insert_regex_wiring_test.js` (34 checks, 3 of them negative controls that revert the fix in a copy of the source and confirm the checks then fail) drives the real engine with the exact option shape the handler forwards and confirms: the regex row fix now applies to `insert` (a trailing `\s*` no longer inserts one row too low); `^function` matching two lines is refused as ambiguous rather than picking the first, and `occurrence:2` resolves it; an invalid regex reports `matched:false` rather than throwing. It also found that `fuzzyContent` was rarely observable before this fix, because `insert` already runs with `autoRescue` on and the rescue path normalises common unicode cases anyway — the difference shows only with `autoRescue:false`, or where the rescue path mislabels the transform (a BOM was tagged `fuzzyWhitespace` instead of `fuzzyContent`; forwarding the flag gives the correct label). `node --check` clean on `lib/match-engine.js` and `lib/mcp-registration.js`; `s4_diagnostics_test.js` 88/88, `s1_occurrence_test.js` 23/23, `s3_identifier_boundary_test.js` 23/23, `s4_engine_probe.js` 107 ok / 0 failures all still pass. **Live-verified 2026-09-22, on `test/_s4_live_scratch.c` after a restart:** `insert {afterContent:'const total = \d;', regex:true, dryRun:true}` inserts after line 2 (previously failed with "content not found"); `insert {afterContent:'const total = \d;\s*', regex:true, dryRun:true}` still targets line 2, not line 3; `insert {afterContent:'^function', regex:true, dryRun:true}` is refused as ambiguous (lines 1, 6), and `occurrence:2` resolves it to line 6 (the `beta` function). `beforeContent` shares the same code path as `afterContent` (`const anchor = afterContent ?? beforeContent` feeding one call into `matchContentEngine`) so is very likely fine, but was not separately live-tested; neither was `replace-block`, `sed`, `replace-all` or `replace-function-body` — see "Not covered yet" below. **Known limits, inherited from `str_replace` and reproduced on purpose:** `hasEncodingIssue` looks unreachable (two lines that share a trim cannot differ by a non-ASCII character once whitespace is stripped), so `fails.encoding` will probably stay 0 — a hypothesis from the code plus a brute-force sweep, not a proof; the fault log's `diffVsBuffer` is always null and its `bufferPreview` anchors at the scope start, not the failure row; "NOT inside  <label>" has a double space; the FOUND OUTSIDE SCOPE tail ("widen the scope hint or change inFunction/betweenHint") does not fit an `endContent` that sits before `startContent`; and with `regex:true` the similarity score compares the pattern as literal text. **The stats counters are about 2x inflated.** In `session/session-stats.json` each real event counted twice in both controlled measurements, and 136 of the 137 non-zero counters were even. It pre-dates S4 and its cause was not investigated, so absolute counts (including the lifetime figures quoted in `match-engine.js`'s header comment and any quoted in earlier entries) are roughly doubled; ratios are unaffected.

## Engine safety (S3): identifier-boundary check for fragment matches, built and unit-tested but NOT wired into any tool (2026-09-20)

**Problem.** `str_replace`'s single-line branch matches with `String#includes`, so `old_str:'count'` matches inside `discount`. The B38 ambiguity guard only refuses when there is more than one hit; a single hit inside the wrong token commits silently.

**What exists.** `lib/identifier-boundary.js` (pure: no Pulsar, no buffer, no I/O) exports `isIdentChar()`, `checkIdentifierBoundary(line, index, len)` returning `{inside, left, right, leftChar, rightChar}`, and `scanLineForFragments(line, needle)`. A boundary only matters on an edge where the fragment itself ends in an identifier character, so `(count` and `count;` carry their own edge. Identifier characters are `[A-Za-z0-9_$]` only, on purpose: `-` and `.` are separators, and non-ASCII letters are not handled (revisit if a real case appears). Bad input never throws.

**Scope decision (PJ, 2026-09-20).** Safety guard only. The module says whether a hit sits inside a longer identifier. It does not implement part-line search/replace, and whether to refuse or warn is left to the caller.

**Verified / not done.** `test/s3_identifier_boundary_test.js` passes 23/23 with a negative control. Nothing requires the module (a grep of `lib/` finds only the file itself), so no live behaviour changed. The module's header said S4 would import it into the engine's substring matcher; S4 as built did not, so wiring it in is still to do.

## Engine occurrence safety (S2b/S2c/S2d): `delete` Mode 4 (`startContent`/`endContent`) honours `occurrence` and takes the nearest closer (2026-09-20, live-verified in a fresh MCP session)

**S2b: `occurrence` now reaches the `startContent` match.** `lib/mcp-registration.js` (~L2087): `matchContentEngine(startContent, ..., _startScope, { occurrence, autoRescue: false })`. `resolveScope()`'s `afterRow` branch returns without reading `occurrence` and without setting `occurrenceConsumed`, so before this a duplicate `startContent` could not be disambiguated even though the ambiguity message advised `occurrence:N`. Same shape as the Mode 5 bug fixed in S2; `read`'s pair mode already forwarded it.

**S2c: the `endContent` match is now the NEAREST hit at or after `startRow+1` (`{ occurrence: 1 }`, ~L2124).** S2b alone was only half a fix. The end match searched to EOF with no `occurrence`, and the engine refuses when more than one hit is found, so every `BEGIN`/`END` pair except the last one in the file failed with "endContent is ambiguous". `occurrence:2` on the probe file worked only because a single `END` follows the last `BEGIN`. The caller's `occurrence` is deliberately still NOT forwarded to the end match: forwarding the same N there would skip closers.

**Behaviour change, supersedes an earlier entry.** The `delete_block` port entry further down (the one that says the engine's ambiguity guard "now covers both anchors") describes a duplicate `endContent` no longer silently taking the first match. For Mode 4 that is reversed on purpose: an `endContent` that occurs more than once after the start now resolves to the nearest one, which is the ordinary meaning of a start/end pair. An `endContent` that must match a later closer needs a more specific string. The "endContent is ambiguous" refusal branch (~L2132-2138) is now unreachable for Mode 4, because `occurrence:1` means the engine never returns `ambiguous` for that call. The branch was left in place; removing it is a separate cleanup.

**S2d: `delete` Mode 4's start-failure block now handles an out-of-range `occurrence` (`mcp-registration.js` ~L2092-2102).** Forwarding `occurrence` (S2b) introduced a failure reason, `occurrenceOutOfRange`, that this hand-written block never handled, so `occurrence:5` against two matches was reported as `startContent not found (occurrence 5)`. Safe, since nothing was written, but the wrong reason. That one reason now goes through the shared `buildFailResponse()` (the same wording Mode 5 uses) and is counted under `fails.anchorAmbiguous`; the block's own ambiguous and not-found messages are unchanged. Found on the first fresh-session run: S2b alone was predicted to give the shared wording, and it did not.

**Verified.** Static: `node --check lib/mcp-registration.js` exit 0; `node test/s1_occurrence_test.js` 23/23 (run after S2c and again after S2d). Engine level, on `test/delete_mode4_occurrence_probe.txt` (`BEGIN` at L2 and L6, `END` at L4 and L8): for `occurrence:1` the old end match was AMBIGUOUS [4, 8] and the new one is L4 (the before/after contrast). **Live, in a fresh MCP session after a restart** (all dry runs, nothing written), `delete {startContent:'BEGIN', endContent:'END', dryRun:true}`: `occurrence:1` previews L2-4 (was refused with "endContent is ambiguous ... lines 4, 8"); `occurrence:2` previews L6-8 (unchanged); `occurrence:5` refuses with "occurrence:5 requested but only 2 matches found (L2, L6). Nothing was changed" (was "startContent not found"); `occurrence` omitted refuses with "startContent is ambiguous ... (lines 2, 6)", naming `startContent`. **Not done:** a negative control against the previous `lib/` for the live tool (the engine-level contrast above stands in for it).

**Finding, not a bug: `inFunction` does not consume `occurrence` in Mode 4.** On `test/delete_infunction_dup_test.js` (`dupfn` at L1-3 and L8-10), `delete {inFunction:'dupfn', startContent:'  return "second";', endContent:'}', occurrence:2}` refuses with "occurrence:2 requested but only 1 match found (L9)", while the same call without `occurrence` previews L9-10. Mode 4's `searchFrom` comes from `findFunctionInBuffer()`, a hand-rolled lookup, not from `resolveScope()`, so `occurrence` is not spent on choosing the function; it counts `startContent` hits from the function's first row to end of file. There is no double application, but `inFunction:'dupfn'` + `occurrence:2` does not mean "the second `dupfn`" here the way it does in Mode 5. The refusal is safe. Whether that meaning is intended is undecided, and it is the `searchFrom` that the planned `str_replace`/hand-rolled-tool port (B32 S4) would move onto `resolveScope()`.

## Engine occurrence safety (S2): `delete` Mode 5 (`matchString`) now honours `occurrence` (2026-09-20, live-verified in a fresh MCP session)

**The change.** One line in `lib/mcp-registration.js` (~L2205): the opts object `delete` passes to `matchContentEngine` is now `{ fuzzyWhitespace, fuzzyContent, regex, occurrence, autoRescue: false }`. Before this, `delete {matchString, occurrence:N}` could not select the Nth duplicate because `occurrence` never reached the content matcher, so the ambiguity message's advice to use `occurrence:N` was wrong for it. This closes S1 known limit (1) for Mode 5 and S1 found-item (6).

**Why it is safe.** `contentOccurrence(scope, occurrence)` in `match-engine.js` returns `undefined` when `scope.occurrenceConsumed` is set, so `occurrence` is not spent twice when `resolveScope()` already used it on an anchor hint (`inFunction`, `afterFunction`, `afterString`, ...). This is the S1 "occurrence is spent once" rule, applied to `delete`.

**Hot-reload gotcha, corrected.** `mcp-registration.js` is only re-read when a *new* MCP session is created (`loadMcpModules()` busts the require cache, called from the session-creation handler in `pulsar-edit-mcp-server.js`). The session that made the edit kept the old module and still returned AMBIGUOUS, which is expected, not a failure. `match-engine.js`, `buffer-helpers.js` and `tool-hints.js` still need a full Pulsar restart.

**Verified.** Static: `node --check lib/mcp-registration.js` exit 0; `test/s1_occurrence_test.js` 23/23 under plain Node. Engine level, `matchContent` on `test/engine_safety_probe.txt` with `autoRescue:false`: `occurrence` omitted is ambiguous (L2, L4), 1 picks L2, 2 picks L4, 3 and 5 are `occurrenceOutOfRange` (total 2). **Live, in a fresh MCP session** (all dry runs, nothing written): on `test/engine_safety_probe.txt`, `delete {matchString:'dup target', occurrence:2}` previews deleting L4 only, `occurrence:1` previews L2 only, `occurrence:5` refuses with "occurrence:5 requested but only 2 matches found (L2, L4). Nothing was changed", and omitting `occurrence` is still AMBIGUOUS with a message advising `occurrence:N` that is now true. On `test/delete_infunction_dup_test.js` (`dupfn` at L1-3 and L8-10), `delete {matchString:'  return "second";', inFunction:'dupfn', occurrence:2}` previews L9 only: the scope consumes `occurrence` once and it is not re-applied to the match. **Not done:** a negative control against the previous `lib/`.

**Test-design note.** A first attempt with `matchString:'return'` + `inFunction:'dupfn'` failed with "content not found" for both `occurrence:1` and `occurrence:2`. That was the test, not the fix: each `dupfn` body contains exactly one `return`, and the failure hint listed the other `return` lines as outside the active scope. A matchString spanning the whole body line resolves correctly. Worth remembering when writing further probes.

**Still open under S2.** `delete` Mode 4 (`startContent`/`endContent`) ignored `occurrence` when this entry was written, because `resolveScope()`'s `afterRow` path returns before reading it. That is now fixed (S2b/S2c/S2d, see the entry above) and live-verified. S1 found-items (4) stats bucketing and (5) misleading `inFunction` wording are still untouched.

## Engine occurrence safety (S1): an out-of-range `occurrence` is refused, never clamped; `delete` no longer auto-rescues (2026-09-20, live-verified after full restart; two stats-bucketing defects and one misleading message found, see Known limits)

**Context — the direction this belongs to.** The decided plan is one universal match engine that every edit tool shares (`resolveScope()` → `matchContent()`/`matchBlock()`/`matchFunction()` → `applyEdit()` → `buildFailResponse()`), with `str_replace`, `sed`, `replace-all` and `replace-document` ported onto it and the hand-rolled matching code deleted. That **supersedes B32's "do not port `str_replace`" ruling** (`mcp-server-refactor-plan.md` still carries the old wording and has not been updated). A read-only audit first established that `resolveScope()` already handles *every* scope hint including `sectionHint`/`preprocBlock`/`preprocSide`, so the scope-level hint gap is closed; what blocked the `str_replace` port was engine *safety*. `str_replace` refuses an out-of-range `occurrence`, but the engine clamped it, so a straight port would have made the highest-traffic tool less safe.

**The bug: six clamp sites, not the two first suspected.** The pattern `hits[Math.min(occurrence ?? 1, n) - 1]` silently turned `occurrence:5` with two matches into match 2. It appeared in `matchContent()` (regex and exact paths), `matchBlock()`, `matchFunction()`, and — missed by the first version of the audit — in `resolveStructuralAnchor()` for both `sectionHint` and `preprocBlock`. That audit had claimed anchor hints already refused safely; that was true for symbol/string anchors (they fall through to an ambiguity error) but **wrong for structural anchors**. Live proof on the old code: `delete {sectionHint:'INIT', occurrence:5, dryRun:true}` on a file with only two `INIT` banners previewed deleting banner 2. Not every site was reachable: `delete`'s `matchString` branch never forwarded `occurrence` to the content matcher, so the `matchContent` clamp could not fire through it. The live exposures were `replace-block` (`matchBlock`), `replace-function-body` (`matchFunction`) and `delete` Modes 1–2 (structural anchors).

**Fix.** Every clamp is replaced by a refusal. Content matchers return `{matched:false, reason:'occurrenceOutOfRange', occurrence, totalMatches, matchLines}`; the structural resolver returns `{ambiguous:true, outOfRange:true, ...}` and `resolveScope()` words it as "has only N matches … occurrence:K was requested. Nothing was changed". `occurrence` must be an integer from 1 to the number of matches — `0`, negatives and non-integers are refused too. `buildFailResponse()` has a dedicated branch so the failure reads as what it is, not as "content not found".

**Design rule: `occurrence` is spent once.** `replace-block`, `replace-function-body` and `insert` forward the *same* `occurrence` to both the scope anchor and the content match, and the old clamp made that double use quietly "work" whenever the scoped window held fewer hits than N. A blanket "out of range is an error" would have broken those calls. So `resolveScope()` now sets `occurrenceConsumed` when an ambiguous anchor was resolved *by* `occurrence`, and the content matchers then ignore it: exactly one hit in the window proceeds, more than one is refused as ambiguous. **One behaviour change to be aware of:** an anchor-consumed `occurrence` with more than one content hit used to pick hit N silently and now refuses.

**Callers updated so the new failure is described truthfully.** `replace-function-body` got its own branch ahead of its "did you mean…" name-suggestion path, which would otherwise have misdescribed it; `replace-block` and `insert` already route content failures through `buildFailResponse()`; `read`'s anchor-pair mode no longer falls back to substring matching on this failure and reports it directly.

**`delete` hardening.** Modes 4 (`startContent`/`endContent`) and 5 (`matchString`) now pass `autoRescue:false`. The engine's automatic rescue can fall through to a full-buffer partial match that lands *outside* the resolved scope (`outsideScope:true`, which `delete`'s `matchString` branch never checked), so a destructive call could be redirected to text the caller never scoped. Explicit `fuzzyWhitespace`/`fuzzyContent`/`regex` still work. Also fixed the first defect from the bump-to-missing-key sweep flagged under "Stats: three silent counting defects" below: `delete`'s `matchString` failure bumped `fails.matchStringNotFound`, a key `edit-stats.js` never had, so every `matchString` failure went uncounted. It now bumps the existing `fails.anchorAmbiguous` (ambiguous / out-of-range occurrence) or `fails.anchorNotFound`; the stats schema was left alone to avoid any lifetime-merge risk.

**Verified.** Static: `node --check` passes on `match-engine.js`, `buffer-helpers.js` and `mcp-registration.js`; `get-diagnostics` 0/0/0 on `match-engine.js`; `test/s1_occurrence_test.js` passes 23/23 under plain Node (pure functions — no Pulsar, no files touched) covering all four content matchers, both structural anchors, the consumed-occurrence rule (including the `replace-block` double-use case) and the failure wording; re-run after the restart, still 23/23. **Live, after a full Pulsar restart** (all dry runs — nothing was written): a probe run *before* the restart still previewed deleting lines 6–8 for `occurrence:5`, confirming the old code was resident until the restart; after it, `delete {sectionHint:'INIT', occurrence:5, dryRun:true}` on `test/s1_structural_probe.c` refuses with "has only 2 matches (lines 1, 6) but occurrence:5 was requested. Nothing was changed"; `occurrence:2` still previews lines 6–8; omitting `occurrence` still reports ambiguity. On `test/delete_infunction_dup_test.js` (`dupfn` at L1–3 and L8–10), `replace-block` and `replace-function-body` with `occurrence:5` both refuse with the new "only 2 matches found (L1, L8)" message and `occurrence:2` previews L8–10 correctly. On `test/engine_safety_probe.txt` (`dup target` at L2 and L4), `insert {afterContent, occurrence:5}` refuses and `occurrence:2` inserts after L4. `get-edit-stats` confirms `delete` `matchString` ambiguity now counts under `fails.anchorAmbiguous` (previously uncounted). **Not done:** a negative control (the same test against the last committed `lib/`) — the run-command guard blocked the extraction as destructive and it was not overridden; the range cases cannot pass on old code since the reason string did not exist. The stats reading was a single post-hoc snapshot, not a controlled before/after delta.

**Known limits, deliberately not addressed here.** (1) `delete`'s `matchString` still cannot select the Nth duplicate because `occurrence` is not forwarded to the content matcher, and the shared ambiguity message advises `occurrence:N`, which does not work for it — forwarding it is the next stage. (Mode 5 was fixed in S2; `delete` Mode 4 had the same gap because `resolveScope()`'s `afterRow` path returns before reading `occurrence`, now fixed by S2b/S2c/S2d and live-verified.) (2) With an explicit `occurrence` and *zero* exact hits, the automatic rescue can still guess a fuzzy match for non-`delete` callers such as `insert`. (3) The substring fallback in `read`'s pair mode was not audited for the same clamp.

**Found during live verification, not fixed.** (4) **Out-of-range refusals are miscounted in stats.** An `insert {afterContent, occurrence:5}` refusal was counted under `insert` `fails.anchorNotFound` (`fails.outOfRange` stayed 0), and a `replace-block {occurrence:5}` refusal was likewise counted under `fails.anchorNotFound`. The user-facing message is correct in both; only the counter bucket is wrong or imprecise. `replace-function-body` bucketed its refusals under `ambiguous`/`ambiguousHint`. Not yet traced to the responsible `bump()` calls, and it is not established whether `outOfRange` is the right target bucket for each tool. (5) **Misleading wording on a duplicated `inFunction` name.** `replace-function-body {inFunction:'dupfn', occurrence:5}` refuses with `inFunction "dupfn" is ambiguous — 2 functions with that name … Use occurrence:N to disambiguate`, although `occurrence` *was* passed. Safe (nothing was written) but the advice is wrong for that call; whether `inFunction` is read there as a scope hint or as the function name has not been checked in the source. (6) **The `delete` `matchString` ambiguity message still advises `occurrence:N`**, which delete Mode 5 cannot honour until `occurrence` is forwarded to the content matcher — this is limit (1) confirmed live, and it is fixed by the next stage rather than by wording.

## Hint-gap closure: `delete` and `read` now resolve every structural hint through the shared `resolveScope()` engine (2026-09-19 to 2026-09-20, live-verified)

**Context:** `insert` had already been ported so that `sectionHint`/`preprocBlock` went through `resolveScope()`, but `delete` still resolved them with its own hand-rolled `resolveStructuralAnchor()` call, and `read` did not accept them at all. That meant a structural anchor failed differently depending on which tool you used, and the shared failure response (ambiguity candidates, retry guidance) never reached them. This was a prerequisite for Stage 1 of the retryable-failures plan, which needs one failure shape across tools.

**`delete` Modes 1-3 ported.** `sectionHint`/`preprocBlock` (Modes 1/2) and `inFunction` alone (Mode 3) now call `resolveScope()` and delete `anchorRow..endAnchorRow` / `searchStart..searchEnd`. Failures go through `buildFailResponse()`. Verified against 10 dry-run baselines recorded *before* the port: the 8 structural cases and the `zeta` `inFunction` case were byte-identical or differed only in wording (`sectionHint "X"` became `sectionHint: "X"`), with identical candidate line lists. One consequence worth recording: a **duplicated function name is now refused with both candidates listed** where the old `findFunctionInBuffer()` path silently took the first — a real silent-wrong-delete hazard, now closed. `occurrence:1` and `occurrence:2` resolve correctly, and a real commit of `occurrence:2` deleted exactly the second definition. One trap in the port: for `inFunction`, `anchorRow` is only the function's *start* row; the full body range is `searchStart..searchEnd`. Using `anchorRow` would have deleted one line.

**`delete` Mode 6: `betweenHint` and `afterString` removed (design decision).** `afterString` pinned only `startLine` while `endLine` still came from the raw parameter — half content-anchored, half positional — and `betweenHint`'s start-to-end range is exactly what `startContent`+`endContent` already does with engine-backed ambiguity handling. Lifetime stats showed **zero** uses of either on `delete`. The rule is now: hints *scope* a content match (`matchString`); only `startLine`/`endLine` are positional, and they are labelled legacy.

**Dead-code bug found and fixed: `tool-hints.js` was keyed on retired tool names.** After `delete-line-range`/`delete-block` were consolidated into `delete` (2026-09-17), `smartSuggestion()` and `ambiguityCheck()` still keyed on the old names, so for `delete` the "NO HINTS USED" nudge never fired, the consecutive-failure escalation fell through to the useless generic "a different editing tool", and the ambiguity suggestion never fired. Nothing errored — it was silently dead behaviour for days. Fixed, and live-verified: after two failures the banner now names `matchString`/`startContent+endContent`/`sectionHint`. `tool-catalogue.js` also still carried the three retired entries and was collapsed to one accurate `delete` entry. **Process note:** after any tool rename or consolidation, grep `lib/*.js` for the OLD name as a string literal *and* as a table key.

**Retry nudge misfired on hint failures (fixed).** `buildFailResponse()` appended `retryNudge()` to *hint* failures too (bad or ambiguous `inFunction`/`afterFunction`/etc.), advertising the tool's *content* field (`matchString`/`old_str`/`anchor`) — which cannot fix a bad hint. Removed from the hint-failure branch; kept on the content-not-found branch. This also stops the same wrong advice on `insert`/`replace-block`/`replace-function-body`. Separately, the ambiguous-function message told callers who had already passed `inFunction` to "use inFunction instead"; that sentence is now suppressed for `inFunction` and kept for `afterFunction`/`afterSymbol`, where it is useful.

**`read` now accepts `sectionHint`/`preprocBlock`/`preprocSide`.** The engine's structural branch already handled a null buffer, so no engine change was needed. Three places had to agree or the feature would half-work silently: the Zod schema, the handler's destructure-and-forward, and the `READ_HINT_KEYS` table in `edit-stats.js` (without which the stat counters have no key to land on). The schema params were added explicitly rather than by spreading `STRUCTURAL_ANCHOR_SCHEMA`, because that bundle also carries `afterContent`/`beforeContent`, which position new text and are meaningless on a read tool. Structural failures now surface `anchorError` messages verbatim, so an ambiguous anchor keeps its candidate line list. Verified live: `sectionHint` unique/ambiguous/`occurrence`, `preprocBlock` with `preprocSide` `open`/`close` returning exactly one line each, not-found, and a regression pass over every pre-existing `read` mode.

**Docblocks rebuilt from the code.** The `resolveScope()` docblock now documents the real priority ladder, `sectionHint`/`preprocBlock`/`preprocSide`/`afterRow`, `endAnchorRow` (set only for range hints), and the `anchorError` contract. The `matchContent()` docblock had been **corrupted by an old botched edit** (a truncated sentence, a stray `* *`, and a duplicated garbled tail) and was rewritten from the actual 5-stage waterfall; `isCodeFile` was dropped from its documented options because the code never reads it.

**Still hand-rolled, deliberately deferred:** `delete` Mode 4's `inFunction`-scoped `searchFrom` (uses `findFunctionInBuffer()`), and `str_replace`'s own resolver block.

## Stats: three silent counting defects fixed (2026-09-20, live-verified)

**Root cause of all three: `bump()` silently no-ops on a key that is not in the tool's schema.** A counter can be wired into a handler and never move, and the aggregate then *looks healthy*.

1. **`read`'s pair-anchor failures were never counted.** The handler bumped `fails.startNotFound`/`fails.endNotFound`, but `read`'s stats schema only had `outOfRange`/`anchorNotFound`/`ambiguous`. Every pair-anchor failure was invisible, which is how `read` showed a perfect 100% (1906 hits / 8 fails). Keys added; lifetime `read` moved to an honest 99%.
2. **`startContent`/`endContent` counters could never move** because the pair mode always bumped `betweenHint`. Now counted under the spelling the caller used. Historical `betweenHint` counts are inflated by this and cannot be split retroactively.
3. **Ambiguous hints were counted as not-found** on `read` and on three `delete` sites, while `delete`'s existing `anchorAmbiguous` key was never bumped. Split correctly, using `e.kind === 'ambiguous' || e.ambiguous === true` (verified against `match-engine.js`: only the *ambiguous* structural `anchorError` sets the flag; the not-found one deliberately does not).

Verified live: `read` session counters moved as `anchorNotFound:1, ambiguous:1, startNotFound:1, endNotFound:1`, and `delete` as `anchorAmbiguous:2, anchorNotFound:1`. Dry-run failures *do* count toward these stats, so deliberate baseline probes drag pass rates down — that is not a regression. **Caution:** several lifetime counters moved by twice the session delta (and every lifetime counter seen is even), which points to lifetime deltas being applied twice. Pre-existing and not investigated; treat lifetime *absolutes* as roughly doubled and trust only ratios. **Not yet audited:** `insert`, `replace-block`, `replace-function-body`, `str_replace`, `sed`, `apply-patch` and the search tools for the same bump-to-missing-key defect (first hit already found: `delete`'s `matchString` failure bumps `fails.matchStringNotFound`, which is not in its schema).

## `features.retryOnFail`: bare-retry after a failed edit, live-verified on `str_replace`; path-comparison bug fixed (2026-09-18 to 2026-09-19)

**Context:** after `resendlessCommit` shipped, `get-edit-stats` showed dry-run previews get almost no real use (0 dry runs across `str_replace`/`insert`/`delete`/`replace-block` despite 1200+/58/10/10 real hits), while *failures* are heavy and real (`str_replace`: 282 lifetime fails — 62 `noMatch`, 78 `ambiguous`, 34 `outOfScope`, 34 `afterNotFound`). Preview-then-commit and failure-then-correct-one-field are different problems, and the second is where the real usage is.

**Design:** fires only on a genuine failure (`matched:false`), never wraps success. Only the last failed call per tool is stashed (`retryPending`, same lifecycle as `pendingCommits` but kept separate, since a tool can have both a pending successful preview and a pending failed attempt). The retry shape is implicit: the caller sends only `{filePath, <field>}` where `<field>` is what `features.retryOnFail.field` names (`old_str` for `str_replace`), and the framework merges that one field into the stashed args and re-runs the full call. The failure text must actively advertise this ("read/grep-file first, then retry with just `{filePath, field}`") — the main lesson from `resendlessCommit`'s near-zero real usage is that an unadvertised capability does not get used. The staleness sweep was extended, not duplicated: any real commit to a file clears both stores for that file. A retry that matches nothing pending falls through to a normal call rather than a custom error, because `{filePath, old_str}` is genuinely ambiguous with "a real but incomplete call". The nudge was deliberately **not** added to the *ambiguous* failure case, whose fix is `occurrence:N` or a scope hint, not a corrected `old_str`.

**Bug found and fixed — relative paths never matched the stash.** `tool-framework.js` compared the caller's path to the stash with bare `path.resolve()`. The stash holds the resolved *absolute* path; a project-*relative* path resolves against `process.cwd()`, not the project root, so the same file compared unequal. Isolated with a controlled test (relative failed twice; absolute, same sequence, merged and committed — path form was the only variable, ruling out stale reload code, a merge-logic bug, and schema-default keys). It hit both the `resendlessCommit` splice and the `retryOnFail` merge; earlier `resendlessCommit` tests happened to use a shape that resolved, so it never surfaced. Fixed with a `_samePath()` helper that runs both sides through `resolveProjectPath()` then `path.resolve()` and returns `true`/`false`/`null`; only a *proven* match counts as the same file (`false` and `null` both refuse — never splice into a possibly-wrong file). **A self-inflicted bug worth recording:** the first insert anchored on a JSDoc opener and landed the helper *inside* the factory's comment block, so it was never defined and every bare-retry call threw `_samePath is not defined`. Repaired and read back. Lesson: read the surrounding lines before inserting next to a comment block; an `afterString` anchor on a comment line is dangerous.

**Verified live after restart:** relative-path bare retry merges and commits; relative-path `insert` + `commitLastPreview:true` commits (previously "Pending preview ... was for a different file" naming the *same* file); absolute-path retry still works; the staleness sweep holds (`str_replace` fails, `insert` commits to the same file, bare retry then falls through to the `new_str is required` guard and writes nothing); `str_replace`'s own hand-rolled not-found return now carries the nudge; the ambiguous failure correctly shows none.

**Scope, honestly stated:** only `str_replace` has `features.retryOnFail` wired. `delete`, `replace-block` and `replace-function-body` are in `RETRY_FIELD_BY_TOOL` (the nudge side is ready) but have no `retryOnFail` registration, so **`delete`'s content-not-found message advertises a bare retry the framework does not support** — it works only by accident, as a fresh complete call with no stashed-hint reuse. Failure flags are also inconsistent across tools (`matched:false`, `inserted:false`, `deleted:false`, `found:false`, `applied:false`), which is why the plan introduces one explicit `retryable:{kind,...}` contract in Stage 1. `insert` returns a misleading `afterString "X" not found` for a *nonexistent file* (blames the anchor for a missing path); small, to be fixed first after Stage 0. Full plan (Stages 0-4, decisions A/B/C) is in `mcp-server-refactor-plan.md`.

## `apply-patch` now previews by default — SHIPPED BUT NOT LIVE-VERIFIED (2026-09-18)

`dryRun` now defaults to `true`, matching the pattern every other dry-run tool uses (preview, then resend with `dryRun:false` to commit). Exactly one line of real behaviour changed; the existing fuzzy-rescue `confirm:true` + `patchRescueStore` path is untouched. An earlier version of this work invented a bespoke `confirm`/`patchCommitStore` commit mechanism and was **reverted** once it was pointed out that a unified, per-tool-capability edit backend is the decided architecture — an ad-hoc mechanism for one tool would have gone against it. Description text updated in both `mcp-registration.js` and `tool-catalogue.js`. `get-diagnostics` clean. **The four planned live checks (no-`dryRun` previews only; `dryRun:false` applies; `dryRun:false` on a never-previewed patch applies directly; the rescue path is unaffected) were never recorded as run.** Also open: `apply-patch` has a **0% lifetime pass rate** (44 fails, split evenly between `contextMismatch` and `exception`), which is worth investigating separately.

## `B84` lint race: two confirmed `linter-bundle` limitations, upstream issues filed, local fallbacks not implemented (2026-09-16 to 2026-09-17, BLOCKED)

**Scope of impact:** this affects **every** `features.lint` tool (`str_replace`, `insert`, `create-file`, and the rest), not just `create-file`/B84 specifically. B84's own `countOnly` mode is not at fault; it inherited a pre-existing race that was already present, and presumably already silently under-reporting, in every lint-enabled tool.

**1. Tab-open case — one edit behind.** Our `lint-helpers.js` force-triggers `editorLinter.lint(false)` and waits for `onDidUpdateMessages` once. `linter-bundle`'s `lint()` gives each request a number used by an internal staleness guard, but the emitted `did-update-messages` event does not carry it, so we cannot tell whether an event is the completion of the pass we just triggered or the tail of a still-running previous one. Reproduced live: each edit reports the *previous* edit's lint state (a separate `get-diagnostics` call was fresh, so the bug is specific to our force-trigger-and-wait path). A deterministic fix needs upstream to add the number to the event.

**2. No-tab case — a hard architectural wall, not a bug.** A file edited via `bufferForPath` with no open tab gets *zero* lint feedback (not stale — empty), confirmed live and by reading source at two independent layers: `EditorLinter`'s constructor throws unless given a real `TextEditor`, and `linter-eslint`'s own `provideLinter().lint()` independently returns null for anything but a `TextEditor`. This is the documented provider contract, so it very likely holds for every `linter`-service provider. `linter-bundle`'s `linter-indie` service is tab-free but accepts only already-computed diagnostics — no `lintText(path, source)` entry point — so using it would mean running ESLint ourselves.

**Status:** two upstream issues filed against `asiloisad/pulsar-linter-bundle`, both awaiting a maintainer response. Local fallbacks ready but *not implemented*: a trailing-quiet-window debounce filtered on `event.buffer === editor.getBuffer()` (heuristic, not deterministic) for #1, and opening the file in a background tab via `atom.workspace.open(path, {activateItem:false})` for #2. The earlier "no lint output" symptom was initially suspected to be a provider or activation problem; it was not. `linter-eslint` was active and correct throughout, and the cause was the debounce race described above.

## New framework capability: `features.resendlessCommit` — commit a dry-run preview without resending arguments (prototyped on `insert`, 2026-09-18)

**Context:** every dryRun-capable tool already follows the same convention — preview, then the caller resends the *entire* call again with `dryRun:false` to commit. For a large `new_str` or a complex set of anchors, that's real duplicated effort on every single edit. `apply-patch` had grown its own bespoke answer to this for one narrow case (`confirm:true` + `patchRescueStore`, fuzzy-rescue only) — but that's exactly the kind of one-off, tool-specific mechanism `mcp-server-refactor-plan.md`'s "unified edit backend with per-tool capability tickboxes" direction exists to replace. An early attempt this session to solve the same problem by hand-rolling a second bespoke mechanism directly on `apply-patch` was deliberately reverted mid-session once that direction was pointed out — the general capability belongs in `tool-framework.js`, gated by a `features` tickbox like `dryRun`/`lint`/`successNudge` already are, not reinvented per tool. `insert` was chosen as the prototype vehicle per the plan's own insert-first sequencing.

**Mechanism (tool-framework.js, entirely generic — no handler code changes required beyond opting in):**
- A tool declares `features: { resendlessCommit: true }`.
- A new module-level store, `pendingCommits` (keyed by tool name), holds `{ args, filePath, timestamp }`.
- Right after `await handler(args, ctx)` returns, if the tool has the feature and the result carries `dryRun: true` (the existing convention every dryRun tool already returns — no new signal needed), the framework stashes that call's full `args`.
- If the *next* call to that same tool is exactly `{ filePath, commitLastPreview: true }` (no other meaningful args), the framework splices the stashed args back in, forces `dryRun: false`, and re-invokes the handler — which never has to know a splice happened.
- One-shot: cleared on a successful splice-commit, on the handler itself returning `dryRun: false` directly (covers a normal `dryRun:false` call too), and — the staleness guard — cleared for *any* tool's pending entry on a file whenever *any* tool commits to that file, since the previewed state may no longer be accurate.
- Two friendly, specific error paths instead of a crash or silent no-op: `commitLastPreview:true` with nothing pending, and `commitLastPreview:true` naming a different `filePath` than what's actually pending.

**Why `commitLastPreview` and not `commit`:** the first draft used `commit:true`, matching `apply-patch`'s existing shorter precedent. Renamed on request to be self-documenting — the param name itself now states the contract (there must have been a prior preview) rather than relying on the error message to explain it.

**Bugs found and fixed during live verification (four real, distinct issues, not variations of one root cause):**
1. **Required-field coercion.** `insert`'s `new_str` is normally required; a bare `{filePath, commitLastPreview:true}` call arrives with `new_str` coerced to `""` rather than truly absent, which the original splice-detection logic counted as "a real extra argument" and refused to splice. Fixed generically — `undefined`/`null`/`""`/`false` are all treated as "not really provided" for this check, not hardcoded to `new_str` by name, so any future `resendlessCommit` tool with a similarly-optional-but-coercible field works the same way automatically.
2. **Missing nudge in the most common path.** `insert`'s three *inline* dryRun response blocks got the `commitLastPreview` nudge added to their text, but its most-used path — anchor-based inserts (`afterFunction`/`afterSymbol`/`afterString`/`betweenHint`) — goes through a shared helper, `buildInsertPreview()` in `match-engine.js`, added in an earlier refactor and missed entirely by a literal-text grep sweep since its response text is generated once, centrally, not duplicated per call site. Fixed once, at the shared helper, covering all three of its callers.
3. **Zod transport-layer rejection.** The `""`-coercion fix above only helps once a call actually reaches the handler — but MCP's own schema validation runs *before* that, and `new_str: z.string()` (required) rejected a bare `commitLastPreview:true` call outright with a raw "new_str: Required" error, never even reaching `tool-framework.js`'s splice logic. Fixed by making `new_str` `.optional()` at the schema level, with an explicit, equivalent guard added inside the handler body (`if (new_str === undefined) return an explicit error`) so a genuine call that omits `new_str` without `commitLastPreview` still gets the same rejection it always did — the schema relaxation only exists to let the narrow splice-eligible shape through.
4. **Staleness guard didn't cover most tools.** The first version of the cross-tool invalidation sweep lived inside `ctx.commit()` — but `str_replace`, the highest-traffic edit tool in the codebase, doesn't call `ctx.commit()` at all; it builds its return value directly via `buildEditResponse()` and returns it from the handler. A sweep placed only inside `ctx.commit()` therefore missed the majority of the codebase's edit tools' commits, `str_replace` foremost among them — live-tested and confirmed: a pending `insert` preview survived a `str_replace` commit to the same file and (by luck of where the stale anchor happened to still resolve) applied anyway on a later `commitLastPreview:true`. Fixed by moving the sweep to the one place that genuinely sees every tool's result regardless of internal path — the same post-handler block where stash-detection already lives — triggered by any `dryRun: false` result with a resolved `filePath`, deliberately *not* gated on the committing tool's own `resendlessCommit` support (a plain `str_replace` must still be able to invalidate `insert`'s pending preview). The original `ctx.commit()`-based sweep, now fully redundant, was removed rather than left as a second parallel mechanism. A scoping bug in the first draft of this fix (`path.resolve` called where `path` wasn't in scope, which would have thrown on every single commit in the codebase) was caught and fixed before it ever reached a restart.

**Verified live, all cases, post-restart, in order:** (a) a plain call with no `dryRun`/`commitLastPreview` — unaffected, writes immediately, confirming the feature is a true no-op unless a real preview occurred. (b) `dryRun:true` — previews correctly, response text includes the `commitLastPreview:true` shortcut (confirmed on the `buildInsertPreview`-backed anchor path specifically, the one that was missing it). (c) `insert({filePath, commitLastPreview:true})` immediately after — spliced the stashed args and applied the exact previewed insert, confirmed via `read-file` showing the exact previewed content at the exact previewed location, with zero `new_str` resent in that final call. Regression-checked a second time after the staleness-guard fix, still passes. (d) `commitLastPreview:true` with nothing pending — friendly, specific error, no crash. (e) `commitLastPreview:true` naming a different file than what's pending — friendly error correctly naming the actual pending file, refuses rather than misapplying. (f) staleness guard — `insert` dryRun-previewed on a file, `str_replace` committed to that same file, then `commitLastPreview:true` correctly refused with "no pending preview" rather than applying a stale insert; confirmed via `read-file` that no stale content landed.

**Status:** closed and live-verified for `insert`. `get-diagnostics` clean throughout on every touched file (`tool-framework.js`, `mcp-registration.js`, `match-engine.js`, `tool-catalogue.js`) after all fix rounds. This went through an unusually high number of restart-and-retest cycles — every one of them surfaced a genuinely new, real bug rather than a false alarm, which is worth keeping in mind as a caution for the next tool that adopts this feature: budget for iteration, not a single clean pass. `apply-patch`'s own bespoke `confirm`/`patchRescueStore` mechanism (fuzzy-rescue only) is a reasonable candidate to migrate onto this now-proven general mechanism instead, unifying the two — not started, suggested as a follow-up rather than pursued unprompted.

## `get-region` and `read-lines` merged into `read` and retired (2026-09-18)

**Context:** `get-region` and `read-lines` were two separate, largely overlapping read-side tools — `get-region` did content-anchor-pair reads (`startContent`/`endContent`, substring match), `read-lines` did line-number and symbol-based reads (`inFunction`, `nearLine`+`radius`, `startLine`+`endLine`, `afterString`, `betweenHint`) via its own hand-rolled matching rather than the shared `resolveScope()`/`matchContentEngine()` engine. Both had real but incomplete hint coverage — `read-lines` had no `afterFunction`/`beforeFunction`/`afterSymbol`/`beforeSymbol`/`occurrence`/`fuzzyWhitespace`/`fuzzyContent`/`regex`, and `get-region`'s own `occurrence` param existed in schema but had zero real-world uses and no real ambiguity guard behind it.

**Change:** built `read` as a single generic anchor-based read tool on `resolveScope()`/`matchContentEngine()`, covering every mode either source tool had plus the previously-missing hints, and added a local substring-fallback (`findSubstringInLines()`) so `get-region`'s original "unique text at or near the start/end" partial-line matching semantics were preserved rather than silently tightened to exact full-line matches. After `read` was built additively and live-verified against its own fixture, `get-region` and `read-lines` were removed outright rather than kept as alias wrappers — `read` is now the only entry point for anchor/line-based reads (`read-file` remains separate — whole-file/paginated dump, a different tool class, untouched by this merge).

**Bug caught and fixed before the tools were retired:** the pair-anchor mode's first draft called `matchContentEngine` directly for `startContent`/`endContent`, which does full-line exact-equality matching — a real, silent regression from `get-region`'s original substring behavior (a caller passing partial-line text, which `get-region`'s own description explicitly invited, would have gotten a false "not found"). Caught via live testing before the aliasing/retirement step, so no existing caller was ever exposed to it. Fixed with a substring-per-line fallback that only engages when `matchContentEngine`'s own attempts find nothing — a genuine ambiguity result from the engine is trusted as-is and never masked by a lucky substring hit landing on just one line elsewhere.

**Housekeeping — stale description text fixed across the file:** every other tool's description or inline error/nudge message that referenced `read-lines` by name (`str_replace`'s failure diagnostics, `insert`'s positional-hint warnings, `read-file`'s truncation note, `grep-file`/`grep-project`/`file-line-count`'s "follow up with" pointers) was updated to point at `read` instead, so no user-facing text references a tool name that no longer exists. `read`'s own header comment, which still described itself as an "additive first step" pending a future alias conversion, was updated to reflect that the merge is now complete rather than in progress. Comments explaining *why* the substring fallback exists (referencing `get-region`'s original behavior as historical context, not as a live tool) were left as-is — they remain accurate.

**Not yet done at write time, now resolved:** this edit touched `mcp-registration.js` and needed a restart before it could be confirmed live. After restart, `list-tools` initially still showed `get-region`/`read-lines` as present and `read` as absent — this turned out **not** to be the ordinary stale-process hazard (a genuine restart had already happened), but a second, independent bug: `tool-catalogue.js` is a separate file that `list-tools` reads its metadata from, and it still had the old entries because the original merge work only touched `mcp-registration.js`. Fixed by updating `tool-catalogue.js` directly — removed the `get-region`/`read-lines` entries, added `read` (group `edit`, matching its actual handler registration), and fixed two more stale `read-lines` mentions in that file's own `list-project-functions`/`file-line-count` descriptions. Confirmed via `list-tools` post-fix: `read` present with correct description and group, `get-region`/`read-lines` fully absent, all cross-references correct.

**Process note for future tool renames/retirements:** `mcp-registration.js` holds the real implementation and its own inline description, but `tool-catalogue.js` is an independent second source of truth that `list-tools` specifically depends on — a rename, port, or retirement needs both files updated, and a project-wide grep for the old tool name (not just a check of `mcp-registration.js`) should be a standard last step before considering any such change closed.

**Status:** closed, source-confirmed and live-confirmed post-restart. Universal hint-naming cleanup (`old_str`/`afterContent`+`beforeContent`/`expectedContent`/`startContent`+`endContent`/`matchString` → one canonical name) remains separate, later, not started.

## `replace-function-body` ported onto the shared match-engine.js, live-verified (2026-09-18 code and verification)

**Context:** `replace-function-body` was fully hand-rolled — its own inline `afterString`/`betweenHint` resolution, its own `occurrence <= 1` ambiguity gate (the same pre-fix convention the `occurrence:1` off-by-one entry below closed everywhere else), and its own call into `findFunction()` (`tree-sitter-symbols.js`) for the actual function lookup. It also carried an `inFunction` parameter with a different, narrower meaning than every other tool on the shared engine: rather than "the function itself" (as `inFunction` means for `str_replace`/`insert`/`delete`/`replace-block`), it meant "restrict the target-name lookup to *inside* this other named function" — an outer-container filter, not an anchor. `afterFunction`/`beforeFunction`/`afterSymbol`/`beforeSymbol` were present in the schema and destructured in the handler but never actually consulted — dead schema, the same class of gap `replace-block`'s port (see entry below) found and fixed for its own hints.

**Decision — drop `inFunction`'s outer-container meaning rather than reconcile or rename it:** confirmed via `get-edit-stats` that `inFunction` had 0 real lifetime uses on this tool across 1486 lifetime edit ops project-wide, and 0 of `replace-function-body`'s 8 lifetime fails were `ambiguous` (all `notFound`) — no evidence any real duplicate-name case ever needed it. Structurally, it was never a real anchor to begin with: every other hint in the schema can resolve a position on its own, but this one only ever modified an already-otherwise-supplied target name (parsed from `newBody`'s own signature line) — a filter, not an anchor. Further, C doesn't allow two function *definitions* sharing a name in one translation unit, so the scenario this hint existed to disambiguate — two real nested bodies with the same name — is close to impossible in valid C, not just empirically rare here. Removed outright, not renamed/preserved. Post-port, `inFunction` on this tool now means what it means everywhere else on the shared engine — "the function itself" — consistent with `str_replace`/`insert`/`delete`/`replace-block`.

**Change:** added `matchFunction(fnName, {allLines}, scope, opts)` to `match-engine.js`, after `matchBlock` and before `applyEdit()` (STEP 4), exported alongside it. Given a `scope` from `resolveScope()` and a `symbols` array passed in via `opts` (reuses the handler's own `getSymbols()` call rather than re-parsing), filters symbols by `name === fnName` and `startRow` within the resolved scope, sorts by `startRow`, then applies the same `occurrence == null`-ambiguous / `occurrence:N`-explicit convention as `matchContent`/`matchBlock` — refusing with the candidate lines rather than ever silently picking one. No anchor-scan or brace-count needed at all (unlike `matchBlock`), since tree-sitter symbol boundaries (`sym.startRow`/`endRow`) are already exact. Returns the same shape `matchBlock` does (`matchLine`/`matchEndLine`/`actualLines`/`sig`/`kind`), so `applyEdit()`/`buildFailResponse()` work on it unmodified. `replace-function-body`'s handler in `mcp-registration.js` was rewritten to: `resolveScope()` → `matchFunction()` → dryRun preview → `applyEdit()` → `buildEditResponse()`, dropping the old inline `afterString`/`betweenHint` resolution, the old outer-container `inFunction` block, and the old `occurrence <= 1` gate entirely. This also gives the tool the B39/B40 drift/verify safety net on its commit path for free, same as `replace-block`'s port.

**Behavior change, not just a refactor:** `afterFunction`/`beforeFunction`/`afterSymbol`/`beforeSymbol` were already present in the schema and destructured in the old handler, but never actually consulted for disambiguation — dead parameters. Post-port, all of them are live and genuinely scope the search, same finding as `replace-block`'s port below.

**Bug caught and fixed before ship (source-inspection, not a live failure):** the first draft of `matchFunction` read `scope.symbols`, assuming `resolveScope()`'s success return carried the full symbols array. Grepped every `symbols` reference in `match-engine.js` before committing and confirmed `symbols` only ever appears inside `scope.ERROR` objects (diagnostic use), never in a success return. Fixed by passing `symbols` in via `opts` instead, computed once by the handler.
